# Decision record

The long record of the decisions of the development, in the order they were taken (from 2026-10-07), with the options,
the measurements and the reasons. The one-line summary of each is the {doc}`decision log <../decisions>`. The record
keeps the internal labels of the time ({doc}`../glossary`); where a later section supersedes an earlier one, the later
one says so, and the code and the user pages are current. Early sections list options with the recommended one first;
"default" means the repository already behaved that way. "Phase 1" and "Phase 2" in the early sections are the two
phases of the port plan (`docs/dev/archive/PLAN.md`): the work on the cluster in the first week, and the analysis
and estimator work that runs anywhere.

## Science choices that fix the V4 campaign (defaults already implemented)

> Superseded in part (state of 2026-11-26). Matching mass: the literature (1−b) mass since 2026-10-09 ("Calibration
> subset and matched draw on the literature (1−b) masses"; `donors.matching_mass = 'literature_one_minus_b'`), and
> for the production pairing the fitted hydrostatic mass on both sides ("Design note: matching on the hydrostatic
> fit mass, in two passes", 2026-10-27). K: 5, with 2,840 views ("Full snapshots only; mini-snapshot fallback;
> composition-aware truth", 2026-10-12). Gas selection: all particles in a sphere for the truth ("Spherical
> hydrostatic truth", 2026-10-09) and the 4 R500c cylinder for the mock gas ("Geometry of the mock gas", release
> 1.1). Metallicity: free, with a flat prior over 0-2 (`massfit.metallicity_mode = 'free'`; "No tuned factors: the
> last replacements", 2026-11-03). The mean molecular weight is that of full ionisation
> (`massfit.mu_convention = 'full_ionisation'`). The closure grid was run several times ("Closure grid: factorial
> data modes and SAS-masked fit arms", "Fitter freeze, proposed for sign-off", "The model averaged over the pixel")
> and since 2026-11-11 does not gate the freeze (PREREGISTRATION section 1).

- **Reference sample.** Default: the 638 bank observations with subset flags;
  the SZ-defined set (607) is a filter. Effect: 24 observations stay
  unmatched by mass (mostly ACT at z > 1.15); the ACT-only redshift
  distribution is marginal (D = 0.120 vs 0.113). Alternative: pass
  `--include-hiz-snaps` to draw from snapshots 45/40, which lowers the
  unmatched count to about 4 at the cost of a handful of poorly resolved
  high-z halos.
- **Matching mass.** Default: catalogue M500 with the (1−b)-corrected value
  stored as a column. Alternative: match on (1−b)-corrected mass. Effect:
  the corrected choice shifts the mock mass distribution up by 0.03 dex
  (Planck) to 0.11 dex (ACT) and removes most of the high-mass gap; the
  catalogue choice keeps the mocks comparable with the G3 real-side numbers.
- **K halos per observation.** Default K = 3 (1,839 views, ~9 h of quick
  fits). K = 5 gives 3,040 views (~16 h) and is the most before the mass
  match degrades. Effect on ML: training set 1.3 k vs 2.2 k views.
- **Gas selection.** Default `fof` (TNG300/100/50 portability). The audit
  showed FoF-only pushes mock masses UP by omitting cool infall gas
  (projected T 1–11 % hotter at R500). Alternative `zoom` for TNG-Cluster:
  more realistic outskirts, breaks cross-simulation comparability. Effect on
  the bias calibration: a few per cent at R500 in the opposite direction
  from the 0.65.
- **Metallicity in the fit.** Default: fixed 0.3 (Lodders), as in G3.
  Alternatives: fixed 0.23 (TNG mean) or free. Effect: fixed 0.3 reads mock
  temperatures 2–5 % low and real ones 5–10 % high (does not cancel); free
  adds a parameter per fit and ~10 % runtime.
- **Mean molecular weight.** Recommendation: switch to the fully-ionised
  convention (0.592 for TNG abundances) for V4 and report upstream.
  Effect: +0.05 dex on every mass, mock and real (0.65 → 0.73 on the G3 mocks).
- **Intrinsic hydrostatic mass definition** stored per halo. Default:
  thermal-pressure only, hot gas above 3×10⁵ K as in the mocks, plus a
  thermal+kinetic variant and a spectroscopic-like variant. Effect: the paper
  can split physics from method with no extra runs.
- **Fit budget.** Default: quick mode (450 steps) for every mock view, long
  chains (2,000 steps) for every real cluster and the showcase mocks. Gate Q
  showed quick and long agree to 0.002 dex in the median with 0.013 dex
  scatter. Effect: ~11 h instead of ~28 h on one node.
- **Closure grid before the freeze.** Recommendation: run it (one node,
  ~2 h) and gate the freeze on 1.00 ± 0.03. Effect: without it the paper
  cannot claim the chain is neutral; the audit's single ideal cluster came
  back 1.2–1.36 × truth.
- **Per-view temet-exact SB maps (`sim.tngref_map`).** Default in v4: on, 2000²,
  bands 0.5–5.0 and 0.5–2.0 keV (`tngref_sb_map.fits`, equal to the official
  TNG-Cluster maps: L(<R500c) to 1e-5, pixel p99 4e-6 dex on 6130348 @ 99 and
  2194356 @ 50). Cost per view: 60–120 s on one core (13–20 M cells), about
  17 MB on disk (53 GB for 3,119 views); per halo +10–20 s of read and no
  change of the peak memory. Options: (a) keep; (b) `tngref_map_npix = 500`
  (~10 s and ~1 MB per view, not pixel-comparable with the official maps);
  (c) off. Effect: (a) roughly doubles the ~70 s/view generation wall.

## Instrument choices (from the W2 inventory and the audit)

- **Camera offsets vs mask frame.** The hypothesis that the masks already
  carry the camera layout rests on ONE observation; the verification on
  ≥ 12 observations was not run. Options: (a) keep the V3 offsets for V4 and
  record the question; (b) run the verification first (login node, ~1 h of
  work). Effect: if (b) confirms it, V4 chip gaps move by up to 1–3′ relative
  to V3 — a visible change in every mock image.
- **Donor depth.** Options: (a) PPS EXPOSURE as today; (b) the exposure-map
  peak (live time × out-of-time factor), which is what the real PN cameras
  deliver. Effect: (a) mocks keep 12–16 % more PN counts than their donors;
  (b) is the like-for-like choice.
- **Window-mode donors (~10).** Options: keep (default) or exclude. Effect:
  negligible on statistics; excluding removes a documented mismatch.
  Decided 2026-10-08: keep them and image each camera through the donor's
  readout window (section "Readout windows" below).

## Hosting and publication

- **Tier B products** (per-camera images of every view, chains, coverage
  maps; ≤ 50 GB trimmed). Options: (a) a restricted Zenodo record uploaded
  during Phase 1; (b) your own storage; (c) drop them (re-fitting then needs
  the cluster again). Effect: (a) gives a DOI for the paper and survives
  your departure.
- **TNG cutout fixtures in the public release.** Options: (a) ask the TNG
  team for permission; (b) replace by an API-fetch script with the user's
  key. Effect: (b) keeps the repository self-contained only for people with
  TNG access.
- **DINOv3 embeddings.** Options: (a) ship with a licence copy and the
  acknowledgement; (b) ship only the recipe. Effect: (b) makes Phase 2
  impossible on a laptop (no re-extraction without a GPU and the gated model).
- **Estimator family for the ML route.** cINN and XGBoost + MDN both sit
  behind one interface; the comparison is a Phase-2 branch question. Nothing
  to decide now except that Phase 1 freezes the three embedding kinds and
  the donor folds (done in the plan).

## Already decided this week (for the record)

> Superseded 2026-10-09 ("Calibration subset and matched draw on the literature (1−b) masses"): the Planck (1−b)
> listed as open below is 0.78. Since 2026-10-27 the catalogue masses and the assumed bias serve as cross-checks
> only ("Design note: matching on the hydrostatic fit mass, in two passes").

Forks for upstream fixes (private until licensed); no contact with the
simulator authors before a draft; fixtures cut from two small halos; bank
format (uint16 mosaic canonical, render derived); ID scheme; three
environments; Kedro off the critical path; quick-mode fits for the mock
campaign; the exposure-map rotation fix and the corrected trees as the
baseline; gate C/Q frozen numbers.

2026-10-08 (matching masses):

- **SZ masses in the pipeline cosmology.** PSZ2 MSZ and ACT DR6 M500c (both
  published in flat H0 = 70, Om = 0.3) are re-solved in Planck15 from their own
  scaling relation for the same observed signal
  (`obs/mass_conversion.py::sz_m500_to_cosmology`; derivation in the module
  docstring and REFERENCE_LEDGER row 46). Effect: PSZ2 ×1.024–1.033, ACT
  ×1.011–1.032; every matching mass is now Planck15. For PSZ2 the main-text
  relation (Planck 2013 XX) is used; the appendix's Arnaud+10 gives the same
  conversion to < 0.1 %.
- **CCCP source: Hoekstra+15, not Herbonnet+20.** The CCCP matching mass stays
  the Hoekstra+15 M500^NFW (Dutton & Macciò c(M)); Herbonnet+20 masses are
  reference rows only (`obs/catalogue/README.md`).
- **Replica (1−b) re-derived** with the G3 computation on the corrected masses
  (`massfit/one_minus_b.py`, REFERENCE_LEDGER row 47): Planck 1.07 → 1.11,
  ACT DR6 1.29 → 1.32, WL 0.93 → 0.89. The WL value had been calibrated on WtG
  M(<1.5 Mpc) read as M500; the SZ values move with the Planck15 conversion.
  Only the stored (1−b)-corrected target (`log_m500c_target_b`) changes; the
  default catalogue matching is unaffected.

2026-10-09 (literature hydrostatic bias of the matching masses):

- **Decided (author): match on the literature (1−b)-corrected mass.** The V4
  samplers (K = 5 replica, maximal-list donors, post-hoc matched draw) match the
  mock TRUE M500c to M_true = M_match/(1−b), with (1−b) from the paper the mass
  comes from; lensing masses are not corrected; nothing is corrected twice
  (`obs/hydrostatic_bias.py`, `ReplicaSpec.matching_mass = literature_one_minus_b`
  in the v4 presets; v32 unchanged; REFERENCE_LEDGER row 50). What each class
  already contains, and what it gets:
  PSZ2 MSZ is hydrostatic (Planck 2013 XX Y–M, b = 0): **0.78**, the Planck 2015
  XXIV baseline (CCCP prior 0.780 ± 0.092, Table 2, §4.3, p. 7). ACT DR6 `M500c`
  (the column we read) is already weak-lensing-calibrated (10^A0 = 3.0e-5,
  M^A10/M^WL = 0.65; DR6 §2.5, p. 7): **1**. CCCP and WtG are lensing masses:
  **1** (the WtG M(<1.5 Mpc) → M500c conversion stays). The Planck15 conversion is
  a z-only factor and (1−b) multiplies M in both Y500 and θ500, so the order of
  the two corrections does not matter. The empirical G3 values (1.11/1.32/0.89)
  are kept as the diagnostic `one_minus_b_empirical_g3`. Gate P compares in true
  mass (reference M_cat/(1−b), θ500 × (1−b)^(−1/3)); legacy populations keep the
  catalogue convention.
- **Open: which Planck (1−b).** Default 0.78 (the PSZ2 companion paper's own
  baseline). Alternatives (all Planck 2015 XXIV Table 2 / §7.1): WtG 0.688 ± 0.072
  (+0.162 dex instead of +0.108), CMB lensing 1/(1−b) = 0.99 ± 0.19 (≈ no
  correction), the 2013 reference 0.8 (+0.097 dex), and the 0.58 ± 0.04 the
  primary CMB requires (+0.237 dex; would leave TNG with almost no halos for the
  massive Planck clusters). A lower (1−b) moves the Planck targets further into
  TNG's scarce massive end.
- **Effect on the target lists** (rebuilt with the same inputs, seeds, K, caps and
  epoch rules; the generated view SET is unchanged: same view ids, halos, axes,
  seeds, splits, mini-snapshot and held-out flags; donors and z_obs change):
  K = 5 replica 3,112 → 3,092 views, coverage 99.1 → 98.4 % of the 635 used,
  **gate P now FAILS in matching mass** (D 0.142 vs 0.10; Planck subset 0.180):
  with ≤ 3 views per halo-snapshot from the nearest snapshot TNG-Cluster cannot
  supply 5 halos per massive Planck cluster (K = 3 passes only nominally, D 0.087).
  v4_max: 18,549 views unchanged, 95 % of the donors and 94 % of the z_obs change;
  v4_box: 297 views unchanged, 89 % of the donors change. Post-hoc sampler on the
  maximal list: the former sweet spot K' = 15, R = 9, dz 0.25 now FAILS (mass D
  0.102, z D 0.077 > critical 0.060); K' = 10, R = 12, dz 0.25 passes strictly
  (mass D 0.041, z D 0.034; 6,289 draws on 4,077 views; > 1e15: 19 draws per
  object, max 90). Details: `campaigns/v4_max/README.md` ("Literature (1−b)").
  (Superseded 2026-10-12: on the full-snapshot lists K' = 10 passes only through p_neff; the
  recommended draws are K' = 5, R = 12 (main) and R = 9 (calibration); view counts 9,615 / 159 /
  2,840. See "Full snapshots only" below.)

## Consolidation after the generation campaign (behaviour changes, 2026-10-08)

Changes from the Kedro/tidiness review (`REVIEW_KEDRO/REVIEW.md`) that change
what the code produces. They do not touch the generation path (views, products,
photometry); each has a test. Merge after the production campaign tag.

- **B5: one donor-fold algorithm and seed.** Before: the view tables
  (`targets_v4.csv`, `donor_folds.csv`) used the donors-stage assignment
  (groups = `cluster_id`, z-quantile blocks x log mass, seed 20261007) and the
  bank manifest used a second algorithm (union-find groups, 2 x 2 strata,
  round-robin, seed 7777), so one donor could sit in fold f in the view table
  and in fold g in the ML manifest. Decided: the documented one (W8 design §2g
  and §5: "5 folds stratified by z and reference mass", the one the committed
  V4 replica carries) is THE algorithm, `donors.assign_donor_folds` with
  `DONOR_FOLD_SEED = 20261007`. It now also accepts the bank's `links` (the
  conditions and redshift donors of one mock join one group; a V4 replica has
  no such link, so its folds are unchanged: `campaigns/v4/donor_folds.csv` is
  reproduced byte for byte from `observations.csv@7c07eea`).
  `bank.assign_donor_folds` is a schema adapter over it (`donor_group` = the
  smallest obsid of the group, `stratum` = `zbin<k>`); the bank CLI default
  seed and `bank.folds.seed` in `conf/{base,fixtures}/parameters/pipeline.yml`
  are 20261007. Effect: bank folds built before this change (seed 7777) are
  superseded; the V4 view-table folds stand. Tests:
  `tests/unit/test_donor_folds_one_algorithm.py` (seed everywhere, the two
  tables agree per donor, no donor or cluster in two folds, the leak check is
  clean, the committed V4 replica's `donor_fold` equals the bank's fold for
  every view).

- **B8: hashed spec fields that no code read.** 71 of the 177 hashed
  `CampaignSpec` fields were read nowhere outside `config.py` (68 by name; three
  more, `instrument.exposure_s`, `instrument.pn_oot_frac` and
  `massfit.nh_fallback_1e22cm2`, only shared a name with something else). They
  documented constants that live in the stage modules, so the spec hash did not
  guarantee the behaviour, and two disagreed with what runs. Decided per field:
  - **wired (1):** `massfit.target_pixsize_arcsec` 10 -> **8** arcsec (every
    G3/V3.2 fit job and the V4 runs pass `--pixsize 8`; 10 was the legacy CLI
    default) and read by the closure-grid fit arms instead of their own literal
    8.0. The fitter CLI default (`fit_hydrostatic.py --pixsize 10`) is the
    fitter branch's to take from the preset (B2).
  - **removed, physical or catalogue constants (5):** `constants.z_sun`,
    `kpc_to_cm`, `kev_to_erg`, `z_solar_ag89`, `x_h_primordial` (the module
    constants of `core.units`, `sim.load_halo_gas`, `sim.compute_apec_spectra`
    are the definitions; they are not campaign choices).
  - **removed, generator internals fixed by other fields (7):**
    `sim.sim_configs` (legacy registry; `sim_name`/`base_path` are read),
    `grid_n` (legacy yt grid; the per-particle sampler has no grid),
    `apec_n_bins`, `apec_kt_min_kev`, `apec_kt_max_kev`, `apec_table` (properties
    of the APEC table file, selected by `apec_version`),
    `intrinsic_lx_apertures_kpc` (the `lx_intr_*` columns are fixed in
    `sim.generate_view`).
  - **removed, V3 legacy donor/redshift draw not ported (7):**
    `donors.log_mass_bw_dex`, `min_neighbours` (20; the port `draw_donor` uses
    10 and no preset path calls it), `z_obs_min`, `m500_frac`, `z_jitter_sigma`,
    `epoch_halfwidth`, `mass_tol_ladder_dex` (the V4 replica uses its own
    `ReplicaSpec` ladder 0.10/0.15/0.20).
  - **removed, tables owned by the modules that use them (2 + 6):**
    `donors.pn_oot_by_mode`, `pn_oot_default` (`draw_donor` and
    `build_replica_targets` hold the table); `instrument.exposure_s` (fallback
    of the legacy CLI only), `pn_oot_frac` (the per-view value comes from the
    donor package), `bands_out`, `cam_filecode` (`instrument` module
    constants), `legacy_instrument` (the full xmm_simulator path is not a preset
    option), `expmap_fix` (the header tag of the corrected V3 trees).
  - **removed, photometry (3):** `photometry.measured_apertures_kpc`,
    `fit_seed_apertures_kpc` (the apertures are fixed in `measure_lx` and
    `build_fit_targets`) and **`low_z_safe_aperture`**: v4 set it `True` but
    nothing read it; the low-z-safe apertures are always part of the image
    photometry, whatever the preset (`measure_fixed_apertures.low_z_safe_apertures`,
    z_min 0.04, clearance 1.5 from `photometry._settings`), so the flag misled a
    reader of `v4.yml`.
  - **removed, fitter internals (40):** `massfit.bands_ev`, `instr_map`,
    `filter_key`, `bg_prior_dex`, `ne_mode`, `ne_beta_prior`, `ne_rc_prior_kpc`,
    `ne_alpha_prior`, `ne_epsilon_prior`, `ne_r_s_prior_kpc`, `ne_rc_init_kpc`,
    `mass_model`, `overdensity`, `c200_prior`, `c200_init_clip`,
    `m500_guess_default_1e14msun`, `m500_guess_floor_1e14msun`,
    `centre_bound_arcsec`, `maxradius_kpc_default`, `rmax_model_factor`,
    `rmax_model_clip_kpc`, `phys_rmax_kpc`, `optimiser_maxloops`,
    `mcmc_steps_long`, `mcmc_steps_quick`, `nwalkers_rule`, `randsamples_long`,
    `randsamples_quick`, `unbracketed_ok_threshold`, `nh_fallback_1e22cm2`,
    `min_halfbox_px`, `mask_rad_arcsec`, `protect_core_arcsec`, `vig_clip_min`,
    `bg_annulus_r500`, `bg_fallback_rmax_frac`, `bg_min_pix_annulus`,
    `bg_min_pix`, `bg_init_default`, `sb_floor`. They are constants of
    `fit_hydrostatic.py`/`prepare_images.py` (another branch owns those files);
    the fit records its own settings in `result.json`/`provenance.json`, and the
    switches the fit takes from the preset (`fit_switches_from_spec`) are all
    still fields. A field the fitter branch starts to read is added back with
    its reader.

  Effect: every preset hash changes (old -> new, 16 hex digits): v32
  `e70ffb9f2d47554f` -> `cec29f72406b547f`, v32_lowz `e6d751b82a13f1b2` ->
  `908843128385ce9d`, v4 `75f0a5968c57f969` -> `0dfdae3bb441a660`, v4_tng300
  `fed18ce9626f49a7` -> `196ffaf44cdc096d` (full digests in INTERFACES §5).
  No generation, instrument or photometry code path changes; products made
  under the old hashes carry the old values (campaign check C16 compares a
  tree's rows with the hash of the preset it is run with, so check an old
  tree with the code it was made with). `conf/base/parameters/*.yml` are
  regenerated. Tests: `test_every_hashed_field_is_read_by_some_code` (a new
  descriptive field fails it), `test_closure_grid_fits_at_the_preset_pixel`.

## Fit fix and real-fit plan merged (2026-10-09)

- **The fit models the PSF and the pn out-of-time events (v4).** Four massfit
  fields, each read by `fit_hydrostatic.fit_switches_from_spec`: `psf`
  (v4 `king-onaxis`, `massfit.fit_psf`), `pn_oot` (v4 `data-template`,
  `massfit.pn_oot`), `pn_oot_cti` (v4 1.4e-3) and `fit_pixels_per_r500`
  (v4 40); v32 keeps None/None/0/None (the G3 fitter). Reason: the V4 closure
  grid's cool-core low bias (`docs/dev/reports/V4-CCBIAS.md`). None of the 40
  fitter constants removed by B8 is read by the merged fitter, so none came back.
- **One fit-pixel rule, from the preset.** Target pixel
  `massfit.target_pixsize_arcsec` (8" in every preset) coarsened to
  R500_guess / `fit_pixels_per_r500` where that is larger
  (`fit_hydrostatic.effective_pixsize`; v4: 8" down to z ~ 0.1). The target
  pixel is now one of the preset switches: `fit-hydrostatic --pixsize`,
  `run_fit(target_pixsize_as=...)` and the closure-grid arms default to it
  (an explicit other value is recorded as a `switch_overrides` entry);
  `slurm/fits.sh` passes `--pixsize 8` only to the frozen legacy fitter.
- **Every fit switch from the preset** in the CLI, `run_fit`, the Kedro
  massfit node and the real-fit plan (`build_realfit_targets` writes
  `--preset v4` and no switch, so the plan carries the PSF and the pn OoT
  template; the earlier plan's `--psf none` is gone).
- Spec hashes (old -> new, 16 hex digits): v32 `cec29f72406b547f` ->
  `dd0fa3c97c92dd26`, v32_lowz `908843128385ce9d` -> `1e809cabd9218418`,
  v4 `0dfdae3bb441a660` -> `29f4d50b825a3ed5`, v4_tng300 `196ffaf44cdc096d`
  -> `4ce20ff8b803077d` (110 hashed fields; full digests in INTERFACES §5).
  Only massfit fields change: no generation, instrument or photometry path.

## Barnes-faithful fitted HSE estimator (2026-10-09, independent review)

> Superseded 2026-10-12 ("Intrinsic hydrostatic truth: one methodological line"): the mu = 0.59
> column `barnes21_nosf_fit` described here is no longer emitted; the headline is
> `barnes21_nosf_fit_mu`.

- **New truth column `barnes21_nosf_fit`; the headline is unchanged
  (`barnes21_nosf`, finite differences).** Review of `compute_hse_mass.py` against Barnes et
  al. 2021 sec. 2.4: eq. 12, mu = 0.59, mass-weighted T and the 50 linear bins over 0-1.5 R500c
  match; the slopes do not. Barnes fits the 3D density (as n_e n_H, eq. 8, gamma = 3, xi < 5)
  and T (eq. 10) with the Vikhlinin (2006) forms and differentiates the fits analytically; we
  use `numpy.gradient`, which makes M_HSE(<r) noisy and non-monotonic. The new estimator takes
  the same 50-shell filling-density profiles (`_bin_profile`), fits rho^2 (slope of rho = half
  the fitted slope) and T_mw by `scipy.optimize.least_squares` in log space (soft-L1, bounds,
  4 starts each, best cost), and evaluates eq. 12 with the FITTED T(r) and analytic slopes at
  the shell centres where data exist; the M500 crossing is the unchanged `_summaries` rule.
- **Choices.** Density: ONE component (Barnes quotes one; a second would be degenerate on 50
  shells), parameters (ln A, r_c, alpha, beta, r_s, xi), alpha in [0, 4.5], beta in [0.2, 2],
  xi in [0, 4.99]. Temperature: T0, r_cool, a_cool, T_min/T0 (Barnes prints T_min T0, a typo),
  r_t, a, b, c; Barnes prints the first factor of t(r) as (r/r_t)^(+1), the a = -1 member of
  Vikhlinin's (r/r_t)^-a, so a is free in [-1, 1]. Fits run in units of R500c; at least 15
  finite shells are required.
- **Flags.** Summary keys `<key>_fit_{rho_ok,T_ok,ok,rho_rms,T_rms,n_shells}`; column
  `hse_fit_ok_<key>` (both fits converged). A failed fit gives NaN M(<r) (no M500).
- **Crossing rule and substructure (ledger row 6).** The innermost downward crossing beyond
  0.4 R500c is our choice (Barnes gives none); Barnes removes substructure only for the images.
- **Star-forming gas always excluded (author, 2026-10-12; our deviation from Barnes et al., who state no
  gas cut for the 3-D profiles).** No fitted variant includes the
  star-forming cells: in TNG their temperature is the effective equation-of-state value of the
  star-formation model, added after the fact, not a physical gas temperature. A no-cut fitted
  variant `barnes21_fit` was written and removed before merging.
- **No spec-hash change.** `TRUTH_COLUMNS` grows by 4 columns (m500, mhse, mu and the flag),
  so the results CSV of truth-column presets gains them (per-estimator columns are not in
  `schemas/tables.py`); the truth-profile files gain `native/barnes21_nosf_fit` (additive,
  format unchanged). `HSE_VARIANTS` has `("thermal-fit", "nonsf", linear:50:0:1.5, "mass")`; `hse_pressure` is a free string.
- **For the author.** Making the fitted variant the headline needs the preset value
  `hse_pressure = "thermal-fit"` (v4 and children), which changes their spec hashes. Not done.

## Generation decisions after the node pilot (2026-10-08)

The author's decisions after the review of node pilot 1 (job 1886473, 238 views,
`$TNGXMM_SITE_PROD/nodepilot_v4`, code c9a0b0c). Spec fields in
`config.py`; the legacy presets keep the legacy behaviour (v32 products byte-exact:
`test_sim_exact`, `test_instrument_exact`).

- **Periodic minimum image at load** (`sim.periodic_wrap`, v4 and the box presets
  True, legacy False). Every particle read for the mock, the truth and the
  `*_tngref` columns/maps goes through `sim.read_halo_source.CampaignHaloSource`,
  which moves `Coordinates` to the minimum image about the halo's `GroupPos`
  (`d -= BoxSize round(d / BoxSize)`, `BoxSize` from the snapshot header); a
  particle that needs no wrap keeps its bits, so no product of a halo away from
  the box faces changes. Evidence: pilot views s050 h10228605 v0 and s091
  h4215937 v1 differed from the official maps because the gas across a box face
  sat ~1 Gpc away (87 production halo-snapshots within 2 R200c of a face,
  `PILOTREVIEW/edge_dist.csv`; 39 within 1 R200c; TNG300: 37 of 1,428 within
  1 R200c). Measured on those two halos (`internal/map_residuals/fof_acceptance.py`,
  FoF map + map of the non-FoF zoom gas against the official map): without the
  wrap the per-pixel closure (FoF + non-FoF vs official) has a 99th percentile of
  |dlog| 3.16 and 1.95 dex (whole map L 0.974 and 0.966 of the official); with it
  the closure is exact (p99 < 1e-5 dex, L(< R500c) closure 1.0000), 151,192 and
  3,534,970 FoF gas cells were moved by the wrap. The mock clip sphere, the photon
  projection and the cylinders stay non-periodic arithmetic on wrapped
  coordinates. `obs_meta.json` records `periodic_wrap` and the number of wrapped
  particles per (type, selection); map header `PERIODIC`, product header
  `PERIODIC`.
- **FoF for everything, in every simulation.** The mock already used FoF; now the
  truth (`sim.truth_selection = 'fof'`: `compute_hse_truth(selection='fof')`, all
  particle types of the FoF group; the catalogue M500c stays the catalogue value)
  and the `*_tngref` columns and per-view temet-exact maps (`sim.lx_reference_gas
  = 'fof'`, map header `GASSEL = 'fof'`) read the halo's FoF members. The box runs
  (TNG300/100/50) read `loadHalo` directly from the snapshot; the sphere cutouts
  are off the production path (`tngxmm sim sphere-cutouts` stays a tool; the
  Kedro cutout node and `slurm/campaign_tng300.sh` are gone). Truth profiles beyond
  ~R200c hold FoF members only (`truth_profile.h5` attributes `particle_selection`,
  `truth_selection`); the particle M500c stays within a few per cent of the
  catalogue value (TNG300 halo 75: e2e passes the 5 % gate).
  **Acceptance against the official TNG-Cluster maps** (16 halos x 3 views at
  snapshots 99, 91, 84, 78, 72, 67, 50, 40, M500c 1.2e14-1.9e15, incl. the two
  edge halos; `GENFIX/fofacc/*.json`). FoF region = the pixels where the non-FoF
  zoom gas adds less than 1 % of the FoF surface brightness (the official map
  should be the FoF map there). Results: the closure FoF + non-FoF = official is
  exact in every pixel (p99 |dlog| < 1e-5; L(< R500c) closure 1.0000); inside the
  FoF region |dlog| p99 = 0.0041-0.0043 dex (the 1 % definition bound), 0 pixels
  with a finite/-inf mismatch; the FoF region covers 17-69 % (median 39 %) of the
  pixels inside R500c and 7-34 % (median 18 %) inside R200c (projected non-FoF
  gas at the 1 % level is common: other groups and the fuzz along +-R200c);
  L_FoF / L_official in R500c = 0.986-0.9996 (median 0.995), in R200c
  0.977-0.998 (median 0.992). Differences outside the FoF region are accepted.
  The e2e `tests/e2e/test_tngref_map_official.py` checks the FoF maps (L(< R500c)
  within 2 %, never brighter than the official map, p99 < 0.005 dex where the
  official map is FoF-dominated) and keeps the exact comparison for the zoom cell
  set (the projection itself).
- **Mini snapshots kept, flagged. REVERSED 2026-10-12: full snapshots only (section
  "Full snapshots only", end of this file).** 9 of the 17 v4 snapshots were mini (95, 87, 80,
  75, 70, 65, 60, 55, 45; `sim/snapshots.py`): no per-cell `GFM_Metals` and
  `GFM_CoolingRate` (17 PartType0 fields absent in all; list in the module and in
  `campaigns/v4_box/README.md`), so the gas loader uses X_H = 0.76 and solar
  ratios scaled by each cell's `GFM_Metallicity` (`Z_elements = GFM_Metallicity /
  Z_sun` of the abundance table for all 7 elements; until 2026-10-12 the code used 1 x solar
  for every cell, which contradicted this sentence) and keeps heated cells (no
  cooling-rate cut); the truth estimators do the same (X_H = 0.76, no heated-cell cut). `snap_is_mini` (and `snap_absent_fields`) is in the v4
  results rows, `obs_meta.json`, the product headers (`SNAPMINI`), the map headers
  and the `truth_profile.h5` attributes; the TNG100/TNG50 targets table carries it,
  the committed v4 / v4_max tables do not (the runner adds it to every row).
  Measured offset (pilot review): `lx_intr_r500` is +2.9 +- 0.4 % higher on mini
  than on full snapshots for the same halos (the per-cell X_H / metal / cooling-cut
  fallbacks); to be modelled or cut on `snap_is_mini` downstream.
- **Readout windows**: see the next section (`instrument.readout_windows`).
- **Truth outside the gas-load slot.** The slot of the campaign pool is held for
  each raw read only (mock gas, `*_tngref` cells, truth particles), through the
  campaign source; the HSE and profile computation run outside it (pilot:
  `runtime_hse_s` median 41 % of the halo time, the 5 slots were the bottleneck).
  The read buffers are released as before (the estimators delete each particle
  type after binning).
- **Box runs TNG100-1 and TNG50-1** (presets `v4_tng100`, `v4_tng50`, built like
  `v4_tng300` by `config._v4_box`; RNG scheme sha256-v1): every FoF central with
  M500c >= 1e14 Msun at the 17 v4 snapshots, 3 axes, donors by the `nearest_zm`
  rule with an explicit use cap of 3 (the cap-factor rule gives 1 here, median
  donor rank 62; cap 3 gives rank 14, as the TNG300 rows' 15), splits by z = 0
  object (object ids 2e6 + group, 3e6 + group), `held_out_resolution = True`
  (an extra test set whatever the split): `campaigns/v4_box/` (TNG100 276 views,
  92 halo-snapshots, 11 objects; TNG50 21 views, 7 halo-snapshots of one object).
  The single TNG50 object has 2.6e8 gas cells in its FoF group at z = 0: its mock
  gas load alone peaks at 122 GB (login-node measurement), so the job runs TNG50
  with one worker (`WORKERS_TNG50`).
- **Production job** `slurm/campaign_v4_all.sh` (one p.vera node): lane A
  TNG-Cluster (v4, 40 workers x 5 gas slots, low z first), lane B TNG300 then
  TNG100 (28 workers x 3 slots) then TNG50 (1 worker), then the TNG-Cluster
  snapshots from the high-z end; a (preset, snapshot) is claimed once per job
  (atomic mkdir); one resumable tree per preset under the out-root; campaign check
  per tree at the end. Split from pilot 1: 4.65 core-min per TNG-Cluster view with
  zoom reads, MaxRSS 89 GB at 40 workers x 5 zoom loads (the FoF reads are smaller).

Effect on the spec hashes (old -> new, 16 hex): v32 `cec29f72406b547f` ->
`61e8c6c3368c3f14`, v32_lowz `908843128385ce9d` -> `926eaa3d8a10531d` (new fields
only; legacy behaviour unchanged), v4 `0dfdae3bb441a660` -> `529c46c59f579e6d`,
v4_tng300 `196ffaf44cdc096d` -> `0acde92674431227`; new v4_tng100
`7a6dddbcc42e00c6`, v4_tng50 `bdb52312a967fde3`.

## Node memory budget (2026-10-09)

Node pilot 2 (job 1886564, code c725daa, two lanes of 40 + 28 workers) was
OOM-killed after 5 minutes at 252 GB (35 oom_kill events): with the truth
computation outside the gas-load slot and reading every particle type of the FoF
group, the slot bounded the concurrent raw READS but not the memory the tasks hold
after them. Scheduling change only (products unchanged):

- **One token pool per node** (`core/membudget.py`), shared by every worker of both
  lanes through a lock-protected JSON state file in the job's scratch directory
  (`TNGXMM_MEM_BUDGET_GB`, `TNGXMM_MEM_BUDGET_FILE`; unset = no budget, the fixture
  regressions run unbudgeted). A halo task takes its estimated peak before its
  first raw read (budget first, gas-load slot second), shrinks to its per-view
  level after the truth, releases at its end (also on exceptions; grants of dead
  processes are dropped by pid + start time). A task never waits while holding
  tokens (no hold-and-wait, so no deadlock); a request above the pool runs alone;
  after 300 s of waiting the oldest request is served first (no starvation). Each
  worker's private memory between tasks (instrument response caches, ~1-1.6 GB)
  is recorded as its resident and counts against the pool; the heap is trimmed
  (`malloc_trim`) between views and after each task in budgeted runs.
- **Estimate**: per step, level above the idle worker = c + b n0 (FoF gas cells,
  `GroupLenType`) [+ p P for the instrument stage, P = (M500c/1e14)^1.6 t_max /
  D_L(z_obs)^2 of the task's views], least-squares lines raised to cover every
  point, times 1.15. Calibrated on 8 halos (n0 2.8e6-5.2e7: TNG-Cluster snap 99
  halo 0, 75/1636750, 67/15192989 (median), 50/1693341, 45/283874; TNG300 99/0,
  91/84; TNG100 60/1; VmHWM per step, `MEMBUDGET/calib*`): gas 405 B/cell
  (lsq 307, R^2 0.99), tngref 303 (259), truth 428 (368), photons 395 (243),
  instrument 3.6 GB + 123 B/cell + 0.12 GB per P (R^2 0.98). The event lists of
  the brightest low-z views dominate (P up to 194 in the production lists: ~37 GB
  for TNG-Cluster 99/252455). TNG50 (2.6e8 cells, extrapolated): ~137 GB.
- **Job** `slurm/campaign_v4_all.sh`: pool = MEM_FRAC (0.80) x the job's cgroup
  limit - 4 GB (parents); `--set MEM_BUDGET_GB` overrides. Per-task report
  (peak RSS, estimate, grant, wait) in the lane logs (`  mem halo=` lines) and in
  `obs_meta.json` (`memory`, budgeted runs only); a node sampler writes free /
  cgroup / summed PSS / pool use every 30 s to `<out-root>/logs/mem_<job>.log`.
- Login-node validation (6 snap-50 TNG-Cluster halos, 18 views, 4 workers): with
  a 20 GB pool the process tree peaked at 13.4 GB PSS (pool use 19.1 GB, 3 tasks
  at once); with an 8 GB pool at 8.2 GB (one task at a time, the parent's 0.5 GB
  and the residents beyond the pool by the run-alone rule); without a pool
  20.7 GB. Measured task peaks were 0.67-0.80 of their estimates. Results CSV,
  every obs_meta (without `memory`), every product identical across the three
  runs (FITS data and keywords, HDF5 data; only creation times differ).

## Readout windows (decided 2026-10-08)

Spec field `instrument.readout_windows`: `'full-frame'` (v32, v32_lowz: every
camera imaged on its full-frame mask whatever the donor's mode; byte-exact
legacy) or `'donor'` (v4, v4_tng50/100/300). Under `'donor'` each camera
images only the silicon its donor mode reads out
(`src/tngxmm/instrument/readout_windows.py`): a 0/1 window on the 900² detector
mask grid (2.5″) is multiplied into `resp.mask`, the active pixels follow, and
the QPB spectrum is scaled by the read-out area (the legacy `area_mask`
normalisation). Every mask lookup (cluster, sky background, QPB, AGN, PN
out-of-time events, out-of-FOV rule) and both EXPMAP constructions
(`inverse-map`, `legacy-embed`) read that mask, so events and exposure maps
share one footprint. PN out-of-time events (full-column model) are assigned to
the rows read out (99 or 64 rows instead of the 200-row column). SUBMODE of
every camera is the donor's (INTERFACES §3); `RDWINDOW`, `WINAREA` headers.

Donor modes in the tables: v4 targets (3112 rows) PN LW 5, SW 15; MOS1 W2 10,
W3 5; MOS2 W2 5, W3 5, FastUncompressed 5. v4_max (18549 rows) PN LW 59, SW
123; MOS1 W2 64, W3 59; MOS2 W2 5, W3 59, FastUncompressed 59.

Geometry (frame of the packaged masks = sky frame of obsid 0827340901 at
PA_PNT 114.2°; CCD edges at 24.5° = `-pn_readout_tilt_deg`; line fits to the
PN row boundary and column gaps give 24.44 ± 0.02°):

| camera, submode | window | source |
|---|---|---|
| PN PrimeLargeWindow | 99 rows of all 12 CCDs, counted from the inner (row-boundary) silicon edge of each CCD row | Users Handbook (UHB) §3.3.2, Table 3 (198 × 384 px); CCF EPN_MODEPARAM_0003 mode 2 (RAWY 101–199); EPN_LINCOORD_0009 (RAWY grows away from the readout node) |
| PN PrimeSmallWindow | 64 rows × CCD 4 (64 columns), from the inner edge of the upper CCD row; CCD 4 = the column left of the central gap (q ∈ [−3.52′, +0.96′]) | UHB (CCD 4; table 63 × 64 px); EPN_MODEPARAM mode 3 (RAWY 136–199 = 64 rows); position and row count from the PPS maps (half-maximum edges 64.0 rows, 64.3 columns) |
| PN FastTiming, FastBurst | refused (no imaging) | UHB |
| MOS PrimePartialW2 | 100 × 100 px (1.1″) = 1.83′ square on the central CCD | UHB Table 3; EMOS1_MODEPARAM_0006 mode 2 lists 110 × 100 RAWX × RAWY, the PPS windows are square (42–45 mask px on both axes) |
| MOS PrimePartialW3 | 300 × 300 px = 5.5′ square on the central CCD | UHB Table 3; EMOS1_MODEPARAM mode 3 (310 × 300); PPS 126–133 mask px |
| MOS FastUncompressed / FastCompressed | central CCD (600 × 600 px, 11.0′) without image, outer CCDs normal | UHB §3.3.2; PPS EXPMAP of 0780291001 MOS2 |
| MOS PrimePartialRFS | full frame | EMOS1_MODEPARAM mode 1 (full central CCD) |
| MOS PrimePartialW1/W4/W5/W6 | refused (no donor; geometry not in the UHB) | — |

MOS window centre = central-CCD centre in the mask frame, MOS1 (−1.158′,
−1.188′), MOS2 (−0.975′, −1.308′) in (q, s): centroid of the PPS windows of
0506210101, 0780291001 (W2) and 0810231001 (W3), consistent with the
central-CCD edges of the mask. The CCF files EPN_MODEPARAM_0003 and
EPN_LINCOORD_0009 are not in the site CCF set; they were read from the public
valid-CCF area of the XMM-Newton SOC (sftp ftp-bdt.cosmos.esa.int).

Agreement with the real PPS EXPMAP2000 of the same obsids (mock: inverse-map
EXPMAP of the windowed response at the donor's roll PA_PNT − 114.2°, camera
offsets 0 as in v4; area of EXPMAP > 0 within 15′ of the pointing, arcmin²;
`zero15` = fraction of that disc with EXPMAP = 0; IoU of the two footprints
within 15′; "central CCD": EXPMAP > 0 area inside the central-CCD square,
i.e. the window itself):

| obsid | camera | submode | real | mock | ratio | zero15 real | zero15 mock | IoU | central CCD real / mock |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0810231001 | PN | PrimeLargeWindow | 350.6 | 342.6 | 0.977 | 0.504 | 0.516 | 0.962 | |
| 0810231001 | MOS1 | PrimePartialW3 | 326.7 | 316.0 | 0.967 | 0.538 | 0.553 | 0.964 | 31.26 / 30.26 |
| 0810231001 | MOS2 | PrimePartialW3 | 516.8 | 504.3 | 0.976 | 0.269 | 0.287 | 0.973 | 31.15 / 30.11 |
| 0502790101 | PN | PrimeSmallWindow | 20.43 | 19.49 | 0.954 | 0.971 | 0.972 | 0.952 | |
| 0506210101 | PN | PrimeSmallWindow | 20.24 | 19.45 | 0.961 | 0.971 | 0.973 | 0.952 | |
| 0506210101 | MOS1 | PrimePartialW2 | 399.1 | 289.4 | 0.725* | 0.436 | 0.591 | 0.724 | 3.88 / 3.49 |
| 0506210101 | MOS2 | PrimePartialW2 | 490.1 | 477.6 | 0.974 | 0.307 | 0.325 | 0.973 | 3.69 / 3.37 |
| 0780291001 | PN | PrimeSmallWindow | 20.28 | 19.38 | 0.955 | 0.971 | 0.973 | 0.945 | |
| 0780291001 | MOS1 | PrimePartialW2 | 298.6 | 288.5 | 0.966 | 0.578 | 0.592 | 0.955 | 3.65 / 3.49 |
| 0780291001 | MOS2 | FastUncompressed | 485.4 | 473.3 | 0.975 | 0.314 | 0.331 | 0.969 | 0.00 / 0.00 |
| 0502790101 (full-frame reference) | MOS2 | PrimeFullWindow | 607.3 | 591.0 | 0.973 | 0.141 | 0.164 | 0.969 | 115.2 / 112.8 |
| 0502790101 (full-frame reference) | MOS1 | PrimeFullWindow | 515.0 | 401.3 | 0.779* | 0.272 | 0.432 | 0.776 | 115.0 / 110.7 |

The mock areas are 2–5 % below the real ones in every mode, as in full frame
(MOS2 0.973): the PPS maps are smeared outward by the attitude (a few
arcsec along every edge); the window shapes and positions overlap with IoU
0.95–0.97. * MOS1 in 2007 (0502790101, 0506210101) still had CCD 3, lost in
2012 and absent from the packaged mask (from a 2018 observation): an epoch
difference of the mask, not of the window. Script:
`$TNGXMM_DEV/GENFIX/windows/scripts/validate.py`.

Open: PN out-of-time events from photons that land in the non-read rows
between a window and the readout node are not modelled (LW OoT fraction
0.16 %); the legacy-core OoT model streaks towards the full-frame node and
loses the part outside the window. MOS1 epochs before 2012 image one CCD
more than the mask. Products made before this change under v4 carry
`SUBMODE = PrimeFullWindow` on MOS and full-frame footprints for window
donors.

## Generation fixes and memory budget merged with the fit fix (2026-10-09)

The two sections above were made on 50df62a in parallel with the fit fix; the
merge keeps both: the generation, instrument and pool code is that of 2b8f1d3
(the node-pilot-2b code), the massfit fields and the fitter those of 701651b.
Spec hashes of the merged presets (113 hashed fields = 107 + the 3 massfit
fields + the 3 generation fields; full digests in INTERFACES §5): v32
`4bae859796e7d24f`, v32_lowz `4f007c1d5d79283e`, v4 `5e9a63a5fa50f632`,
v4_tng300 `f7fb5d4781c741d7`, v4_tng100 `3778137fc658f7cb`, v4_tng50
`fdc3b09620a3ffe6`. The hashes quoted in the two earlier sections are those of
each branch alone. Products of 2b8f1d3 and of the merge are identical for the
same views (the hash differs through the massfit fields only).

Closure grid after the merge: `validation.closure_grid` generates the ideal
clusters through the merged instrument stage (readout windows under v4; the
108 grid donors are all full-frame PN/MOS, so their products are unchanged
apart from the `RDWINDOW` / `WINAREA` keys; FoF and the periodic wrap act on
TNG particles only and do not enter). The placeholder snapshot 0 of an ideal
cluster no longer writes `SNAPMINI` / `PERIODIC` keys (a closure view has no
snapshot). Login check: one real-background cell (2e14, z = 0.5) of both arms,
merged vs 701651b: every image and exposure-map array identical.

## Node-pilot-2b fixes: filling-consistent HSE density, per-donor CCDs (2026-10-09)

**HSE shell density** (author: keep FoF everywhere). Spec fields
`sim.hse_shell_density` (`'geometric'` v32/v32_lowz, `'filling'` v4 and the box
presets) and `sim.hse_min_filling` (None / 0.1). Every truth estimator that pairs
a shell gas density with a cell-averaged T or P (`th_vw`, `th+kin`, all Barnes
2021 variants incl. `barnes21_mock`, Pearce 2020, the truth-profile phases and
their HSE curves) now uses ρ = Σm / ΣV_cell over the selected cells (V = m/ρ),
the density of the gas present; the shell filling factor ΣV_cell/V_shell is
recorded (`gas/<phase>/fill`, truth-profile format 2) and shells with fill < 0.1
are omitted from every HSE curve (0.1: the non-SF zoom gas fills ~1 everywhere;
the FoF non-SF gas fills ≥ 0.63 at R500c in all 22 validation halos, so the cut
only removes the filament-dominated shells far outside R500c, where a gradient of
a few clumps says nothing about the shell). The X-ray-like estimators (shell
median / volume-weighted n_e) and `th_med` were already consistent. M_gas(<r),
M_tot(<r) stay the FoF particle sums. `m500c_hse_msun` is nullable with the flag
`hse_crossing` (`none` / `single` / `multiple`); the headline is the INNERMOST
downward crossing of 500 ρ_c beyond 0.4 R500c (unchanged rule, now documented
and flagged); C12 requires NaN exactly when `none`.

Validation (login node, 22 TNG-Cluster halos recomputed from the snapshots with
the v4 keywords, both selections and both densities; zoom-geometric reproduces
node pilot 1 exactly, FoF-geometric node pilot 2b exactly). Barnes21-nosf
M_HSE(<R500c)/M500c and headline M500_HSE/M500c, medians [16-84 %]:

| | zoom (old) | FoF old | FoF new |
|---|---|---|---|
| z > 0.7 (15 halos, snaps 45/50/55), Mhse(<R500)/M500c | 0.810 [0.76, 1.02] | 1.194 [1.00, 1.39] | 0.731 [0.50, 0.88] |
| z > 0.7, M500_HSE/M500c | 0.848 [0.66, 1.05] | 1.285 [1.01, 1.48] | 0.781 [0.46, 0.92] |
| z < 0.3 (7 halos, snaps 84/91/99), Mhse(<R500)/M500c | 0.836 | 0.920 | 0.820 |
| z < 0.3, M500_HSE/M500c | 0.843 | 0.892 | 0.817 |

Per halo, FoF-new / zoom: z < 0.3 median 0.980 at R500c (7/7 within 10 %),
headline 0.994 (7/7 within 10 %); z > 0.7 median 0.840 at R500c (2/15 within
10 %), headline 0.925 (10/15 within 10 %). FoF-old / zoom: 1.319 and 1.489 at
z > 0.7, 1.097 / 1.059 at z < 0.3. Zoom-filling / zoom-geometric = 0.998
(the zoom gas fills its shells). The ACCEPTANCE "FoF-new ≈ zoom inside R500c"
HOLDS at low z, and where the FoF gas fills its shells at every z (median FoF
fill 0.99 at 0.7 R500c for z > 0.7; in s045 h7122163 the Barnes profiles agree to
3 % out to 0.83 R500c), but FAILS at R500c for z > 0.7: −16 % (median) where FoF fills
0.63-0.82 of the R500c shell. The residual is the FoF selection itself, not the
density definition: the FoF members near the group boundary are the denser gas,
and the estimators that were already filling-consistent (X-ray-like shell
medians, `th_med`) show the same FoF/zoom ratio at z > 0.7 (0.79-0.82 at R500c,
0.87-0.91 for M500). Only a spherical (non-FoF) gas selection for the HSE truth
removes it; not switched (author's call). Halos present in both pilots:
s045 h7122163 at R500c zoom 0.768 / FoF-old 1.401 / FoF-new 0.609, M500 0.848 /
1.339 / 0.784; s050 h10228605 0.775 / 0.988 / 0.731, 0.808 / 0.989 / 0.781;
s091 h4215937 0.691 / 0.730 / 0.677, 0.598 / 0.600 / 0.596; s099 h252455
(zoom from pilot 1, FoF only recomputed) 0.879 / 0.950 / 0.835, 0.867 / 0.927 /
0.838.

**Per-donor active CCDs** (author: "if the donor dropped MOS1 CCDs, we drop them
too"). Spec field `instrument.active_ccds` (`'mask'` v32/v32_lowz: the packaged
2018 masks; `'donor'` v4 and the box presets). Data-package products (`tngxmm
donors active-ccds`, sha256 in `$TNGXMM_DATA/tables/SHA256SUMS`):
`epic_ccd_maps.fits` (per-segment label maps of the 900² detector grid: MOS CCD
squares from EMOS1/2_LINCOORD_0019, focal-plane Y mirrored and rotated by 270°
(MOS1) / 0° (MOS2), the orientation that holds every packaged-mask pixel and
puts the two missing MOS1 slots at CCD3/CCD6; MOS1 CCD3, CCD6 and segment 14 =
the outer stripe of CCD4 whose columns are blanked on board since the
2012-12-11 event, measured on the 89 pre-2005 MOS1 PPS maps stacked in the mask
frame, median footprint eroded by 2 px of attitude smearing so the stack
reproduces the packaged area of CCDs 2/5/7 to 0.2 %: 53 026, 52 060 and 8 889
mask pixels) and `donor_active_ccds.csv` (per observation and camera the
segments with ≥ 50 % of their read-out pixels exposed in the donor's own 0.5-1
keV PPS EXPMAP, the fractions, the PPS file, the epoch rule). 638 observations:
MOS1 353 × {1,2,4,5,7}, 195 × {1,2,3,4,5,7,14}, 89 × all; the measured set
equals the epoch rule for every donor with a map (exposed fractions ≥ 0.99 or
≤ 0.003) except 0747420101 (excluded donor, 57' core offset, anomalous
products); MOS2 and pn: every CCD in every donor (pn not relevant); 7 donors
without a MOS map and 9 without a pn map use the epoch rule (`CCDSRC =
date-rule`). Instrument: the CCD-masked response (mask, active pixels, QPB per
area) before the readout windows; a post-2012 donor keeps the packaged response
object (products byte-identical to `'mask'`). Headers `CCDRULE`, `CCDLIST`,
`CCDSRC`.

Mock / real PPS EXPMAP area (band 0.5-1 keV, EXPMAP > 0, 40 donors per epoch
with their own windows, inverse-map construction), median: MOS1 0.951 (2013+),
0.943 (2005-2012), 0.948 (pre-2005) — was 0.951 / 0.756 / 0.642; MOS2 0.952,
pn 0.915 in every epoch (unchanged). Login regeneration (4 TNG-Cluster views at
snaps 45/78/87 incl. a pre-2005 and a 2005-2012 MOS1 donor and the windowed
s045 donor, 1 TNG300 view with a 2005-2012 donor): campaign check PASS in both
trees (C12/C16 PASS, hse_crossing single); per-view MOS1 area ratios 0.935-0.953.

Spec hashes (116 hashed fields): v32 `c8211e17a1fca690`, v32_lowz
`eaa2e09c147c5feb`, v4 `ab580b804afd7e52`, v4_tng300 `d7772bed257ed1f5`,

## Campaign Lx background: masked-clipped, no silent clipping (2026-10-09, campaign check C6)

**Finding** (C6 on node check 3, `prod/nodecheck3_v4`): s045 h12196778 v0 stored
`lx_r500 = 0` with the aperture fully covered. A mock AGN of 2.2e-13 erg/s/cm² (~20 000
counts, ~100x the median source) sat 85″ from the cluster, in the R500 background annulus
(64″ aperture): the plain annulus mean rose from ~2 to 9.95 counts/px, the net counts went to
−3310, and `n_net = max(..., 0)` plus the NaN→0 mapping of the generation ("V3.1
convention") wrote 0. The same error class put sources inside the 1 and 1.5 Mpc apertures
(h12196778: 3.5x and 3.3x the intrinsic Lx; TNG300 s050 h9: 2.5x and 4.4x) or in their annuli
(s045 h14304468: `lx_1mpc = 0`). `lx_1mpc` is the mass seed of the real and mock fits.

**Decision**: spec field `photometry.lx_bkg` — `'mean'` for v32/v32_lowz (legacy, outputs
byte-identical), `'masked-clipped'` for v4 and the box presets
(`photometry/estimate_lx_background.py`, `measure_lx.campaign_lx`):

- point sources masked with the hydrostatic fit's source lists, the same rule on both sides:
  mocks the view's `pts_sources.fits` above the per-donor density threshold
  (`massfit.mock_source_min_flux_cgs`), real data the PPS point sources with
  `EP_DET_ML > massfit.source_det_ml_min` (`measure_lx.view_lx_sources(kind='obs')`);
- mask radius from the EPIC on-axis King PSF (r0 5.495″, α 1.588): at least the fit's 30″
  (EEF 0.87), extended until N·K(r)·A_pix falls to 0.1 of the local level (N from the image:
  counts within 30″ above the local ring level, over the EEF), at most 150″. The C6 AGN gets
  ~100″; a fixed 30″ left its wings in the annulus (s045 h7122163 R500 2.3x);
- sources inside the aperture are masked too, as the fit masks them; masked aperture pixels
  are filled with the mean of the unmasked pixels in the same 8″ ring about the centre
  (the cluster's azimuthal profile). Masking only the annulus left the 1/1.5 Mpc outliers
  (up to 3.9x / 5.3x the intrinsic Lx);
- core protection as the fit (90″, sources and pixels), limited to 0.4 of the aperture
  radius: at z > 1 90″ exceeds R500 and would protect the annulus AGN; 0.15 masked
  sources on the cluster core (s045 h7122163, h18768704 R500 flagged `masked`);
- background = sector-clipped annulus mean (12 sectors, 3 x max(Poisson, 1.4826 MAD)),
  the backstop for undetected sources and wings; whole sectors are clipped, not pixels
  (a per-pixel clip at 0.5 counts/px removes the Poisson tail: 9 % low);
- no clipping: `lx_<ap>` NaN unless `lx_<ap>_flag == 'ok'`; flags `ok`, `nondetection`
  (net <= 0), `masked` (masks over > 50 % of the aperture, or < 200 annulus px left),
  `uncovered` (the legacy coverage rules); `lx_<ap>_signed` keeps the signed value;
  `lx_n_sources_masked` (-1: no source list / no usable donor threshold, clipped background
  only). The NaN→0 mapping of the generation is gone for v4; the bank's `lx_zero` and the
  `lx_*_zero` filter rules treat NaN as unmeasurable (mock rows).
- C6 (v4): per aperture, `uncovered` exactly where the aperture is < 98 % covered or the
  covered annulus < 200 px (tolerance bands), NaN (never 0) unless `ok`, the signed value
  consistent; a v4 tree without flag columns fails C6.
- repair without regeneration: `tngxmm photometry remeasure-lx <tree> --preset P` (Kedro
  node `photometry.remeasure_campaign_lx`, `params:photometry.remeasure_lx.roots`) rewrites
  the Lx columns and `obs_meta.json` in place from image, coverage, `pts_sources.fits` and the
  generation inputs of `obs_meta.json`, with provenance (`lx_remeasured`, `lx_pre_remeasure`,
  `*.csv.prelxfix.bak`); idempotent (a second run rewrites identical bytes).

**Validation** (all 42 views of `nodecheck3_v4` re-measured into a copy under
`tngxmm_dev/LXFIX/`, original untouched). Measured / intrinsic (`lx_intr_*`), before (lx > 0)
→ after (flag ok):

| aperture | n | median | scatter (std, dex) | MAD (dex) | range |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R500 | 38 → 40 | 0.765 → 0.776 | 0.127 → 0.108 | 0.088 → 0.079 | 0.27–1.17 → 0.34–1.13 |
| 1 Mpc | 40 → 41 | 0.823 → 0.848 | 0.215 → 0.096 | 0.107 → 0.099 | 0.14–3.53 → 0.57–1.44 |
| 1.5 Mpc | 34 → 36 | 0.898 → 0.918 | 0.287 → 0.147 | 0.201 → 0.102 | 0.16–4.43 → 0.39–1.56 |

Outliers: h12196778 R500 0 → 0.58, 1 Mpc 3.53 → 0.92, 1.5 Mpc 3.32 → 0.97; TNG300 s050 h9
R500 0 → 0.64, 1 Mpc 2.49 → 0.60, 1.5 Mpc 4.43 → 1.39; h14304468 1 Mpc 0 → 0.69; s050
h19905640 / h16176788 1.5 Mpc 0 → 1.03 / 0.39. Every former 0 is now either measured (5
apertures) or `uncovered` (9, all at z < 0.1 with the aperture off the detector). Flags:
R500 40 ok / 2 uncovered, 1 Mpc 41 / 1, 1.5 Mpc 36 / 6; no `nondetection` or `masked`.
Clean apertures: only 5 of 126 have no listed source within reach (with ~100-300 sources
above the donor threshold per field); 3 agree with the legacy value to 4e-8, 2 differ by
+6.5 % / +5.7 % (a clipped sector). Elsewhere the median |change| is 7 % (AGN flux removed

the new preset hashes, as for any tree made before a spec change. Every original results
column other than `lx_*` and `exposure_eff_s` (recomputed identically) is byte-unchanged.

Not changed: `measure_apertures` (the angular-background photometry of `measure_mock_lx` /
`measure_obs_lx`, which gives the real fits' `lx_1mpc_peak` seed) has the same plain-mean
background and `max(n_net, 0)`; outside this fix's scope (one estimator per table).  Superseded for v4 by "Fit seed Lx" below
(the seeds of both sides now come from `measure_lx.seed_lx`).

Spec hashes (129 hashed fields: `photometry.lx_bkg` and twelve `lx_*` parameters): v32
`c04de6d9f1fc3181`, v32_lowz `746cfa47921ec78d`, v4 `63086c35239d2d2d`, v4_tng300

**Masked-area correction (author requirement 2026-10-09: AGN filtered in the source apertures
as well as the annuli, for every measured Lx, identical on mocks and real data; the correction
documented and validated).**

- What is masked: the same list and radius rule in the aperture and the annulus of each of
  `lx_r500`, `lx_1mpc`, `lx_1p5mpc`. Source list: real data the PPS point sources
  (`EP_EXTENT = 0`, `EP_DET_ML > 10`), the list the real fits mask; mocks the
  `pts_sources.fits` AGN above the per-donor density threshold. Chosen over running a
  detection on the mocks: the mock pipeline has no emldetect step, and the density-matched
  threshold is by construction the detection-equivalent list the mock fits already mask
  (same list on both sides of the fit and of the Lx). Real observations go through the same
  function: `measure_obs_lx.measure_obs_lx_masked` (fixture A1835 0551830101 at the X-ray
  peak: 70 PPS sources masked, R500 15 % masked, area_corr 1.019, Lx 1.179e45 vs 1.221e45
  in the angular-background convention; A2029 0551780401: 1 Mpc 5.075e44 vs 4.990e44).
- Correction: masked aperture pixels take the mean counts of the unmasked pixels in the same
  8″ ring about the centre, i.e. the azimuthal area-fraction correction per ring,
  C_corr = Σ_unmasked C + Σ_k n_mask(k) ⟨C⟩_k, then N_net = C_corr − B·N_ap
  (`estimate_lx_background.ring_filled_sum`: formula, assumptions, limits, precedent
  `vikhlinin2006`, `pratt2009`, ledger rows 48-49). Assumption: azimuthal symmetry within a
  ring, masks independent of cluster structure (true for mock AGN). No profile model, so no
  beta-model parameters. Per view: `lx_<ap>_mask_frac`, `lx_<ap>_area_corr`; > 50 % masked →
  flag `masked`, Lx NaN.
- Validation by mask injection (all 42 nodecheck3_v4 views, R500 and 1 Mpc apertures,
  1/2/4/8 extra masks on empty positions with the view's own mask radii, 20 draws each,
  6480 trials; corrected net counts over the view's own measurement):

  | masked fraction | n | ring fill median [16-84 %] | global area scaling median [16-84 %] |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 0-0.05 | 511 | 1.000 [0.999, 1.002] | 1.025 [0.986, 1.037] |
  | 0.05-0.1 | 1140 | 1.000 [0.996, 1.006] | 1.039 [1.017, 1.063] |
  | 0.1-0.2 | 1555 | 1.000 [0.988, 1.021] | 1.102 [1.054, 1.161] |
  | 0.2-0.3 | 770 | 1.002 [0.967, 1.039] | 1.218 [1.141, 1.288] |
  | 0.3-0.5 | 1551 | 1.002 [0.958, 1.051] | 1.420 [1.287, 1.606] |
  | 0.5-1 | 953 | 1.019 [0.894, 1.179] (mean 1.047) | 1.969 [1.642, 2.722] |

  The ring fill is unbiased to 0.2 % up to half the aperture masked, with a scatter growing
  to ±5 %; beyond 50 % it drifts (+5 % mean, ±15 %), hence the flag. A global area scaling
  is biased high by 2.5-42 % (the radial gradient). Against the intrinsic Lx on the repaired
  tree (117 ok apertures; masked fraction median 0.066, max 0.41; area_corr median 1.02,
  range 0.977-1.194): measured/intrinsic median 0.840 / 0.814 / 0.834 / 0.881 for masked
  fractions 0-0.05 / 0.05-0.1 / 0.1-0.2 / 0.2-0.5 (scatter 0.11-0.14 dex): no trend with the
  masked fraction.


## Spherical hydrostatic truth (2026-10-09)

**Decision (author).** The hydrostatic / intrinsic truth (every HSE estimator column,
`m500c_hse_msun`, `hse_crossing`, the particle M500 at z_snap and z_obs, the
truth-profile curves) uses ALL particles in a sphere about `GroupPos`, in every
simulation; photons, maps and `*_tngref` stay FoF; the `*_mock` estimators keep the
mock's cells (the FoF members inside the sphere). The filling-consistent density
(`hse_shell_density = 'filling'`, `hse_min_filling = 0.1`) and the `fill` datasets are
kept; with a sphere the non-SF filling factor at R500c is ~1 and becomes a sanity check
(campaign check C17, window [0.9, 1.1]).

**Implementation.** Spec fields `sim.truth_selection_hse` (`'same-as-truth'` v32/v32_lowz,
`'sphere'` v4 and the box presets) and `sim.truth_sphere_r200c` (3.0). A separate stage
after the generation, per (simulation, snapshot): `sim/truth_sphere.py`, `python -m
tngxmm.core.runner campaign truth-sphere`, `slurm/truth_sphere.sh`; in Kedro inside
`generate_views` after each run's pool. Sphere radius `r_cut = max(3 R200c, 1.02 max(3.15
R500c, 2 R200c))` (the second term: the outermost radius any truth estimator reads).

- Periodic boxes: one pass over the snapshot's chunk files (they are consecutive slices
  of the particle list, not sub-volumes, so every chunk is read and the selections are
  accumulated before any profile). Per chunk the Coordinates of gas, DM, stars and BHs
  are wrapped into [0, L), binned on a voxel grid; ONE `cKDTree` of the snapshot's halo
  centres (`boxsize = L`) is queried per occupied voxel (all spheres that can reach it,
  any overlap), then the exact minimum-image test decides each (row, sphere) pair. A
  query per row cost 30 s for a dense 25 M-cell chunk; per voxel 13 s.
- TNG-Cluster: the halo's original zoom (FoF + outer-fuzz ranges of the offsets file),
  cut to the sphere, one zoom per worker; other zooms are never mixed in. A sphere that
  reaches low-resolution DM (PartType2) within `r_max` is flagged (`sphere_contaminated`,
  counts and the nearest distance recorded); PartType2 never enters the truth (as the
  zoom truth of node pilot 1).
- Do once, reuse: the selected global indices per halo and type are persisted
  (`<tree>/truth_cutouts/<sim>/snap_<snap>.h5`, delta-encoded int64, sha256 per array);
  the truth can be recomputed from them alone, reading only the selected rows (a box in
  gather passes: each chunk read once for the union of the rows of a batch of halos
  bounded by `--gather-gb`). The truth from the stream and from the cutouts is identical
  bit for bit (unit test on a synthetic box and zoom; the 22 halos below).
- The stage REPLACES the generation's truth: `truth_profile.h5` format 3 with
  `truth_selection = 'sphere'` and `sphere_*` attributes; then the truth columns of the
  results CSVs and `obs_meta.json` are rewritten from the files with each row's z_obs
  (values only, no column added, CRLF kept, first original kept as
  `.pre_sphere_truth.bak`). Resumable (a halo whose file holds the spherical truth of the
  current spec hash is skipped) and idempotent (the apply step can be rerun any time;
  a generation resumed after the stage never overwrites a spherical file, its new rows
  are fixed by rerunning the apply step). The generation itself is unchanged (it still
  computes its FoF truth, which the stage supersedes).

**Validation (1), TNG-Cluster: the 22 halos of the node-pilot-2b validation** (stage on
the login node from the snapshots, v4 keywords). With the geometric density (node pilot
1's definition) the spherical truth reproduces node pilot 1's zoom truth BIT FOR BIT in
all 22 halos (all 34 numbers per halo: M_HSE(<R500c)/M500c and M500_self of 17
estimators, the mock ones included); with the v4 filling density it equals the FIX2
zoom-filling truth bit for bit; the particle M500 at z_snap and z_obs equal pilot 1's
rows exactly; stream (a) and cutouts (b) truth files identical in all 22. Barnes21-nosf,
medians [16-84 %]:

| | sphere (v4, filling) | zoom pilot 1 (geometric) | FoF-new (pilot 2b) |
|---|---|---|---|
| z > 0.7 (15), Mhse(<R500c)/M500c | 0.807 [0.74, 1.03] | 0.810 [0.76, 1.02] | 0.731 [0.50, 0.88] |
| z > 0.7, M500_HSE/M500c | 0.851 [0.67, 1.05] | 0.848 [0.66, 1.05] | 0.781 [0.46, 0.91] |
| z < 0.3 (7), Mhse(<R500c)/M500c | 0.840 [0.68, 0.94] | 0.836 [0.68, 0.94] | 0.820 [0.67, 0.93] |
| z < 0.3, M500_HSE/M500c | 0.842 [0.59, 0.94] | 0.843 [0.59, 0.95] | 0.817 [0.58, 0.91] |

Per halo sphere / pilot 1: median 0.998 at R500c and 0.999 for the headline (22/22
within 10 %; the difference is the density definition only); sphere / FoF-new at z > 0.7:
1.193 at R500c (3/15 within 10 %) and 1.084 headline: the −16 % FoF deficit is gone.
Non-SF filling factor at R500c: median 1.0008, range [0.9995, 1.0021]. One sphere reaches
low-resolution DM inside r_max: s099 h18083780 (113 particles, nearest at 0.75 r_max =
2.37 Mpc); in the other 21 the nearest low-res particle is at 1.27-4.67 r_max.
Cost per halo on the login node (3 processes on 4 cores): (a) 40-90 s (zoom read +
truth), (b) 20-80 s; peak RSS 17.3 GB for the largest (s091 h4215937, 6.8e7 sphere
particles), ~0.25-0.4 GB per million sphere particles. Cutout index files: 6-15 MB per
snapshot for 2-5 halos (before the delta encoding).

**Validation (2), TNG300 edge halo** (login node, author's limit: 5 chunk files, one at a
time, RSS < 10 GB): snapshot 99, all 159 targets as spheres; halo 13 (centre x = 204287
of 205000 ckpc/h, 0.20 r_cut from the face, its sphere wraps): the stream selection on
chunks 6, 7, 300, 598, 599 equals the brute-force minimum-image selection on exactly those
chunks for it and the three next nearest-face halos, all particle types; the gathered
fields equal the stream's.

**Validation (3), throughput and projection.** TNG300 chunk: 1.23 GB of coordinates
(gas + DM + stars + BH); read 1.0-7.2 s (0.2-1.3 GB/s single stream); selection of 159
spheres 24-25 s in the dense cluster chunks (50 M rows), 7-10 s elsewhere; fields of the
selected rows in-stream 4-11 s; gather of the union of 159 spheres 6-22 s. Coordinates
per snapshot: TNG300 739 GB (600 chunks), TNG100 286 GB (448), TNG50 483 GB (680). Index
storage: 0.05 bytes per index in cluster chunks, 2.6 in scattered fuzz. Projected
per-halo truth memory from the catalogue FoF lengths x the measured sphere/FoF ratio (gas
2.0, DM 1.6): TNG300 median 6 GB (max ~50 GB at z = 0), TNG100 25-80 GB, TNG50 ~450 GB.
Projected node time (`slurm/truth_sphere.sh`, lanes sequential): TNG-Cluster 4,755
halo-snapshots x ~55 s / 16 workers ~ 4.5 h; TNG300 per snapshot ~8-10 min stream (24
workers; 2.6 core-h of selection, 739 GB of I/O) + 1-3 gather passes of ~3 min + truth
(159 halos x ~20 s / 16) ~ 10-25 min, 17 snapshots ~4 h; TNG100 14 snapshots ~2-3 h (3
truth workers); total ~11-12 h (limit 24 h). Cutout files: TNG-Cluster ~2-5 MB per halo
(~10-15 GB in all), TNG300 ~2.5 GB for snapshot 99, ~15-20 GB in all. TNG50 (7 halo
snapshots, ~1.1e9 particles per sphere) exceeds a 250 GB node with the in-memory truth
and is left out of the default job; it is computed by the streamed truth (section "Streamed
spherical truth", 2026-10-13, below).

Spec hashes (118 hashed fields): v32 `6b30afa402d2c67e`, v32_lowz `94c322f1acb77726`, v4
`7bc5c8f30e0e1246`, v4_tng300 `32e1bfdf13150e91`, v4_tng100 `9dfa25bac2023607`, v4_tng50
`7aff90adb3046ecd` (the legacy hashes move with the new fields; legacy products
unchanged).

## Streamed spherical truth: TNG50 on a normal node (2026-10-13)

**Problem.** The in-memory truth (`compute_hse_mass.compute_hse_truth` through
`truth_sphere.compute_sphere_truth`) holds every particle of the sphere and ~40 derived
float64 arrays per gas cell: 0.25-0.4 GB per million particles. A TNG50-1 halo 0 (M200c
1.8e14, R200c 1.2 Mpc, `r_cut` 3.6 Mpc, 1.1-1.4e9 particles from the FoF lengths 2.6e8 gas,
4.2e8 DM, 1.3e8 stars x the measured sphere/FoF ratios) needs ~450 GB; the author wants it on
a normal p.vera node (250 GB), not p.huge.

**Decision.** The estimators need only per-shell sums on fixed radial grids, and sums are
additive over chunks. `sim/compute_hse_stream.py::compute_hse_truth_streamed` reads the
sphere chunk by chunk (`source.iter_particles`; `truth_sphere.StreamedSphereSource` reads
consecutive slices of the sphere's sorted global rows from the snapshot, never the whole
sphere), runs the reference's per-cell arithmetic on the chunk, adds the chunk's shell
sums to running totals and drops it. The estimators (finite differences, the Vikhlinin
fits, M500 / R500 crossings, every truth-profile array, `_truth_profile_finish`) are then
the reference's own functions on the totals. The in-memory path is kept unchanged as the
reference. `truth_mode` (`campaign truth-sphere --truth-mode`, `run_snapshot(truth_mode=)`):
`memory`, `streamed`, or `auto` (default: streamed above `truth_sphere.STREAM_ABOVE_PARTICLES`
= 3e8 particles per sphere, i.e. TNG50 only; every sphere of the existing TNG-Cluster /
TNG300 / TNG100 campaigns stays in memory, so their products are unchanged). No spec field
(no spec hash moves). A streamed sphere is never gathered. The truth-profile file format is
unchanged; a streamed file carries one additional attribute, `sphere_truth_mode =
'streamed'`.

**What is accumulated, and why it is the same number.**
- Shell sums (mass, cell volume, n_e V, T m, T w_sl, P V, kinetic moments, emission
  weights, n_cells, the Barnes (50 linear), Pearce (50 log), HSE (48 log) and truth-profile
  grids, the three phases non-SF / hot / mock): `numpy.bincount` with the running total of
  every bin put in front of the chunk's weights. bincount adds the weights to their bins
  one after the other in input order, so this continues the reference's single sequential
  sum over all the cells: the shell sums are BIT-IDENTICAL to the reference's whatever the
  chunk size (unit test: float32 weights, a mask, chunkings of 1, 6 and 60 pieces).
- Sums the reference takes with numpy's pairwise `.sum()` over the cells inside R500c
  (R500c temperatures, the emission-weighted metallicity, the mock's R500c temperatures) and
  the float32 inner mass `m_in`: the pieces are kept in sphere order (~50 B per cell inside
  R500c, small) and summed once, the same array, the same bits (the float32 ones would
  otherwise differ by 1e-7).
- Shell MEDIANS (`th_med` P and rho of the non-SF gas, `X_*_nemed` n_e of the hot gas, the
  truth profile's `ne_med` of the non-SF / hot / mock phases) are EXACT, not binned or
  approximated: the per-cell values (float64 P, n_e, native float32 rho, two int16 shell
  indices, the hot flag: 29 bytes per non-SF cell inside 3.15 R500c; mock: 12 bytes) are
  kept and reduced with `numpy.median` per shell after the pass. This buffer, ~10 GB for a
  TNG50 halo 0, is the only state that grows with the number of gas cells (not with the
  dark matter, ~60 % of a sphere); a further reduction (two-pass histogram bisection) is not
  needed.
- Cumulative particle masses (`mass_profile`, the 4000-radius grid for the M500c crossing,
  `truth_profile/mass/*`; DM, stars, BH and gas): per-gap mass histogram of each chunk,
  cumulated at the end. The reference takes `cumsum` over the radius-sorted particles; the
  two are sums of the same numbers in a different order, equal to ~5e-11 (the reference is the less accurate: its sequential
  float64 cumsum of 3e6 equal DM masses is off by 2e-11 and of 3e7 by 5e-10, the per-gap histogram by 1e-14). The
  quantities built on them differ by the same amount: `m500c_particles_msun`, the
  `*_avg0.8-1.2` columns of the literature estimators, `M_*` of the mass group. Nothing else
  does: the headline `barnes21_nosf_fit_mu` and every other estimator column are
  bit-identical.
- DM, stars, BH (the bulk of the particles): coordinates (and masses) only, one pass each.

**Equality (measured).**
- Unit tests (`tests/unit/test_sim_hse_stream.py`, synthetic zoom and the synthetic box
  through the stage): all arrays of the result (summary, profiles, mass profile, the whole
  truth-profile structure) bit-identical to the reference except the cumulative-mass family
  (<= 3e-14 here, tolerance 1e-9), for chunk sizes from 99 rows, with and without
  `GFM_Metallicity`, `GFM_Metals`, `GFM_CoolingRate`, `profile`, the mock, a mock radius, both
  shell densities, `mu059_fit`, `nosf_fd`; the stage's truth files (`truth_mode` memory vs
  streamed, streamed from fields held by the stream) likewise.
- TNG-Cluster halo 20179195 (snapshot 40, 5.8e6 particles, `campaign_v4_litb` cutout),
  login node: 283 of 301 arrays bit-identical, the other 18 are the cumulative-mass family:
  largest relative difference 4.8e-11 (`M_dm`), `m500c_particles_msun` 4.2e-11,
  `barnes21_*_avg0.8-1.2` 3.1-3.4e-11; the headline fit `barnes21_nosf_fit_mu_M500self`,
  `th_med` and `true` identical. Through the stage (`run_snapshot` mode `cutouts`): the
  streamed and the in-memory `truth_profile.h5` have 246 bit-identical arrays and 19 in the
  mass family (<= 5e-11).
- TNG300-1 halo 285 (snapshot 99, 5.2e6 particles, the box path: rows scattered over all 600
  chunk files): 283 of 301 arrays bit-identical, the 18 others the cumulative-mass family
  (largest 4.2e-11 `M_dm`, `m500c_particles_msun` 1.6e-11, `*_avg0.8-1.2` 0.6-1.6e-11).
- The headline fitted mass `barnes21_nosf_fit_mu_*` is bit-identical because the fit inputs
  (the Barnes-grid shell sums) are; with plain chunked `bincount` sums (no carry-in) the
  fit moved by 3e-8 in M500c-self and 2e-6 in a single shell of the profile (the
  `least_squares` fits converge to xtol 1e-10 from inputs that differ in the last bits):
  that is why the sums are made exactly sequential.

**Memory (measured, projected).** Peak RSS of the process (`ru_maxrss`, login node), before
the truth (imports, group catalogue, the sphere's indices) and at the end:

| halo | particles (gas selected) | in memory | streamed |
|---|---|---|---|
| TNG-Cluster 20179195, snap 40 | 5.8e6 (2.0e6) | 1.01 GB (0.13 before) | 0.98 GB, 2 M-row chunks; 0.42 GB, 250 k-row chunks |
| TNG300 285, snap 99 | 5.2e6 (1.8e6) | 1.15 GB | 1.19 GB, 1 M-row chunks |
| TNG100 5, snap 67 | 5.0e7 (1.8e7) | not run (~15-20 GB) | 2.91 GB (0.62 before) |
| TNG100 11, snap 78 | 5.2e7 (1.9e7) | not run | 2.92 GB (0.64 before), 8 readers |

The chunk working set is 360 B per gas row (TNG-Cluster halo, chunks of 0.5 M / 1 M / 2 M rows:
0.51 / 0.69 / 1.04 GB). What grows with the sphere is the state of the pass, measured on TNG100
halo 11 (`stream_stats`): median buffers 32 B and pieces for the pairwise sums 59 B per selected
gas cell (cells inside 3.15 R500c; the 18.9e6 cells hold 0.60 + 1.12 GB), 2.28 GB above the
baseline in all, ~100 B per selected gas cell with the transient of the median reduction.
Projection for TNG50 halo 0 (sphere 1.1-1.4e9 particles; gas 4-5e8 in the sphere, 3.5-4.5e8
inside 3.15 R500c; indices 1.3e9 x 8 B):

| item | GB |
|---|---|
| sphere indices (int64, shared by the forked workers) | ~10 |
| gas state of the pass (100 B x 3.5e8; 4.5e8: 45) | ~35 |
| one gas chunk of 2e6 rows (360 B/row) | ~0.7 |
| Python, h5py, catalogue | ~0.5 |
| **peak of one truth process** | **~46 (upper bound ~60)** |

The selection stage (stream pass, 24 chunk workers, then `write_cutouts` / `read_cutouts` of
~1e9 indices) peaks at ~30 GB. Both are far below the ~450 GB in memory and the 250 GB of a
normal node; four TNG50 snapshots (78, 84, 91, 99; one halo each) fit side by side
(`TNG50_SNAP_PARALLEL`).

**Time.** The arithmetic is cheap (login node, one core, warm cache: gas main pass ~2 us per
row, mock pass ~1 us, DM 0.55 us); the cost is the READ of rows scattered over the whole
snapshot (the outer fuzz and other FoF groups of the sphere), latency bound on a cold file
system: one reader gets ~30 000 gas rows/s (TNG100 halo 5: 22e6 gas rows, 734 s of 896 s;
TNG300 halo 285: 2.3e6 rows, 819 s of 870 s; the same for the in-memory truth, 904 s). The
streamed source therefore has reader processes ahead of the truth process
(`--read-workers`, `StreamedSphereSource.iter_particles`: forked readers read the next
`read_workers + 2` slices of the sorted rows, the consumer takes them in order; identical
arrays, unit-tested): 8 readers on the 2-core login node read 122 000 gas rows/s
(TNG100 halo 11, 52e6 particles: 250 s in all instead of ~900 s). TNG50 halo 0 (5e8 gas rows,
2.2e8 mock rows, 7.5e8 DM): ~1 h per snapshot on the login node's 2 cores with 8 readers,
expected 20-30 min with 16 readers on the node; plus the stream pass (24 chunk workers) and
the cutout writing. Four snapshots one after the other: ~4-6 h.

**Run.** `slurm/truth_sphere.sh` with `SIMS="v4_tng50"`: `TNG50_TRUTH_MODE=streamed`
(default), `TNG50_WORKERS=1` (one halo per snapshot), `TNG50_CHUNK_WORKERS=24` (the stream
pass), `TNG50_READ_WORKERS=16` (readers per truth process), `TNG50_SNAP_PARALLEL=1` (3 snapshots
side by side: ~140 GB), `--no-stream-fields` as for every box; `TNG300_TRUTH_MODE` /
`TNG100_TRUTH_MODE` default `auto`. Not submitted.

## Fit seed Lx: mocks and real data through the same function (2026-10-09)

**Author decision**: the real fits' mass seed is measured with the SAME pipeline as the
mocks' (same function, apertures, masking rule family, centre definition).  Before, the real
seed `lx_1mpc_peak` came from `measure_apertures` (11-14′ angular annulus, plain mean, net
clipped at 0; legacy `obs_lx_apertures_recentred.csv`), the mock seed of
`build-fit-targets` from the same function, while the campaign columns used the
masked-clipped estimator.

**Switch**: spec field `massfit.seed_lx_photometry` — `'angbkg'` (v32, v32_lowz:
`measure_apertures`, legacy outputs unchanged) or `'campaign'` (v4 and the box presets):
`photometry/measure_lx.py::seed_lx`, the campaign estimator (`photometry.lx_bkg =
'masked-clipped'`, shared body with `campaign_lx`) on 0.35 Mpc, R500, 1 Mpc, NaN unless the
flag is `ok`.

- mocks: `build-fit-targets --preset v4` (`measure_seed`) at `CLUSTX/CLUSTY`, the view's AGN
  above the per-donor density threshold masked; `lx_1mpc` equals the campaign column;
- real: `tngxmm photometry obs-seed-lx` (`photometry/build_obs_seed_lx.py`) on the built
  0.5-2 keV mosaic with the summed band-2 PPS exposure maps, at the 16″ X-ray peak (the fit
  centre, as `CLUSTX/CLUSTY` is the mock fit's), the PPS point sources the real fit masks
  (`<realfit_v4>/pps_srclist`, `EP_EXTENT = 0`, `EP_DET_ML > 10`; 2 observations without a
  list: clipped background only, `lx_n_sources_masked = -1`); z, N_H, `teff_s` and the
  catalogue M500 (R500, T for the ECF) from `observations.csv`.  Product:
  `$TNGXMM_DATA/tables/obs_seed_lx_v4.csv`, 638 rows, sha256
  `ff21e0ad4c560713adbcd8c35b127ec3874c4488c04aa048179207b66929a597` (in `SHA256SUMS`),
  provenance `obs_seed_lx_v4.provenance.json` (spec hash, git hash 18eb2b6, input
  checksums, command).  `build-realfit-targets` reads it (centres checked to < 1″).
- Not changed: the ML photometry family `seed_lx_*_erg_s` (`measure_fixed_apertures`,
  never read by the fitter) and the closure grid (intrinsic-Lx seeds).

**Calibration re-checked.**  The 1 Mpc constants (M0 2.030, slope 1.792) were fit to the
campaign 'mean' `lx_1mpc` (local 1.1-1.6 r annulus), not to `measure_apertures` (own fit on the
same 13127 views: M0 1.824, slope 1.569): the old real seed applied a calibration of another
estimator (on the mocks guess/true M500 = 1.055 [0.74, 1.47]).  The 0.35 Mpc constants are the
`measure_apertures` fit (reproduced exactly); the core-ratio constants (0.5691, 0.0638) are
not reproduced by either table (`measure_apertures`: A 0.551, B 0.219).  New
`guess_mass.SEED_CALIBRATIONS['campaign']`, all 13143 ok views of `campaign_v32` re-measured
with `seed_lx` under the v4 spec (CLUSTX/CLUSTY, per-donor AGN; true M500c, z_snap; flag ok):

| relation | n | M0 / A | slope / B | scatter |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Mpc M-L | 13112 | 1.987 | 1.742 | 0.153 dex |
| 0.35 Mpc M-L | 12772 | 2.834 | 2.467 | 0.200 dex |
| c200 core ratio | 12664 | 0.5555 | 0.1864 | 0.193 dex |

guess/true M500 median 1.008 (0.153 dex).  Mock flags: 1 Mpc 13112 ok / 31 uncovered;
0.35 Mpc 12772 ok / 360 masked / 11 uncovered.  Masked-clipped / campaign 'mean' `lx_1mpc`
