differ from the v1 preset; changing them moves the v1 spec hash, so they go in with the release that runs the refits.
Evidence comes from the release 1.1.3 closure grid: 216 cells (M500 = 2, 4, 8e14 Msun; z = 0.06, 0.2, 0.5; cc and ncc;
four data modes; three seeds), one arm per job, every arm on the same cells.

| setting | value | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Galactic column of the fit | **total, N_HI + 2 N_H2 (Willingale et al. 2013) of the field** (`massfit.fit_galactic_nh = 'willingale13'`; v1: HI4PI) | the mocks and the real clusters are absorbed by it; with HI4PI the two donors with N_H,tot / N_HI = 1.84-1.88 give M_fit / M_true 1.19-1.51, with the total column 0.78-0.87 like the others; clean-cell scatter at z = 0.2 0.25 -> 0.07 in ln M (job 1889901) |
| background seed where no annulus beyond 2 R500 is on the detector | **the observation's own measured flat level** (`massfit.bg_seed = 'donor-table'`, with the unit fix of this release; v1: `typical-epic`) | the typical level was calibrated on the V4 background and lies 25-37 per cent below the release 1.1 background; the own level is a measurement of the observation, no table fitted to mocks (its vignetted part is still the typical one); closure effect small (z = 0.06 bgonly 0.759 -> 0.775) |
| background prior width | 0.1 dex (unchanged) | 1 dex changes nothing (+0.016 bgonly, -0.040 real; job 1889917) |
| vignetted fraction of the background | free (unchanged) | frozen at the seed changes nothing beyond noise (job 1889917) |
| optimiser | one start (unchanged) | nine starts: -0.009 bgonly, -0.020 real, +0.009 clean at z = 0.06 (job 1889917) |
| chains | quick, 150 + 300 steps (unchanged) | long chains (500 + 1500) move ln M by -0.002 (95 per cent -0.006 to +0.011; job 1889894) |
| cluster PSF in the fit | on-axis King (unchanged) | clusters generated with the energy- and angle-dependent King: +0.001 (-0.021 to +0.019; job 1889922) |
| particle background shape in the fit | flat (unchanged) | mocks with a flat QPB instead of the FWC density: +0.02 to +0.05 (job 1889922) |
| metallicity | free; Gaussian prior 0.3 +- 0.1 only below 20,000 net counts (unchanged) | the Gaussian prior at every level does not move the background cells (bgonly 0.756 / 0.936 / 0.880 against 0.775 / 0.913 / 0.875; job 1889936) |
| c-M prior | Dutton & Maccio (2014) below 20,000 net counts (unchanged) | not active in any closure cell (all above the threshold) |
| responses | `responses_v1` | the release set |
| luminosity of the matching and of the L_X-M test | `lx_1mpc` (fixed 1 Mpc aperture, the campaign seed photometry), mocks and real data alike | no catalogue mass enters it |
| AGN-only closure cells | free background (fixed in this release) | the unresolved AGN make a background; frozen at zero they gave 0.98 / 0.73 / 0.70 (total column) |
| fit QC | the certify rules (prior edges of c200 and Z, outer-pressure run-away, centre, QC exclusion) **plus a flag `no_bg_annulus`** for a fit without a background annulus on the detector (`bg_src` other than `annulus`) | these fits are biased low by about 25 per cent whenever the data hold a background (below); 30 of the 167 V4 real fits (29 at z < 0.13, the last at z = 0.15) |

**Closure under the proposed settings** (job 1889935, arm `v1_frz`). Median M_fit / M_true over the cells:

| data mode | z = 0.06 | z = 0.2 | z = 0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| clean (cluster only) | 1.032 | 0.945 | 0.999 |
| AGN only | 1.091 | 0.998 | 0.822 |
| background only | 0.775 | 0.913 | 0.875 |
| real (background and AGN) | 0.747 | 0.863 | 0.893 |

Against the registered rule (1.00 +- 0.03 in every cell), 13 of the 72 cells pass. The test fails, and it is reported
as it is.

**What is known about the remaining deficit.**
* z = 0.06: the fitted background is about 20 per cent too low in every camera and band below 4.5 keV, at every radius,
  and the cluster model takes that light. The fit is not held to a wrong seed: the corrected own seed is 15-25 per
  cent above the truth. The data alone prefer the low background, because a cluster that fills the field and a flat
  background cannot be told apart there. This is a property of a single pointing of a cluster larger than the field,
  not a setting, hence the QC flag.
* z = 0.2 and 0.5: the fitted background is within 1-3 per cent of the truth in every band, yet M_fit / M_true is
  0.86-0.91 with a background against 0.95-1.00 without one. None of the following is the cause:
  * the background level;
  * the particle background shape;
  * the PSF;
  * the chains;
  * the start;
  * the metallicity prior;
  * the column.

  The per-fit scatter is about 0.1 in ln M, so the median of 18 fits per cell group is known to about 0.03.

**Left open.**
* The cause of the 9-14 per cent deficit with a background at z >= 0.2.
* The S/N dependence of the closure: the thinned arm was not rerun on the release 1.1.3 grid.
* The pn 0.5-1 keV field level of the mocks: +0.20 in ln against the real observations.
* Whether the real refits use quick chains (proposed, as the mocks).

The real refits (638 observations, prepared in `tngxmm_dev/V113/realfit_113`) and pass 1 wait for the author's
sign-off of this section.

### Counts check of the fitter freeze: the rule, written before the run (2026-10-27)

The author's sign-off of the fitter freeze is conditional on this check.

* Run: closure arm `v1_frzthin`, the proposed freeze settings (arm `v1_frz`) on the 216 cells regenerated with each
  absorbed cluster photon kept with probability 0.54. Background, AGN and instrument are unchanged, and the random
  streams are those of the unthinned cells.
* Statistic: for each data mode and redshift (12 groups of 18 cells), the median over the cells of the paired
  difference d = ln(M_fit / M_true)_thinned - ln(M_fit / M_true)_v1_frz, with a 95 per cent bootstrap interval over
  cells (4,000 resamples).
* Rule: the deficit does not depend on counts if, in each of the six groups with a background (bgonly and real, at
  z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5), |median d| <= 0.05 and the 95 per cent interval includes zero. In addition, no group of the
  twelve may have |median d| > 0.10.
* The clean and AGN-only groups are reported, but enter only through the 0.10 limit.
* The tolerance 0.05 is about 1.5 times the standard error of a median of 18 fits with a per-fit scatter of 0.1 in
  ln M.

### Counts check of the fitter freeze: result (2026-10-27). The rule fails.

Job 1889962, arm `v1_frzthin`: 215 of the 216 fits converged. Evaluated with `internal/ledger_tests/closure_counts_check.py`
exactly as stated above (output `closure_113/counts_check.json`). Median d = ln(M_fit / M_true)_thinned -
ln(M_fit / M_true)_v1_frz, with its 95 per cent bootstrap interval:

| data mode | z = 0.06 | z = 0.2 | z = 0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| background only | +0.030 [-0.123, +0.082] | -0.034 [-0.128, +0.026] | **-0.161 [-0.215, -0.026]** |
| real | **+0.053 [-0.134, +0.199]** | -0.030 [-0.113, +0.076] | -0.021 [-0.197, +0.063] |
| clean | -0.020 [-0.051, +0.014] | -0.012 [-0.052, +0.009] | -0.017 [-0.175, +0.052] |
| AGN only | -0.001 [-0.044, +0.032] | -0.002 [-0.063, +0.037] | +0.090 [-0.036, +0.295] |

Two of the six background groups break the rule:
* background only at z = 0.5: the thinned cells give 16 per cent less mass, and the interval excludes zero;
* real at z = 0.06: +0.053, just outside the 0.05 tolerance, with an interval that includes zero.

The freeze is therefore not signed off. The cells without a background do not depend on the counts (|d| <= 0.02).

**The same cells at ten times the exposure** (job 1889966, arm `v1_frzdeep`, clean and background-only cells at
z = 0.2 and 0.5, every camera time x 10; paired with `v1_frz`). Median M_fit / M_true at exposure x 0.54 / x 1 / x 10:

| cells | x 0.54 | x 1 | x 10 |
|---|---|---|---|
| background only, z = 0.2 | 0.834 | 0.913 | 0.865 |
| background only, z = 0.5 | 0.779 | 0.875 | 0.897 |
| clean, z = 0.2 | 0.945 | 0.945 | 0.941 |
| clean, z = 0.5 | 0.973 | 0.999 | 0.962 |

The paired change from x 1 to x 10 is:
* background only: -0.032 [-0.143, +0.066] (z = 0.2) and +0.018 [-0.115, +0.224] (z = 0.5);
* clean: +0.002 and -0.068.

Ten times the counts does not remove the deficit with a background (0.87-0.90). The deficit is not a low-count bias
that vanishes with depth; fewer counts make it larger at z = 0.5. Both point to a mismatch between the fit model and
the data, not to statistics.

### The background-cell deficit: what the fit gets wrong (2026-10-27, login-node analysis)

* **Temperature, not density** (`internal/ledger_tests/closure_profiles.py`). The fit model of each z = 0.2 and 0.5
  cell (arm `v1_frz`, 72 fits) was rebuilt from its chain, with 40 posterior samples per fit. Median ratio of fitted to
  true value:

  | radius | T, background only | T, clean | ne, background only | ne, clean |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0.5 R500, z = 0.2 | 0.89 | 1.00 | 1.01 | 0.98 |
  | 1.0 R500, z = 0.2 | 0.74 | 1.06 | 0.92 | 1.04 |
  | 0.5 R500, z = 0.5 | 0.85 | 1.02 | 1.00 | 0.99 |
  | 1.0 R500, z = 0.5 | 0.76 | 1.08 | 0.94 | 1.03 |

  The density is recovered within a few per cent. With a background, the temperature falls with radius to three
  quarters of the truth at R500; without one it does not. The hydrostatic mass follows the temperature.
* **The fitted background is too centrally concentrated.** Against the background-truth views:
  * the 4.5-12 keV background inside R500 is 9-11 per cent above the truth, while outside R500 it is right;
  * that excess is 20-33 per cent of the cluster's own 4.5-12 keV counts inside R500, enough to bias the temperature
    as seen;
  * the vignetted fraction of the fitted background is above the truth in every camera and band (0.5-1 keV MOS: 0.26-0.39
    against 0.18-0.23; 2-4.5 keV MOS: 0.16-0.18 against 0.05; 4.5-12 keV: 0.03-0.06 against about 0).

  The fit gives part of the flat particle background the vignetting of the sky. That puts too much background where
  the cluster is, and the subtraction removes the cluster's hard counts.
* **The deficit does not track the background-to-cluster surface-brightness ratio at R500** across cells (Spearman
  +0.07 to +0.43, the opposite sign to an S/N effect). Together with the deep-exposure result, this points to the
  split of the background between its two components, not to the background level or to statistics.
* Within the chains, the mass is not correlated with any background parameter (|Spearman| <= 0.09), so the effect is a
  shifted optimum, not a broad posterior.
* Running: job 1890039 (root `closure_113g`):
  * arm `v1_frzbgtrue`: the background frozen at its truth (level and vignetted fraction fitted to the
    background-truth view, `fitfix bg_fixed = 'truth'`);
  * arm `v1_frzsplit`: the vignetted fraction measured on the annulus beyond 2 R500 by a two-component fit and frozen
    there.

  If the oracle arm closes, the background split is the cause.
* **The oracle confirms the background split as the main cause** (job 1890040, arm `v1_frzbgtrue`, 108 fits). In
  these fits the background is frozen at the level and vignetted fraction fitted to each cell's background-truth view.
  Median M_fit / M_true, with the paired change against `v1_frz`:

  | cells | v1_frz | background known | paired d ln M (95 per cent) | clean, for reference |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | background only, z = 0.06 | 0.775 | 0.960 | +0.215 [+0.149, +0.263] | 1.032 |
  | real, z = 0.06 | 0.747 | 1.000 | +0.249 [+0.202, +0.344] | 1.032 |
  | background only, z = 0.2 | 0.913 | 0.903 | +0.038 [-0.079, +0.061] | 0.945 |
  | real, z = 0.2 | 0.863 | 0.960 | +0.107 [+0.031, +0.135] | 0.945 |
  | background only, z = 0.5 | 0.875 | 0.951 | +0.119 [+0.012, +0.185] | 0.999 |
  | real, z = 0.5 | 0.893 | 0.948 | +0.097 [+0.015, +0.134] | 0.999 |

  With the background known, five of the six groups gain 10-25 per cent and come within about 5 per cent of the clean
  cells; background only at z = 0.2 does not move beyond its noise. The split of the background between its
  components accounts for most of the deficit. A residual of up to about 5 per cent against the clean cells remains,
  as does the clean cells' own 0.945 at z = 0.2.
* **Arm `v1_frzsplit`** (job 1890039): the vignetted fraction is measured on the annulus by a two-component fit and
  frozen there. This is worse (0.68-0.94), because the annulus alone does not determine the split well enough.

### Particle background as XMM-ESAS / X-COP (option B, 2026-10-28): references and corner measurements

* **Method chosen by the author:**
  * the quiescent particle background (QPB) of each fitted image is the filter-wheel-closed (FWC) image of the
    camera and band;
  * its level is fixed before the fit by the observation's own unexposed corners, CCD by CCD (MOS) or quadrant by
    quadrant (pn), the pn corner counts with the out-of-time events removed;
  * the sky keeps its vignetted model;
  * residual soft protons are a separate component with the ESAS soft-proton detector map
    (CCF `XMM_SPDETMAP`);
  * the pn out-of-time events keep their own template.

  This is the imaging practice of X-COP (Ghirardini et al. 2019, Sect. 2.2.2: the rescaled background images
  are subtracted with a fixed level) and of XMM-ESAS (mosback, pnback, proton; Snowden et al. 2008; Kuntz & Snowden
  2008). In the X-COP spectral fits the FWC normalisation is left free within 10 per cent (Sect. 2.4.2).
* **The one remaining difference:** X-COP fits single-band (0.7-1.2 keV) surface-brightness profiles and takes the
  temperatures from separate annular spectra; our fit forward-models four bands jointly.
* **Ledger:** entry `fit.particle_background_esas`, 14 evidence blocks, all boxed on the cited pages (ESAS task
  documents of SAS 22.0 from ESA, the Kuntz & Snowden 2008 NASA preprint, Snowden et al. 2008, Ghirardini et al.
  2019, Eckert et al. 2017). The fit setting is `massfit.qpb_model` (hashed only when set; no preset sets it; not
  implemented yet; implemented further down in this entry, commit 9a7a975, and the preset default
  `'esas-fwc-corners'` since 2026-11-03).
* **Corner measurements of all 638 observations** (job 1890054, `donor_tables.qpb_corners`, table
  `tngxmm_dev/V113/qpb_corners/donor_qpb_corners.csv`).
  * Per camera and band, the corner scale relative to the CCF FWC lists, within the PPS flare intervals.
  * In the camera's corner band (MOS 2.5-12 keV, pn 5-12 keV), the scale of each MOS CCD and each pn quadrant.
  * 5 / 3 / 6 observations have no usable MOS1 / MOS2 / pn list.
  * Median scale in 4.5-12 keV: MOS1 1.06, MOS2 1.06, pn 0.89. Median corner counts: 3,700-4,700 per camera, about
    1,000 per pn quadrant and 650-1,300 per outer MOS CCD.
  * The MOS values reproduce the data package's MOS background table exactly.
* **Departures from ESAS** (stated in the module and to be added to the ledger):
  * no augmentation with archival corner spectra (the observation's own counts only);
  * the central MOS CCD, which has no corner, takes the scale of the camera's outer CCDs (ESAS builds a pseudo-CCD 1
    corner spectrum from them);
  * the pn out-of-time correction of the corner counts is column-wise from the FWC column profile, where ESAS
    subtracts the corners of an out-of-time event list.
* **Known risk for the pn:** release 1.1 measured that scaling the pn QPB with its corners puts the replicas' pn
  1-4.5 keV background 0.13-0.16 dex below their donors' (ledger `instrument.donor_corner_qpb`). The real-data check
  of the template (step 4a) will show whether the pn in-field QPB follows its corners.
* **Step 4a, the template against the real data** (job 1890057, `internal/ledger_tests/qpb_template_check.py`, 471
  observations with a usable first exposure). For each observation, camera and band, the QPB template is compared with
  the PPS image in the field beyond max(2 R500, 6') of the cluster, in rings of 6-9', 9-12' and 12-15' from the optical
  axis. The template is the FWC events of the CCF, scaled per CCD / quadrant with the observation's corners and carried
  to the sky with the detector-to-sky map fitted to the exposure's events (residual 0.45").

  The data there are QPB + soft protons + sky, so the template must lie below them. Median template / data:

  | camera | 0.5-1 keV | 1-2 keV | 2-4.5 keV | 4.5-12 keV |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | MOS1 | 0.37-0.41 | 0.67-0.76 | 0.63-0.74 | 0.85-0.91 |
  | MOS2 | 0.29-0.44 | 0.69-0.78 | 0.68-0.74 | 0.90-0.93 |
  | pn | 0.31-0.43 | 0.53-0.67 | 0.64-0.75 | **1.09-1.19** |

  * MOS: the template is below the data, with the hard band closest; template / data rises outwards, as it must when
    the vignetted sky falls off and the QPB does not. In 4.5-12 keV it is above the data for 5 per cent (MOS1) and 9
    per cent (MOS2) of the observations. Before 2004 the MOS ratio is lower (0.73).
  * pn: in 4.5-12 keV the corner-scaled template is above the data for 78 per cent of the observations, median 1.16
    (16-84 per cent 0.90-1.23), in Full Frame (1.13) and Extended Full Frame (1.16) alike and in every epoch after
    2004. That is impossible for a background component. The pn corner level as measured here overestimates the
    in-field QPB by 15-20 per cent.
  * Candidates for the pn: soft-proton contamination of the pn corners (the ESAS cookbook names it, n4.html), or an
    incomplete out-of-time correction of the small pn corners. ESAS's own answer is the archive augmentation of the
    corner spectra, which we do not have.
* The fit's background model (`massfit.qpb_model = 'esas-fwc-corners'`, commit 9a7a975) and the real-data templates
  are implemented and tested. The mocks' side (step 3: corner levels per CCD and quadrant, the soft-proton map,
  template images) is in progress. The pn issue needs a decision before the pn is part of it.

### The pn corner level (option d): the rule, written before the work (2026-10-28)

* **Correction to step 4a.** The pn template of job 1890057 used the CCF's extended-full-frame FWC set
  (`EPN_FWC_EFF_0001`), not the full-frame set `EPN_FWC_0005` that the corner scales are relative to. `fwc_file`
  sorted all files matching `EPN_FWC_*.CCF`, and the EFF file sorts last; it had been copied into the site CCF for
  the wrong-template test shortly before. Fixed: `fwc_file` and `_latest_ccf` now accept only a camera's own numbered
  sets. With the full-frame set, the pn ratio on a 12-observation subset is higher still (4.5-12 keV, 1.28-1.35
  instead of 1.09-1.19), so the set was not the cause of the excess. The pn baseline of step 4a will be remeasured with
  the corrected code.
* **Success rule for the pn corner level.** On the step-4a field rings (beyond max(2 R500, 6') of the cluster; rings
  6-9', 9-12', 12-15' from the optical axis), for the pn in 4.5-12 keV:
  * the median template / data <= 0.95, and
  * the fraction of observations where the template exceeds the data <= 10 per cent (the MOS have 5 and 9 per cent),

  in every pn mode (Full Frame, Extended Full Frame) and every epoch (before 2005, 2005-2009, 2010-2014, 2015-2019,
  2020 on, each with at least 20 observations, else merged with the next).
* **Steps, in order of closeness to ESAS**, each followed by the same measurement:
  1. the pn corner rate as ESAS pnspectra / pnback compute it: corner counts of the observation's own out-of-time
     event list subtracted, per quadrant;
  2. soft-proton screening of the pn corners (corner light curves; ESAS proton / protonscale);
  3. the pn corner geometry (corner masks against the ESAS definition, camera mode, readout-direction streaks).

  If none reaches the rule, the best result and what remains go back to the author with options (a) and (b).

### The pn corner level: the cause of the excess (2026-10-28)

* **Step 1 (ESAS out-of-time list).** The PPS hold no pn out-of-time event list (the XSA PPS pn file list of
  0745120101 has only `PIEVLI` among the event lists). ESAS's procedure needs the ODF and `epchain withoutoftime=yes`
  in the SAS container (`TNGXMM_SITE_SASRUN`). Not run: the check below found the cause first. The column-wise OoT
  model removes a median 0.33 per cent of the pn corner counts, too little to explain 15-35 per cent.
* **Cause: the band of the PPS pn hard image.** The PPS pn 4.5-12 keV images leave out PI 7.8-8.2 keV, the Cu
  K-alpha line of the pn particle background. The selection is in the image header (XPROC0, SAS 21, PPS 21.51):
  `(RAWY>12) && PATTERN<=4 && (PI in (4500:7800] || PI in (8200:12000]) && (FLAG & 0x2fb0024)==0`. The MOS images and
  the other pn bands are contiguous. The template summed the FWC events over all of 4.5-12 keV, so in the pn hard band
  it carried a line that the data do not have.
* **Check** (`internal/ledger_tests/pn_qpb_hardband.py`, 14 observations with field pixels, 2002-2024): the
  observation's own in-FOV events with our selection, the PPS image, and the template, on the step-4a field rings.

  | band | template / events | template / PPS image | PPS image / events |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 10-12 keV (scale `corner_hard10_pn`) | 0.94 | - | - |
  | 4.5-12 keV, contiguous | 0.95 | 1.31 | 0.72 |
  | 4.5-12 keV without 7.8-8.2 keV | 0.92 | 0.92 | 1.00 |

  The template is below the events for all 14 in every band. The pn corner level was right; the comparison was not.
  Neither out-of-time events nor soft protons in the corners are needed to explain the excess.
* **Fix in the template** (`donor_corner_qpb.PPS_BAND_GAPS_EV`, `pps_band_parts`; used by
  `qpb_template.observation_templates`): the FWC events are taken in the PI ranges of the PPS image. The corner scale
  of the band stays the one measured over contiguous 4.5-12 keV (the corner-to-FWC-corner ratio). Step 4a is rerun on
  all 638 with the fix to apply the rule per mode and epoch.
* **Wider effect, not fixed (needs a decision).** The same gap is absent elsewhere:
  * the instrument stage writes the mock pn 4.5-12 keV images from contiguous 4.5-12 keV, cluster and background
    alike, so mock pn hard images carry the Cu line and the cluster's 7.8-8.2 keV counts that real images do not;
  * the fit's response for the pn band 5000 integrates over 4.5-12 keV (`emin_keV`, `emax_keV`), on real and mock
    images alike, so on real images it predicts cluster counts in 7.8-8.2 keV that were removed.

  Fixing either changes mock products or fit records (spec hashes). (Correction, same day: the +0.20 in ln of memo 4
  is the pn 0.5-1 keV field, which the gap does not touch; the hard-band field is measured in the next section.)
* **Result on all 638** (job 1890062, commit dde9a5f, `V113/qpb_template_check_c`; 471 observations with pn field
  pixels beyond max(2 R500, 6')). pn, 4.5-12 keV, template / PPS image:

  | group | n | median | template above data |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | all | 471 | 0.90 (16-84 per cent 0.70-0.95) | 3.8 per cent |
  | Full Frame | 237 | 0.88 | 5.1 per cent |
  | Extended Full Frame | 232 | 0.91 | 2.6 per cent |
  | before 2005 | 44 | 0.80 | 6.8 per cent |
  | 2005-2009 | 37 | 0.91 | 2.7 per cent |
  | 2010-2014 | 163 | 0.88 | 1.8 per cent |
  | 2015-2019 | 121 | 0.93 | 7.4 per cent |
  | 2020 on | 106 | 0.89 | 1.9 per cent |

  The rule holds in every mode and epoch. The pn now sits with the MOS (MOS1 0.88 and 4.5 per cent, MOS2 0.92 and
  8.5 per cent). Rings 6-9', 9-12', 12-15': pn 0.85, 0.88, 0.92. The two Large Window pn observations get a zero
  template (not yet understood; too few for the rule, to be fixed before the pn of a Large Window donor is used).
  Steps 2 and 3 of option (d) are not needed.
* **Site CCF.** `EPN_FWC_EFF_0001.CCF` had been copied into the site CCF for the wrong-template test (2026-10-27
  23:34). The upstream xmm_simulator's CCF scan parses the number after `EPN_FWC_` of every file and raised on it,
  so any instrument stage run on the site CCF between 23:34 and 00:26 would have failed (found by the e2e test; no log shows the
  error). Moved to `V113/ccf_extra` and looked up there through `$TNGXMM_CCF_EXTRA` (commit 1b5d618).

### The pn Cu-line gap everywhere (approved 2026-10-28; switches off in the presets)

> Since 2026-10-30 ("Integration of 2026-10-30: one codebase, no tuned factors"): `pn_hard_band = 'pps'` is
> switched on in every preset.

The author approved applying the PPS selection of the pn 4.5-12 keV band (PI 7.8-8.2 keV left out) to the mocks and the
fit, on condition that a published source supports it in the ledger.

* **Source** (ledger `instrument.pn_hard_band_cu_gap`, proofs boxed in the ledger HTML): Mateos et al. (2009), Sect. 2,
  footnote 4: "In the 4.5-12.0 keV band pn photons with energies between 7.8-8.2 keV were excluded in order to avoid the
  instrumental background produced by Cu K-lines" (the 2XMM pipeline, SAS 7.1.0). The current PPS keep it: the XPROC0
  history of the PPS pn images (PPS 21.51, SAS 21.0) is the entry's instrument source. Katayama et al. (2004, p. 3)
  give the size of the Cu line in the pn background. No PPS product specification or catalogue paper with the PI limits
  was found among the 3XMM/4XMM papers in the references area (Rosen et al. 2016 and Webb et al. 2020 do not state
  them).
* **Settings** (hashed only when set; off in every preset, so the v1 hashes stay):
  * `instrument.pn_hard_band = 'pps'`: the mock pn band-5000 images (and the QPBEXP images and FWC density maps of
    that band) without 7.8-8.2 keV; header key PIGAP. Where the pn QPB level comes from the image decomposition of the
    data-package table, the band-5000 factor is rescaled to the FWC rate without the gap and to the measurement's
    calibration without the gap (`simulate_instrument.pn_gap_qpb_level`); a level from the corners (the ESAS table)
    scales the FWC data band for band and is left as it is.
  * `massfit.pn_hard_band = 'pps'` (fitfix `pn_hard_band`): the cluster count rate of the pn band-5000 image is the
    band's ApecRateCalc rate minus that of 7.8-8.2 keV (`fit_hydrostatic.apply_pn_band_gap`). The gap holds 1.4-3.6
    per cent of the pn hard-band cluster counts (XSPEC, PN_Medium PATTERN<=4 ARF: 2.2 / 3.1 / 3.6 per cent at kT 3 / 6
    / 10 keV, z = 0.06; 1.4 / 2.6 / 3.2 at z = 0.5).
  * With both on in the four production presets the hashes would become: v1 e52bcdb2af8e6299, v1_tng300
    8b925ca8ddd54bd8, v1_tng100 0bdbddad2b1945d2, v1_tng50 da4eb9aa02335600. Not switched: the hard rule keeps the v1
    hashes until the author says so explicitly.
* **Calibration of the level rescaling** (`internal/ledger_tests/pn_gap_calibration.py`, login node, golden fixture
  views 0-2 x 3 seeds at the nominal background, measured with the table's own decomposition): b / nominal = 1.106
  (scatter 0.009) for the contiguous band and 1.067 (scatter 0.045) without the gap, against the nominal rate of the
  same band. `PN5_GAP_CALIBRATION = 1.0669` is the second number.
* **Finding: the stored pn hard-band calibration does not match the current stage.** The data-package table divides
  the pn 4.5-12 keV flat level by 0.947 (`pipeline.yml` background.calibration), the response of the decomposition to
  nominal mocks of an earlier stage. The current stage returns 1.106 for the same measurement (9 replays). So the v1
  mocks' pn hard-band flat level is about 1.106 / 0.947 = 1.17 times the donor's as the decomposition sees it. The
  replays are 3 views of one halo; the table calibration came from a larger replay set. Not changed; it needs a replay
  run of the table calibration with the current stage.
* **The pn hard-band field of the current mocks** (`sky_level_4a.py --band 5000`, the 467 views of memo 4, existing
  contiguous mocks, field beyond 2 R500): median ln(mock / real) of the total field level: MOS1 +0.12 (13 views), MOS2
  +0.10 (212), pn +0.27 (261); vignetted part pn +1.00, flat part pn -0.15. The pn field of the mocks is 31 per cent
  brighter than the real one in 4.5-12 keV, close to what the Cu line adds to a contiguous band (the gap holds 28 per
  cent of the FWC pn rate in 4.5-12 keV). The decomposition puts the line's ring-shaped excess into the "vignetted"
  part. The check of mocks made with the gap needs the 467 views regenerated (a node job; in the closure plan).
* **The two pn Large Window observations** (0810231001, 0161560101): a Large Window exposure reads part of each CCD and
  has no usable unexposed corners (corner counts 0-4 in every band), so the corner scale is 0 and the template zero.
  XMM-ESAS pnspectra refuses these modes ("SUBMODE must be PrimeFullWindowExtended or PrimeFullWindow"). Fixed by
  following ESAS: the ESAS fit leaves out pn images of Large and Small Window exposures (`fit_hydrostatic.esas_specs`;
  the MOS images of those observations stay), and the real-data template refuses them with that message.
* **The rest of the ESAS fit** (commits on release-v1.1.3, all off by default):
  * `bg_fixed = 'zero'` (clean closure cells): the QPB template set to zero, the sky and soft-proton levels at their
    floor and frozen, as the mixture model does;
  * MOS CCDs in the anomalous state left out (fitfix `esas_ccd_mask = 'anomalous'`, default of the ESAS model): the
    CCDs with a 0.5-1 keV excess in the donor table `soft_ccd` (observation: DONORID for a mock, OBS_ID for real data)
    have their pixels' exposure set to zero, through a CCD map of each image (real: the majority CCDNR of the
    exposure's events per pixel; mock: the stage's CCDMAP product). Ghirardini et al. (2019, Sect. 2.4.1) and Snowden
    et al. (2008, Sect. 4) discard such CCDs;
  * the wrong-template switch (fitfix `qpb_fwc = 'oldest'`): mocks read QPBALT, real data build the template from the
    oldest FWC set (MOS FWC_0001; pn the extended-full-frame set, now in the site directory `TNGXMM_CCF_EXTRA`);
  * closure arms: generator `v1_esas` (corner levels per CCD / quadrant from the ESAS table, ESAS soft-proton map, the
    model images with QPBALT and CCDMAP, the pn PPS band); fit arms `v1_esas` (ESAS fit), `v1_esasalt` (oldest FWC),
    `v1_esasmix` (the freeze candidate's mixture background on the same cells), all with the total Galactic column and
    the pn PPS band.

### No tuned calibration factors (the author, 2026-10-28)

* **Rule.** The author: "None of this calibration factor approach! Completely disallowed." No fitted, replay-measured
  or hand-derived factor may be applied to a mock background or emission in new work. A quantity must come from
  calibration data (ESA CCF, FWC event lists) or from the observation's own measurement (e.g. its corners).
* **Removed.** The rescaling of the pn band-5000 decomposition level for gap mocks
  (nominal(full) / nominal(no gap) x 0.947 / 1.067, `pn_gap_qpb_level`, `PN5_TABLE_CALIBRATION`,
  `PN5_GAP_CALIBRATION`) and its replay script `pn_gap_calibration.py` are deleted. The section above that describes
  them is superseded. Job 4 (a replay "recalibration") is cancelled.
* **Instead.** With a donor background, `instrument.pn_hard_band = 'pps'` draws the pn particle background only on the
  donor's own corner level (the ESAS path: `qpb_level = 'corners-band'` with `qpb_stage_<band>_pn` in the background
  table). The FWC spectrum is drawn at the corner scale and binned in the PPS PI ranges, with no further factor
  (`simulate_instrument.check_pn_gap_level` refuses the image-decomposition level). This needs no hidden ratio: the
  gap holds 27.60 per cent of the stage's FWC spectrum and 27.52 per cent of the CCF EPN_FWC_0005 in-FOV events
  (PATTERN <= 4, the PPS flag mask), so the two FWC data sets agree to 0.1 per cent. With the nominal background, the
  FWC spectrum in the PPS ranges is used as it is.
* **Presets.** The switch of the four v1 presets to `pn_hard_band = 'pps'` is NOT made. The data package's background
  table has no pn corner level, so the production stage would refuse the gap. Switching needs the pn corner levels in
  the data package (an author decision). The prepared preset switch is kept as a patch,
  `tngxmm_dev/V113/preset_pps_switch.patch`. It gives the hashes v1 e52bcdb2af8e6299, v1_tng300 8b925ca8ddd54bd8,
  v1_tng100 0bdbddad2b1945d2, v1_tng50 da4eb9aa02335600. The release-v1.2 multisim branch moves the presets too
  ('full' abundance default: v1 198b80e9... -> 2e0dca90... there), so whoever merges recomputes the final hashes
  and re-registers `realism.frozen` once.
* **Hygiene.** `tests/unit/test_pn_hard_band_gap.py::test_no_calibration_factor_in_the_stage_or_the_fit` fails if
  a module-level `*CALIBRATION*` constant, a `pn_gap_qpb_level`-like rescaling or the numbers 0.947 / 1.067 reappear
  in the instrument stage, the corner-QPB module or the fit's ESAS code.
* **Factors already in production** (audit, 2026-10-28; listed for the author, none used in new work):
  * `conf/base/parameters/pipeline.yml:175-178` `donor_tables.background.calibration`: per camera and band, the median
    over 195 nominal-background replays of (decomposed flat level / nominal rate). `donor_tables/background.py:63`
    computes it and `instrument/donor_background.py:396-431` (`shrunk_band_scales`) divides by it. It sets
    `qpb_<band>_<cam>` of `donor_background.csv`. In v1 it sets the pn particle level in every band (the MOS take
    their corner levels). Physical replacement: the pn corner level (`qpb_stage_<band>_pn` from the donor's own
    corners and the CCF FWC, the ESAS table of this release).
  * `instrument/donor_background.py:391,393` `BAND_PRIOR_DEX = 0.3`, `BAND_FLOOR_DEX = 0.03`: hand-chosen shrinkage
    of the band factors towards the 4.5-12 keV level. `:248` `QPB_SCALE_RANGE = (0.2, 10)`: a clip. `:~430` band 1
    takes band 2's factor. In v1: the pn. Replacement: the corner scale per band (no shrinkage needed when the
    corners measure each band).
  * `instrument/calibration_axis.py:62` `MOS_RGA_DIPOLE_PPS_MEASURED`: the azimuthal MOS vignetting modulation fitted
    on 60 donors' PPS exposure maps (not a CCF value). In v1 it multiplies cluster, sky and AGN photons and the
    exposure maps. Replacement: none in the CCF; what SAS eexpmap applies (the measurement reproduces it), to be
    taken from the SAS/CCF vignetting model directly.
  * `config.py:498` / `:698` `pn_oot_cti = 1.4e-3` per row: the pn out-of-time energy mis-correction measured on the
    real streak hardness. In v1, stage and fit. Replacement: the CCF CTI calibration of the out-of-time events, if it
    exists.
  * `instrument/donor_background.py:523-526` `FWC_REGISTRATION`, `FWC_SMOOTH_ARCMIN`: the FWC density registered on the
    packaged masks by cross-correlation (spatial only). Replacement: the CCF DET-to-mask geometry (as the ESAS path does
    with the observation's own DET->sky map).
  * `donor_tables/fwc.py:83-86` `band_conv`: the ratio of the stage's FWC spectrum to the CCF FWC event rate per band.
    Both are calibration data, so this is a unit conversion, not a tuned number. It enters the MOS corner levels of v1.
  * Hand-chosen selection thresholds (not normalisations): `donor_soft_ccd.py:50,52` (anomalous-CCD classification),
    `donor_footprint.py:60-61`, `conditions_donor.py:88,94-95,98` (matching costs, pointing scatter from 45 repeat
    observations, band-mean ARF weights).
  * From literature or CCF, not tuned: the sky model (`simulate_instrument.skybkg_spectrum`; LHB, halo and CXB norms
    imported from xmm_simulator, provenance to be documented), Moretti et al. 2003 AGN counts, Willingale et al. 2013
    column, Handbook out-of-time fractions, CCF pattern fractions, effective area, vignetting and PSF.
## Multi-simulation pipeline (2026-10-27, branch `multisim`)

The approved plan: one Kedro graph for TNG-Cluster, TNG300, FLAMINGO, SIMBA and EAGLE, in which only an `ingest`
stage per simulation family (`tng`, `swift`) differs; it writes canonical datasets (simulation descriptor, halo
table, materialised gas cubes of half-side 5.66 R500c in physical units) and every later stage reads only those.
FLAMINGO and TNG-Cluster are the production pair; SIMBA and EAGLE are validation runs. Two abundance modes for the
emission, both hashed spec fields: `full` (every element the simulation tracks) and `common` (total Z with AG89
ratios for every simulation). This section holds the measurements of phase P0 that the author's decisions rest on.

### P0: the two abundance modes on 20 halos per simulation (login node)

Scripts: `internal/ledger_tests/multisim/p0_read_tngfmt.py`, `p0_read_flamingo.py`, `p0_read_eagle_orig.py`,
`p0_metals_measure.py`, `p0_summary.py`; output `tngxmm_dev/MULTISIM/p0/` (`metals_<sim>.csv`, `summary.md`). Gas:
the FoF members (TNG format) or every particle (FLAMINGO, through the SWIFT cell index) inside 1.1 max(R500c, 1 Mpc)
of the potential minimum; T > 3e5 K, not star forming, and net cooling for TNG only. Rest-frame 0.5-2 keV, 3-D
spheres R500c and 1 Mpc. "Legacy" is the emission of release 1.1 (reference table at Z / 0.019366); `full` uses He
and C N O Ne Mg Si Fe of each cell and puts the elements the simulation does not track at solar ratios scaled by the
remaining metal mass ("other-solar"); `full_fe` ties those to iron instead (the legacy photon rule). The fitted-like
temperature and metallicity come from a single-temperature APEC (lodd, one abundance for C-Ni, as XSPEC applies
it) fitted to the summed ideal spectrum, 0.5-7 keV, Poisson weights at 1e5 counts.

Medians over the halos, 16-84 per cent in brackets (sphere R500c; the 1 Mpc sphere agrees within 0.005 dex except
SIMBA, full/common -0.018):

| simulation (snapshot, z) | M500c [Msun] | dlog L common/legacy | full/common | full_fe/full | T_fit full/common - 1 | Z_fit full - common |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TNG-Cluster (84, 0.20) | 1.0e14-1.6e15 | -0.023 [-0.024, -0.023] | +0.001 [-0.003, +0.002] | +0.000 | +0.003 [-0.002, +0.010] | -0.043 [-0.055, -0.031] |
| TNG300 (84, 0.20) | 1.0e14-8.5e14 | -0.024 [-0.024, -0.023] | -0.005 [-0.012, -0.000] | +0.001 | +0.012 [+0.000, +0.049] | -0.035 [-0.048, -0.023] |
| FLAMINGO (73, 0.20) | 1.0e14-1.5e15 | -0.016 [-0.018, -0.008] | +0.011 [+0.006, +0.016] | -0.017 | +0.054 [+0.037, +0.069] | +0.003 [-0.087, +0.028] |
| SIMBA (140, 0.19) | 6.9e13-7.4e14 | -0.007 [-0.013, +0.001] | -0.013 [-0.025, +0.001] | +0.002 | +0.105 [+0.020, +0.135] | -0.025 [-0.081, -0.010] |
| EAGLE (26, 0.18) | 3.0e13-2.6e14 | -0.019 [-0.021, -0.016] | -0.003 [-0.013, +0.007] | -0.024 | +0.067 [+0.052, +0.093] | -0.001 [-0.034, +0.018] |
| EAGLE Recal-L025N0752 (26, 0.18), groups at kT 0.3 keV | 1.5e12-1.0e13 | -0.005 | -0.031 [-0.046, +0.019] | -0.030 | +0.001 | -0.129 |

* **Legacy against common is helium.** The reference table of the legacy emission carries APEC's AG89 helium (He/H =
  0.0977 by number); `common` keeps helium at the lodd value of the fit's abundance table (0.0792). With helium set to
  AG89 the common luminosity equals the legacy one to +-0.003 dex (4 TNG300 and 2 SIMBA halos). The gas's own He/H
  (TNG: 0.083, X_H = 0.755) lies between the two; `full` uses it.
* **Luminosity.** Between the two modes L_X differs by at most 0.013 dex in median for clusters, 0.03 dex for the
  0.3 keV EAGLE groups.
* **Fitted temperature.** Where a model's element ratios depart from solar, a one-temperature, one-abundance fit of
  the `full` spectrum is biased: +0.3 per cent for TNG-Cluster (3.5 keV), +1 per cent TNG300 (2.5 keV), +5 per cent
  FLAMINGO (2.8 keV), +7 per cent EAGLE (1.2 keV), +10 per cent SIMBA (1.5 keV). Under `common` it is absent by
  construction. A temperature bias of this size moves a hydrostatic mass by about the same fraction.
* **Untracked elements.** Tying them to iron instead of the remaining metal mass changes L_X by -0.017 (FLAMINGO) and
  -0.024 dex (EAGLE), where the untracked mass (Z minus the tracked metals) is large compared with iron.
* **Smoothed against particle metallicity.** FLAMINGO stores the element fractions smoothed only and the total
  metallicity both ways: in the common mode the particle Z changes L_X by -0.001 dex [-0.004, +0.004], T_fit by -0.1
  per cent and Z_fit by -0.01 (R500c) and -0.02 (0.15-1 R500c). The Eagle100 conversion carries the smoothed values
  only; the original-format EAGLE run on Vera (Recal-L025N0752, groups) has both: L_X +0.002 [-0.059, +0.040] dex,
  T_fit +4 per cent (R500c) and +2 per cent (0.15-1 R500c), Z_fit +0.001. FLAMINGO's own cooling and X-ray tables
  use the smoothed abundances.
* **Side findings.** (1) The legacy photon spectrum scales each metal by its mass fraction over the solar one,
  not per hydrogen atom; for TNG gas the two differ by X_H,sun / X_H = 0.7507 / 0.755 (-0.6 per cent in the line
  strengths; the band luminosity of the legacy mode is unaffected, it comes from the table). (2) The
  SOAP catalogue finding of `docs/dev/LX_CROSS_SIM.md` (branch lx-gas-explore) is reproduced: at z = 0.2 our legacy L_X is -0.20 dex below the SOAP
  rest-frame value (median of 20 halos), i.e. +0.035 dex above SOAP / (1+z)^3, against +0.038 in that study. (3) The FLAMINGO `full` result with the particle total Z
  is not usable (it mixes the particle total with smoothed elements) and is not reported.

**Temperature and ionisation inputs** (descriptors `src/tngxmm/ingest/descriptors/*.yml`): TNG and SIMBA the stored
internal energy and electron abundance (T from both with the cell's X_H); EAGLE the converted internal energy and
electron abundance (EAGLE stores T and no electron abundance; for the hot gas the converted x_e is 1.17, the value of
full ionisation of H and He); FLAMINGO the stored Temperatures and ElectronNumberDensities (the Ploeckinger and
Schaye 2020 tables), u derived from them for the truth.

**Resolution** (emitting particles inside R500c): TNG-Cluster 9.8e5 at 1.0e14 Msun, TNG300 1.2e6, SIMBA 3.0e5 at
6.9e13, EAGLE 1.8e6 at 3.0e13, FLAMINGO 967 at 1.0e14 and 2.2e4 at 1.5e15. FLAMINGO is three orders of magnitude
coarser; its resolution and image-realism test (phase P3) decides whether its images can be used.

### P0: storage of the materialised cubes

Bytes per gas particle of a canonical cube (`tngxmm.ingest.cube_io`, every schema field the simulation provides,
gzip 4 with shuffle; `p0_storage.py` on the same halos): TNG-Cluster 92.2, TNG300 93.2, SIMBA 93.1, EAGLE 92.7,
FLAMINGO 90.1; dark matter (positions only, the FoF dark matter of one TNG-Cluster halo) 17.9, stars and black
holes (with masses) taken as 25. Positions, masses and densities are double precision, as the photon draws require (below).

| sample | gas particles | other particles (truth) | size |
|---|---|---|---|
| TNG presets, the 3,258 halo-snapshots of the 5.66 R500c cubes (job 1889862) | 5.22e10 (TNG-Cluster 4.12e10) | 4.9e10 in the truth spheres (r_cut = 3 R200c) | gas 4.8 TB, other 0.9 TB (4.4e10 DM, 4.7e9 stars): 5.7 TB |
| FLAMINGO, every field halo above 1e14 Msun at the 30 snapshots 47-76 nearest the 638 pool redshifts (z = 0.05-1.5) | 50,636 halo-snapshots, mean 1.33e4 gas, 1.50e4 DM, 1.5e3 stars | (in the cube) | 1.5 MB per halo-snapshot: 76 GB |
| FLAMINGO, the nine snapshots nearest the TNG full snapshots (77, 75, 73, 71, 69, 67, 63, 57, 47) | 24,058 halo-snapshots | | 36 GB |
| SIMBA, EAGLE (halos above 1e14 Msun at the snapshots of the pool redshifts) | a few hundred halo-snapshots, 1e6-6e7 gas each | | under 0.3 TB (estimate from the FoF counts) |

(Superseded by the design change below: only FLAMINGO is materialised.)  Space: `/vera/ptmp/gc` (the group's fileset) holds 800 TB, 672 TB used, 129 TB free (84 per cent, `df`, 2026-10-27);
no per-user quota tool answers on the login node (`mmlsquota`: "GPFS is down on this node"). **Verdict: the space
is available**; the plan as written needs about 5.9 TB, 4.6 per cent of the group's free space. Options that reduce it, for the author:
(a) as planned; (b) store the non-gas particles only inside the truth radius 1.02 max(3.15 R500c, 2 R200c) instead of
3 R200c (about half of them); (c) store non-gas positions in single precision relative to the centre (the truth is
deterministic and tolerates the rounding; the photon path does not, so gas positions stay double).

### P0: recommendation on the abundance mode (for the author)

* **`common` as the production default, `full` as a hashed sensitivity arm.** The aim of the multi-simulation
  campaign is the hydrostatic bias across models. With `full`, the models' own element ratios bias the fitted
  temperature by up to 10 per cent (SIMBA, EAGLE, FLAMINGO 5 per cent), so part of a difference in mass bias between
  models would be a difference in nucleosynthesis yields seen through a one-abundance fit, not in the gas
  dynamics; `common` removes that term for every model and snapshot, also opens the 80 TNG mini snapshots, and
  changes L_X against `full` by at most 0.013 dex for clusters. Effect: every product of a `common` preset differs
  from the legacy TNG products (-0.023 dex in L_X, the helium of the reference table).
* **`full` as the default, `common` as the arm.** Each model's own prediction for the spectra, closer to what the
  model claims; the fitted-temperature bias then belongs to the model. Effect: model comparisons of the mass bias
  carry the yield term above.
* Either way the legacy emission stays the value of the TNG presets until a release changes it (spec field unset,
  hashes unchanged).

### Design change (author, 2026-10-27, after P0): index files, canonical cells in memory

The canonical gas cubes are NOT materialised for the TNG family (no 5.9 TB).  What the `ingest` stage writes per
simulation: the descriptor, the halo table (`tngxmm ingest halos`, `ingest.tng.halo_table`; FLAMINGO's beside its
cubes) and the index files of each halo's cube (the existing format `tngxmm-mock-cutouts-1` of the mock-cutout stage;
the cubes of half-side 5.66 R500c of the 3,258 TNG halo-snapshots, job 1889862, take 8.2 GB).  One reader per family
loads a halo's cells by index straight from the snapshots and converts them in memory to the canonical gas
(`ingest.tng.canonical_gas` with the descriptor's rules: units, star-forming flag, net-heating rule, temperature,
element fractions); every later stage receives only that object (`sim.emission.GasCells`).  This is the route
`runtime.cube_store = 'index'` of P1, the default; the TNG presets and `v1_simba`, `v1_eagle` use it.

FLAMINGO is the exception, by decision of this branch: its cubes are materialised in the canonical format
(`tngxmm ingest swift`, about 1.5 MB per halo-snapshot, 76 GB for the 50,636 halo-snapshots of the pool redshifts,
36 GB for the nine v1-like snapshots).  Reason: the SWIFT snapshots and SOAP catalogues are lossy-compressed and
only HDF5 1.10 reads them (the embed stack on Vera), while the generation runs in the sim stack, whose HDF5 refuses
them; reading by index during the generation would need a second interpreter per halo.  `v1_flamingo` therefore
sets `runtime.cube_store = 'canonical'`.  The materialised format also serves the fixtures and the regression test
of the TNG route (P1).  The storage verdict of P0 is superseded: about 8 GB of index files for TNG (existing) and
36-76 GB for FLAMINGO.

The abundance default and the helium rule of the common mode stay with the author; both modes remain hashed
switches, unset in every production preset.

### P1: the canonical layer for TNG, and the regression

* **What was built.** Stage `ingest` (first in `tngxmm.STAGES`): `ingest/schema.py` and `ingest/cube_io.py` (the
  canonical cube, INTERFACES section 4.9), `ingest/descriptor.py` with the seven descriptors, `ingest/tng.py` (TNG
  fields to canonical cells, one halo's cube, a single-pass ingest of a few box halos), `ingest/swift.py`
  (FLAMINGO), `ingest/canonical_source.py` (the HaloSource protocol over a cube), `ingest/truth.py` (the spherical
  truth from a cube), the commands `campaign ingest` / `tngxmm ingest tng|swift` and the Kedro node
  `ingest.build_canonical_cubes`.  The gas loader is split into the TNG conversion (`ingest.tng.canonical_gas`)
  and a simulation-independent emission (`sim/emission.py`); the photon sampler takes any set of spectral
  components (`generate_photons._spectral_components`: the legacy nine, or those of an abundance mode).
* **Two ways to read the same TNG gas.** `runtime.cube_store = 'index'` (default and the production route after the
  design change above: the cube index files of the mock-cutout stage, read from the snapshot and converted to
  canonical cells in memory) or `'canonical'` (`campaign run --cube-root`: materialised cubes; FLAMINGO, fixtures,
  tests).  Runtime fields, not hashed, because the products are the same bytes (below).
* **Spec.** New fields hashed only when set: `sim.source` (with `sim.descriptor_sha256` in the canonical JSON),
  `sim.emission_abundances`, `sim.emission_untracked`.  The hashes of `v1`, `v1_tng300`, `v1_tng100`, `v1_tng50`
  are unchanged (`198b80e9d4563eec`, `ea44c41bd7505585`, `8c67f0845616ba25`, `1af83cda12b0cc4b`).  New presets:
  `v1_flamingo`, `v1_simba`, `v1_eagle` (INTERFACES section 5).
* **Regression on the fixture halo** (the recipe of `tools/make_v1_golden.sh` without the fits, login node;
  baseline = the same recipe on the base commit 10b499a, because release 1.1.3 already differs from the stored
  `golden_v1.1.1` in `conditions.json` and `obs_meta.json`, e.g. `dims_skipped`):
  * refactored code, index route, against the baseline: every product identical (events, per-camera images and
    exposure maps, merged images, source lists, tngref maps, truth profile, obs_meta.json of views 1 and 2) except
    `lx_intr_r500` of view 0 in `obs_meta.json` and the results CSV: 1.106599334368138e+44 against
    1.1065993343681378e+44, one unit in the last place.  Cause: the canonical cell mass is stored in Msun
    (m 1e10 / h) and converted to grams once (x 1.989e33), where the legacy loader computed m UnitMass / h; the
    emission measure of 27 per cent of the cells moves by at most 4 units in the last place (relative 8e-16), and
    the sum over the R500c cylinder by one.  The Poisson draws of the photons did not change (the probability that
    a relative change of 1e-15 moves a Poisson draw is of order the number of photons times 1e-15).
  * canonical route against the index route of the same code: identical, file by file
    (`tests/e2e/test_canonical_regression.py`), except the backup of the results table taken before the spherical
    truth (`*.pre_sphere_truth.bak`).  There the generation-time FoF truth reads the mock's gas through the
    HaloSource view of the cube, whose comoving positions and single-precision densities are recomputed from the
    physical values, and six `*_mock` diagnostic columns and two temperatures differ by 1e-8 to 1e-10 (relative).
    The spherical truth replaces them; the final results table is identical.
  * spherical truth from a canonical cube of half-side 5.66 R500c (gas cut from the 7.2 R500c fixture) against the
    snapshot route's `truth_profile.h5`: largest relative difference over the 108 datasets 3.3e-7, headline
    `barnes21_nosf_fit_mu` 2e-8 (the round trip of single-precision densities through physical units).
  * campaign check C1-C17: PASS on both routes.
* **Unit tests** (`pytest -m "not cluster and not e2e"`, sim stack of the login node): 1,494 run, 3 fail, the three
  `test_instrument_active_ccds.py` tests that fail on the base commit too (environment of this login node); 39
  skipped (optional stacks absent from the sim environment).  New: `tests/unit/test_ingest_canonical.py` (cube
  format, TNG ingest bit for bit, the HaloSource view, the abundance modes, the descriptors and spec fields, the
  canonical route with the TNG readers blocked, the SWIFT conversion).  e2e:
  `tests/e2e/test_canonical_regression.py`, 2 passed (13 min, login node).
* **Not done: moving the TNG readers.**  The plan moves `read_halo_source`, `truth_sphere`, `select_halos`,
  `build_box_halos`, `sphere_cutouts`, `sph_map`, `project_tngref_map`, `build_zoom_target_ids` and the native
  part of `load_halo_gas` into `ingest.tng`.  They stay in `sim/` for now: release 1.1.3 is being changed on
  another branch, and a rename of these files would turn each of its edits into a merge conflict.  What holds
  instead: a preset that names a descriptor reads only canonical data (halo selection from the cube manifest, gas,
  catalogue rows and truth from the cube; `tests/unit/test_ingest_canonical.py::test_canonical_path_reads_no_snapshot`
  runs the canonical source with illustris_python and the snapshot readers blocked).  The TNG presets keep the
  native reads where they are TNG-only checks (temet reference, generation-time FoF truth before the spherical
  stage).  The move is a mechanical follow-up once the branches meet.

### P2: SIMBA and EAGLE

* **Route.** Both are read through the TNG-format conversions on Vera with the TNG reader and their descriptors:
  index files of the mock-cutout stage (one streaming pass over the box), the cells converted in memory with the
  descriptor's rules (no net-heating cut: the converted `GFM_CoolingRate` is not a model output; star forming SFR >
  0; no wind flag available for SIMBA), the spherical truth from the snapshot (`campaign truth-sphere`).  Presets
  `v1_simba` and `v1_eagle`; snapshots nearest the v1 redshifts (SIMBA 151, 145, 140, 134, 129, 124, 116, 105, 90;
  EAGLE 28-17), every halo of the target list, no temet reference, each simulation's own cosmology in the
  generation.  Both are internal validation runs (the conversion's author has not been contacted).
* **Fixtures** (`conf/fixtures/targets/targets_{simba,eagle}_fixture.csv`, donors of the TNG fixture): SIMBA halos 7
  (M500c 1.14e14) and 2 (2.28e14) at snapshot 140, three views each; EAGLE halo 4 (1.29e14) at snapshot 26, three
  views.  Campaign check C1-C17 PASS for both.
* **Index route against materialised cubes** (SIMBA, the same halos also ingested into canonical cubes with
  `ingest.tng.ingest_box_halos`): `lx_intr_r500`, `lx_intr_1mpc`, `lx_r500`, `lx_1mpc` identical, the hydrostatic
  truth equal to 9e-8 (sphere from the snapshot against the sphere from the cube).
* **Intrinsic L_X against the cross-simulation study** (rest 0.5-2 keV, 3-D sphere R500c, legacy emission, the gas of
  the canonical cube against the FoF gas of `xsim_tngfmt.py` on branch lx-gas-explore): SIMBA +7e-5 dex (both halos),
  EAGLE +5e-5 and +9e-5 dex (`internal/ledger_tests/multisim/p2_check_lx.py`).
* **Size.** A 5.66 R500c cube of a 1e14 Msun cluster holds 2.7e6 gas cells in SIMBA and 4.4e7 in EAGLE (EAGLE's gas
  particle mass is 1.8e6 Msun); the EAGLE generation of one halo takes about 7 minutes on one core of the login node.
* **Open (closed by P2b for the winds).** SIMBA's decoupled winds are not excluded (no flag in the conversion; `OPEN_ITEMS.md`); the truth
  estimators that read `GFM_CoolingRate` as a diagnostic (`_literature_estimators`, cooling cut variants) read the
  converted field for SIMBA and EAGLE. This held in the P2 route only. P2b replaced it: the presets compute the truth from the original-snapshot cubes, which carry no cooling rate, so these estimators count no cell as heated, and the conversions supply only the halo catalogue (ledger `multisim.net_heating`, `multisim.truth_on_cubes`).

### P2b: SIMBA and EAGLE from their original snapshots (2026-11-01)

Author decision (2026-10-30): read SIMBA and EAGLE from the original snapshots, as cutouts streamed like
FLAMINGO L1_m8, instead of the TNG-format conversions.  Presets `v1_simba` and `v1_eagle` now name the
descriptors `simba` (family `gizmo`) and `eagle` (family `eagle`) and read materialised canonical cubes
(`runtime.cube_store = 'canonical'`).  The conversions keep the descriptors `simba_tngformat` and
`eagle_tngformat` and supply the halo catalogue only.

* **SIMBA** (`tngxmm.ingest.simba_remote`).  The SIMBA team serves each m100n1024 snapshot as one 263 GB HDF5 file
  over HTTP (`simba.roe.ac.uk`, range requests answered).  Every dataset is contiguous and uncompressed, so a row
  range is one byte range.  The rows are not sorted in space as a whole, but 8192 consecutive gas rows span about
  3 Mpc/h, so a cube is made of long runs.  The reader does one pass over the coordinates of each snapshot (26 GB,
  21 min at 14-20 MB/s), keeps the bounding box of every 4096-row block (`index_<snap>.npz`, a few MB), then reads
  only the overlapping blocks and the selected rows.  Two fixture halos at snapshot 140: 27.7 GB and 14 min with
  the index, 0.64 GB for the selection.
* **EAGLE** (`tngxmm.ingest.eagle_remote`).  The particle data of Ref-L0100N1504 are on the hdfstream service that
  serves FLAMINGO (`cosma:/EAGLE/Fiducial_models/RefL0100N1504`), sorted by Peano-Hilbert key on a 64^3 grid
  with a hash table per file.  The cell of every key is measured from the data (first, middle and last particle of
  the key must agree), so no Peano-Hilbert code is copied (`pyread_eagle` is GPL, this repository MIT).  Halo 4 at
  snapshot 26: hash index and key cells 7.5 min, the cube (4.2e7 gas, 4.5e7 DM) 7 min.
* **Against the conversions** (`internal/ledger_tests/multisim/orig_vs_tngformat.py`, the same halos ingested from
  both; outputs in `tngxmm_dev/MULTISIM/orig/`).  SIMBA halos 7 and 2: every matched gas particle bit-identical in
  every field; the original cubes hold 1929 and 6638 more gas particles and the conversion as many more star
  particles: exactly the particles with DelayTime > 0.  The conversion wrote SIMBA's decoupled winds as PartType4,
  the way TNG writes its wind phase, so they never emitted there either; L_X(0.5-2 keV, R500c) is identical.
  EAGLE halo 4: the same particles in every kind, positions, masses, u, SFR and every element fraction equal to
  rounding; the stored temperature equals the conversion's to 7e-7 (median).  EAGLE stores no electron abundance:
  x_e is now that of full ionisation of the particle's smoothed composition (the conversion's hot-gas value was H and
  He alone).  Inside R500c the metals carry 0.14 per cent of the electrons (median, 0.9 per cent at the 99th
  percentile) and L_X changes by +0.0027 dex.
* **What changed for the products.**  SIMBA's S and Ca are now their own components in the `full` mode (the
  conversion had put them in 'other'); SIMBA's wind flag is set (it was not needed: see above); EAGLE keeps its
  particle metallicity beside the smoothed one.  The open item `multisim.simba_winds` is closed.
* **Not done.**  The production halo lists of both simulations and their snapshots; the e2e test of the presets on
  the new cubes (`tests/e2e/test_multisim_presets.py`, needs `TNGXMM_MULTISIM_CUBES`).

### P4: the TNG mini snapshots under the common mode

* **What a mini snapshot loses under `common`** (`internal/ledger_tests/multisim/p4_mini_common.py`, the 40 TNG-Cluster
  and TNG300 halos of P0 at snapshot 84, full fields dropped as a mini snapshot lacks them): L_X(0.5-2 keV) +0.0085 dex
  [16-84: +0.0072, +0.0103] in the R500c and the 1 Mpc spheres, all of it from X_H = 0.76 replacing the cell's own
  (0.755 in the median); the missing net-heating cut changes nothing (0.0000).  So within `v1_common` the views of
  mini snapshots are 2 per cent brighter than those of full snapshots for the same gas.  Options for the author:
  (a) keep it and report it; (b) use X_H = 0.76 at every snapshot of a common-mode TNG preset (consistent across
  snapshots, 2 per cent off the gas's own hydrogen at full snapshots).
* **Preset `v1_common`**: TNG-Cluster at every snapshot 40-99 (60, of which 51 mini), `sim.emission_abundances =
  'common'`, stream key `v4_common`; otherwise v1.
* **Target list** (`campaigns/v4_common/`, the same replica rules and seed as `campaigns/v4`, halo table of 16,221
  primary-target halo-snapshots): 3,159 views on 2,067 halo-snapshots, 2,684 of them at mini snapshots.  Against the
  nine full snapshots: |z_obs - z_snap| median 0.006 against 0.033, p90 0.023 against 0.088, max 0.111 against 0.210;
  epoch fallbacks 59 against 432; coverage 0.994 against 0.929; **gate P passes** (matching mass KS D 0.056 against
  the critical 0.061; 0.241 with nine snapshots), with every other variable passing as before.  Caveat: the pass
  rests partly on reuse.  The cap of three views per halo-snapshot now applies to snapshots 0.02-0.05 apart in z, so
  the same massive zoom target appears at many neighbouring epochs: views per zoom target median 6, p90 27, maximum
  89 (nine snapshots: 9, 18, 24).  Neighbouring snapshots of one halo are not independent clusters, so the effective
  sample of the massive end is smaller than the view count says; folds and bootstraps grouped by zoom target (release
  1.2) account for it.
* Not done: the cube index files of the 51 mini snapshots (a `campaign mock-cutout` node job) and any generation.

### P3: FLAMINGO

* **Ingest** (`ingest/swift.py`, `tngxmm ingest swift`, embed stack): SOAP field halos (`VR/HostHaloID == -1`) with
  SO/500_crit masses and radii, centre `VR/CentreOfPotential`; every gas, dark-matter, star and black-hole particle
  of the cube of half-side 5.66 R500c through the SWIFT top-level cell index; canonical gas with the stored temperature and
  electron density (x_e = n_e / n_H with the smoothed X_H), u derived from T, the smoothed element fractions, the
  smoothed total metallicity with the particle value beside it, SFR > 0 as star forming, the smoothing length.  One
  cluster takes about 6 s and 1.2 MB (100 clusters of 1.0e14-1.5e15 Msun: 118 MB, 10 minutes).  Preset `v1_flamingo` reads the materialised cubes
  (design change above); its truth is the spherical truth of the cube, computed in the generation.
* **Fixtures** (`conf/fixtures/targets/targets_flamingo_fixture.csv`): SOAP halos 862577 (M500c 1.64e14) and 1601118
  (2.54e14) at snapshot 73, three views each: campaign check C1-C17 PASS.  `tests/e2e/test_multisim_presets.py`
  (FLAMINGO and SIMBA; EAGLE on request): 2 passed, 1 skipped, 18 minutes.
* **Against SOAP** (`p2_check_lx.py` on 10 halos per snapshot, spaced in mass): our L_X(0.5-2 keV, sphere R500c,
  legacy emission) minus SOAP's rest-frame value divided by (1+z)^3, median: +0.041 dex at z = 0, +0.037 at z = 0.2,
  +0.045 at z = 0.5.  The (1+z)^3 factor of LX_CROSS_SIM holds at z = 0.5 too (there it was suspected, not
  verified); the remaining +0.04 dex is our APEC table against FLAMINGO's Cloudy tables, as in that study.
* **Resolution** (`p3_resolution.py`, 100 field clusters at z = 0.2 spaced in mass rank, 1.0e14-1.5e15 Msun;
  z-axis view, depth +-4 R500c; nominal 30 ks, 1500 cm^2): the effective number of emitting particles,
  n_eff = (sum L)^2 / sum L^2, is 27, 387 and 616 in the projected annuli 0-0.15, 0.15-0.5 and 0.5-1 R500c (medians),
  and the expected counts per effective emitter 59, 10 and 3.4 (16-84 per cent: 27-211, 7.8-24, 2.4-7.8), nearly
  independent of mass.  For comparison SIMBA gives 0.9, 0.12, 0.02 (two halos), EAGLE 0.04-0.08, 0.006, 0.001 and
  the TNG-Cluster fixture halo 0.02, 0.005, 0.03 (one halo, at z = 0.5).  Correction (independent check of this
  item, 2026-10-28): counts per emitter is not a resolution metric.  The photons of a particle are spread over its
  kernel (emission-weighted support radius 147, 189, 264 kpc in the three annuli; 58 weighted neighbours, as
  SWIFT's Wendland C2 with eta = 1.2348), which covers 30-60 PSF elements, and neighbouring kernels overlap; per PSF
  element the FLAMINGO number is below 1 in the core.  A noise-free kernel map has a sampling noise of 11-16 per cent
  per pixel (split halves), below or near the Poisson noise of a 30 ks view outside 0.15 R500c.  What L1000N0900
  does limit is small-scale structure: the projected kernel FWHM in the core (about 95 kpc) exceeds the PSF HEW
  below z_obs of about 0.5, so structure below about 100 kpc (cool-core shape, cavities, small substructure) is not
  represented.  The quoted counts also assume z = 0.2, while these views are observed at the TNG donors' z_obs
  (median 0.096).
* **Image realism, a first look** (`p3_paired_targets.py`, `p3_realism.py`): the 100 clusters, one view each, observed
  under the donor package of the TNG-Cluster view of the nearest M500c at snapshot 84 (production tree
  `campaign_v1_full`), and the 50 real donors of those packages; the 17 statistics of the realism evaluation.  Paired
  medians FLAMINGO - TNG-Cluster: net counts in R500 -0.35 dex [-0.45, -0.30] (FLAMINGO is fainter at fixed mass, as
  in LX_CROSS_SIM), centroid shift +0.007 [+0.001, +0.016], log P2 +0.49 [+0.19, +0.66], log P3 +0.46 [+0.19, +0.71],
  compact sources -4.1.  Against the real donors the shape statistics of FLAMINGO are further off than those of
  TNG-Cluster (KS distance centroid shift 0.27 against 0.13, P3 0.27 against 0.12, P2 0.19 against 0.14).  Placing
  the photons with the Wendland kernel (`sim.photon_spread = 'sph-kernel'`) instead of the volume-equivalent sphere
  changes none of this (paired medians: centroid shift -0.0006, log P2 -0.012, log P3 -0.021, concentration -0.010);
  the test is uninformative, because the two placements have nearly the same median projected radius (0.31 H against
  about 0.26 H).  In noise-free maps a point placement gives the same P2, P3 and centroid shift to 0.05 dex as well,
  and removing 1/2 to 7/8 of the particles changes P2 by at most 0.02 dex, P3 by at most 0.16 dex and the centroid
  shift by 0.001 (independent check); thinning the TNG views to FLAMINGO counts and signal-to-background moves P2 and
  P3 by 0.03 and 0.02 dex.  None of these reproduces the 0.46-0.49 dex gap, which depends on mass (about +0.5 dex
  below 1.6e14 Msun, reversed above 3e14 Msun with 10 pairs) and on the centre (an offset of 10 pixels raises P3 by
  0.6 dex); the bootstrap above treats 100 pairs as independent though they share 76 TNG views.
  Not separated yet: the power ratios and the centroid shift grow with noise, and the FLAMINGO views have 0.35 dex
  fewer counts and a background of the current release, while the TNG-Cluster views are of release 1.1.1 (their
  background statistics are far from the real ones, KS 0.7 for the rate).  The test that separates resolution from
  signal-to-noise is a node job (below).
* **Verdict for now** (corrected 2026-10-28): FLAMINGO L1000N0900's integrated quantities (luminosity, spectral
  temperature, the hydrostatic truth) are usable.  Its images are usable for statistics at the scale of R500c (net
  counts, P2, P3, centroid shift, outer slope) above about 2e14 Msun, and not for structure below about 100 kpc
  (concentration inside 0.1 R500c, cool-core and cavity morphology, small substructure).  The P2/P3/centroid-shift gap
  to TNG-Cluster is not attributed: no test so far ties it to resolution, and the controlled test below separates
  resolution from sample, physics and signal-to-noise.  The higher-resolution run L1_m8 (gas particles 1.3e8 Msun,
  kernel about 4 times smaller) is the direct check.
* **C17 on FLAMINGO.** The sum of the SPH volumes m / rho of the non-star-forming gas in the R500c shell is 0.95 of
  the shell volume in the median (0.84-1.05 over the 100 clusters), so the sanity window 0.9-1.1 of campaign check
  C17, set for Voronoi cells, flags 10 of them.  Not changed: whether SPH runs get their own window is the author's
  call.
* **Node job** (approved by the author on 2026-10-27, run as described in the next section): the controlled image
  test on 200 FLAMINGO clusters and 200 TNG-Cluster views.

### P3: controlled image test (criteria stated before the job)

* **Question.**  Are FLAMINGO L1000N0900 images usable for image-level work (realism, morphology, the fit on images),
  given that its gas particles (8.6e9 Msun, measured on the cubes; 900^3 particles per species) put several to tens
  of photons on each emitter?  And is any difference from TNG-Cluster due to particle resolution or to
  signal-to-noise?
* **Pairs** (`internal/ledger_tests/multisim/p3_controlled_pairs.py`): 200 TNG-Cluster halos of snapshot 84, evenly
  in mass rank, one view each from the max list (the view whose donor was used least), and for each the FLAMINGO
  field halo of snapshot 73 nearest in M500c (each used once; 200 distinct FLAMINGO halos, |dlog M500c| median
  0.000, at most 0.048); 156 distinct donors.  The FLAMINGO row is the TNG row with the identity replaced, so both
  members carry the same donor package and z_obs.
* **Arms** (`internal/ledger_tests/multisim_p3_controlled.py`, one node job), all with the current code (instrument
  and background of release 1.1.3) and the conditions rule `donor` (the donor package's own exposure, background and
  N_H, no jitter: the production rule `nn` draws a conditions donor and a roll from each view's own stream, so the
  two members of a pair would not share them); truth columns and the temet reference off:
  `fl` FLAMINGO, photons in the volume-equivalent sphere; `flk` FLAMINGO, photons from the Wendland C2 kernel
  (`sim.photon_spread`), same streams; `tng` TNG-Cluster; `tngd` TNG-Cluster degraded to the FLAMINGO gas particle
  mass (new hashed-when-set field `sim.cell_resample_msun`: cell i kept with probability m_i / m_FL (1 in 720 on the test halos),
  its mass divided by that probability, so the expected mass and luminosity are kept and the number of emitters is
  FLAMINGO's), smoothing lengths from the density with 58 weighted neighbours in the Wendland C2 support
  H = 1.936492 h, photons placed with that kernel (`sim.photon_spread = 'sph-kernel'`); `tngd` uses the streams of
  `tng` (same views, donors, roll and seeds).  Both TNG arms read the cube index files of half-side 5.66 R500c (the
  tree `campaign_v12_cubes`, every halo of snapshot 84), and every arm uses `sim.mock_box_r500 = 5.66`, the size of
  the FLAMINGO cubes.
* **Count matching.**  Net 0.5-2 keV counts in 0.15-1 R500 of the donor, with the donor's background region
  (`realism.matching.view_counts`), at f = 1; each TNG view (and each degraded view) thinned to the counts of its
  FLAMINGO partner, f = min(N_fl / N_tng, 1), by replaying the instrument on the stored photon list
  (`conditions = {rule: donor, thin: f}`) at the roll of the FLAMINGO view (new `replay_view(posangle_deg=...)`).
  Pairs where TNG is the fainter keep f = 1 and are reported (statistics also given without them).  A second replay
  of every count-matched TNG view with a new instrument realisation (`reseed = 1`) is the noise floor.  Seeds: the
  instrument draws of a view come from one stream in which its cluster photons are drawn before the backgrounds, so
  a shared seed cannot give two different photon lists the same background realisation; the pairs share donor
  package, conditions, z_obs and roll, not the noise.
* **Statistics.**  The 17 statistics of the realism evaluation on every arm and on the real observations of the 156
  donors; per statistic the paired median difference against the count-matched TNG views (`tng_cm`) with a 95 per
  cent bootstrap interval and the Kolmogorov-Smirnov distance to the real set; the grouped classifier two-sample test
  (classifiers lr and gb, groups = donors, `realism.c2st`) of every arm against the real set, and the paired AUC
  difference against `tng_cm` with its donor-bootstrap SE (`realism.c2st.paired_difference`).
* **Extra statistics** (not in the realism evaluation): P2 and P3 with the Poisson noise power sum_i C_i (r_i/R)^(2m)
  removed (Boehringer et al. 2010; Weissmann et al. 2013), and P2, P3 and the centroid shift at the fixed start
  centre (simulation centre; catalogue peak for the real images) without recentring, with the recentring offset.
  Paired differences are also given in three TNG-Cluster mass bins (1-1.6e14, 1.6-3e14, above 3e14 Msun).  The
  pairs are 200 distinct TNG-Cluster halos and 200 distinct FLAMINGO halos, so the bootstrap over pairs is a
  bootstrap over distinct halos; the pairs are matched in M500c (above).
* **Resolution rule, fixed before the job (primary).**  With signal-to-noise fixed by count matching, resolution is
  not the cause of the FLAMINGO - TNG-Cluster gap if the degraded TNG views differ from the full-resolution ones
  (median paired difference tngd_cm - tng_cm) by less than 0.15 dex in log P2 and in log P3 and by less than 0.005
  in the centroid shift.  Reported also for the Poisson-corrected and the fixed-centre statistics.
* **Usability criteria, fixed before the job (secondary).**  FLAMINGO images count as usable when, against the
  count-matched TNG-Cluster views,
  * U1 (classifier): for both classifiers on all 17 statistics, AUC(FLAMINGO vs real) - AUC(tng_cm vs real) <= 0.05,
    and its one-sided 95 per cent upper bound (difference + 1.645 SE) <= 0.10; and
  * U2 (morphology): for each of conc, slope_05_1, centroid_shift, log_p2, log_p3 and n_compact,
    KS(FLAMINGO, real) - KS(tng_cm, real) <= 0.10.
  Verdict: `usable` when `fl` passes both; `usable with the kernel placement` when only `flk` passes; otherwise `not
  as realistic as TNG-Cluster at R500 scale` (integrated quantities unaffected).  The same criteria applied to the noise-floor arm
  are reported as a check that the bounds lie above the noise.
* **Attribution** (reported, not part of the verdict): for each morphology statistic the share of the FLAMINGO -
  tng_cm difference that the degraded TNG views reproduce, (tngd_cm - tng_cm) / (fl - tng_cm); a share of 0.5 or more
  attributes that statistic's difference to particle resolution, a share near 0 to the physics of the two runs
  (feedback, gas fractions) at matched counts.
* **What is not tested:** FLAMINGO's higher-resolution runs (only L1000N0900 is on Vera); the
  production conditions rule `nn` (identical in expectation; the rule changes which observation lends the conditions,
  not the instrument).

### P3: controlled image test, result (job 1890069, 2026-10-28)

* **Run**: one p.vera node, 49 minutes (frozen code `14584f1`): 4 x 200 views generated without an error, 600
  replays, 1,200 mock and 156 real feature rows; stage `stats` in the embed stack on the login node.  Output:
  `tngxmm_dev/MULTISIM/p3c/job/run/` (`verdict.json`, `stats_features.csv`, `stats_c2st.csv`, `stats_massbins.csv`).
  Before matching the TNG-Cluster views carry +0.21 dex more net counts than their FLAMINGO partners (median); 15 of
  200 pairs are capped (TNG fainter); after matching the remaining difference in log net counts within R500 is
  -0.07 dex (FLAMINGO fainter in the core, the matching annulus being 0.15-1 R500).
* **Resolution rule (primary): met.**  Degraded minus full-resolution TNG-Cluster, count-matched, median paired
  difference with 95 per cent bootstrap interval over the 200 distinct halos: log P2 -0.011 [-0.061, +0.031], log P3
  -0.019 [-0.141, +0.076], centroid shift +0.0003 [-0.0001, +0.0010]; Poisson-corrected P2, P3 -0.020, -0.031;
  fixed centre P2, P3, w -0.006, -0.051, -0.0001.  All below 0.15 dex / 0.005: at matched counts, sampling the gas
  with FLAMINGO L1_m10 particles and kernels does not change P2, P3 or the centroid shift, at any mass bin
  (1-1.6e14, 1.6-3e14, above 3e14 Msun: |P2|, |P3| <= 0.1 dex).
* **The earlier gap is not present under control.**  FLAMINGO minus count-matched TNG-Cluster: log P2 +0.075
  [-0.12, +0.25], log P3 -0.19 [-0.36, -0.00], centroid shift +0.0046 [+0.0010, +0.0089], against +0.49, +0.46 and
  +0.007 in the first comparison (old TNG tree and background, 76 reused TNG views); no mass trend within the
  intervals.
* **Usability criteria (secondary): U1 met, U2 not met.**  U1: AUC(vs real) FLAMINGO - count-matched TNG -0.000
  (lr; upper bound +0.036) and -0.032 (gb; -0.000): FLAMINGO views are as hard to tell from the real ones as the
  count-matched TNG views (AUC 0.77 / 0.82 against 0.77 / 0.85).  U2: KS excess against the real set 0.13 for the
  concentration and 0.21 for the slope at 0.5-1 R500 (both above 0.10); centroid shift 0.09, P2 0.00, P3 -0.09,
  compact sources -0.02 pass.  The noise-floor arm passes both.  Verdict by the stated rule: `not as realistic as
  TNG-Cluster at R500 scale`, because of the concentration (FLAMINGO 0.215, TNG 0.242, real 0.252, median) and the
  outer slope (FLAMINGO -5.4, TNG -3.8, real -3.8).  Kernel placement changes neither (KS excess 0.16, 0.17).
* **Reading.**  The degraded TNG views keep TNG's concentration (-0.006) and outer slope (+0.17, interval
  -0.41 to +0.69), so the FLAMINGO profile difference is not an image-sampling effect of the particle mass; it is
  the gas distribution of the L1_m10 run itself (its physics, or the effect of the low resolution on the
  hydrodynamics, which subsampling a high-resolution run does not reproduce).  L1_m8 separates the two.  For now:
  L1_m10 images are usable for P2, P3, the centroid shift and the classifier-level realism at matched counts; not
  for the concentration and the surface-brightness slope.

### Emission abundances: the author's decisions (2026-10-28)

* **Default: 'full'.**  `sim.emission_abundances = 'full'` (every element the simulation tracks, per cell) and
  `sim.emission_untracked = 'other-solar'` are now the SimSpec defaults, so every production preset carries them
  (v1, v1_tng300, v1_tng100, v1_tng50, v1_flamingo, v1_flamingo_l1m8, v1_simba, v1_eagle); unset (None) remains the
  legacy emission of release 1.1, as an explicit opt-out.  'common' stays the option of v1_common, the only mode for
  the TNG mini snapshots: they carry no element fractions, and 'full' refuses them (error naming v1_common).
* **Helium.**  'common': He/H at the Lodders (2003) solar value of the table the fitter uses (`abund lodd`):
  n(He)/n(H) = 0.0793, A(He) = 10.899 (Lodders Table 4), 7.92e-2 in XSPEC's and SOXS's lodd table.  'full': each
  simulation's own helium per cell; TNG (GFM_Metals), FLAMINGO (SmoothedElementMassFractions), EAGLE
  (SmoothedElementAbundance, via the TNG-format conversion) and SIMBA (via the conversion) all track it.  A
  simulation that tracked metals but not helium would get the Lodders solar He/H (the rule of
  `tngxmm.sim.abundances.components`); none of the five does.  Code: no change was needed for either rule.
* **Known difference** (recorded, not changed): S and Ca.  FLAMINGO and EAGLE do not track them and their own
  cooling ties them to Si (mass ratios 0.094 and 0.605); SIMBA tracks them but its TNG-format conversion folds them
  into the 'other' column.  In 'full' mode they enter, with the other untracked metals, at solar ratios scaled by
  the remaining metal mass ('other-solar').
* **Spec hashes** (`spec_hash()`; the generation hashes change too):

  | preset | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | v1 | 198b80e9d4563eec | 2e0dca902e8f8c07 |
  | v1_tng300 | ea44c41bd7505585 | 20bc760c88e5fcea |
  | v1_tng100 | 8c67f0845616ba25 | 0092472961a67239 |
  | v1_tng50 | 1af83cda12b0cc4b | cc10dd5e511385c4 |
  | v1_flamingo | 4bd2224139426a8c | b332c1352c63188e |
  | v1_flamingo_l1m8 | ac209ec5c96d87a8 | 0b7f4c35d5feaffe |
  | v1_simba | a7c9fe1babce4244 | 81d2c5af20c7fcdc |
  | v1_eagle | c9388b1faf005d65 | d601cd1bb60fe814 |
  | v1_common | f41cc585fa005068 | f41cc585fa005068 (unchanged: 'common', untracked rule unset) |

  The pinned hashes of the tests follow (test_config, test_instrument_cluster_psf, test_donor_exclusion,
  test_massfit_bg_seed).
* **Consequences, flagged for the author:** (1) the frozen realism registration (`realism.frozen.spec_hash` in
  `conf/base/parameters/pipeline.yml`) follows the preset to 2e0dca902e8f8c07, the release-1.1.1 value kept in a
  comment ("a changed preset is another production"); its configuration hash is unchanged.  (2) The golden fixture
  products (`golden_v1.1.1`) are of the v1 spec 198b80e9d4563eec: the C1-C17 fixture test skips until they are
  regenerated with `tools/make_v1_golden.sh` under the release that carries this change (recorded as
