  the mismatch); planning and the closure generation refuse donors without a response set. Arms: the calibration error
  is bracketed by [`v1_xcallo`, nominal] (Schellenberger et al. 2015 with a correct ACIS calibration: -22.6 per cent at
  10 keV for the three cameras against the paper's 23, -21.7 for the pn against its 29, not reproduced) and the
  EPIC-to-NuSTAR pair `v1_nustarhi`, `v1_nustarlo`; `v1_xcalhi` is a diagnostic; `v1_obsresp` and `v1_obsresp_shared`
  generate with the donor's responses and fit with each set. The report states its limits: one temperature, no
  background, equal camera exposures, a contiguous pn hard band. Defaults and spec hashes unchanged (todo/r5-responses,
  d51d9ed; merge 4cc6b69). The adoption, both sides on the per-observation responses and the SAS selection, moves every
  spec hash and needs the response set (one node); it is the author's (STATUS Q20), and its branch is cut again on the
  merged tip.
* **The mock's catalogue entry (A3, B1, B2, C4; rule version 3).** Position: the search starts at the local maximum
  reached from the halo centre by steepest ascent within the 4 R500c cylinder and never takes the cylinder's global
  maximum. PSZ2: then the highest local maximum within 10' (repeated while a higher one is in reach) and its peak, on
  the white-noise filter. ACT DR6: the S/N-weighted centre of the S/N > 3 segment that holds it (Hilton et al. 2021
  Sect. 2.2; ACT DR6 Sect. 2.2.2), labelled inside the cylinder, on the 2.4' map filtered with tau B / (C_l B^2 + N_w),
  a CMB + white model standing in for DR6's map-estimated noise: C_l the Planck 2018 lensed TT (PLA to l = 2508, CAMB
  2.0.5 at the same parameters to 10,000, within 0.3 per cent of the PLA spectrum over 2-2508; package table
  `sim/calibration/cmb_tt_lensed_planck2018.csv` with its provenance), N_w = 14 uK arcmin (Naess et al. 2025, p. 15).
  The map is scaled so that the halo's own maximum carries `fixed_SNR`, which keeps the start in its segment (the
  segment of version 2 could drop it). MACS: the X-ray peak. Why the CMB filter: the review found the version-2
  centroid, taken on the white-noise map, a worse estimate than the peak it replaced; its segments were 3.1-11.7 times
  those of a CMB filter, the old white peak lay nearer the CMB-filter centroid than the white centroid in 11 of 12
  cached ACT views (median 3.3" against 9.4"), and both merges of the review's scan were products of the white filter.
  Under version 3 the centroid lies a median 2.3" (0.09 r90; 90th percentile 12") from the white peak of the same
  detection, against 4.1" (32") for the version-2 centroid; versions 2 to 3 move the ACT positions by median 6.5" (0.26
  r90; 90th percentile 41", at most 264"). Rejected: the white filter with the peak, which is not DR6's estimator.
  Brackets: N_w 10 or 20 moves the positions by median 0.9", at most 8"; the three-band filter by median 1.1", 90th
  percentile 5.5", at most 37". Declared: one band for DR6's three, one median depth, no foregrounds or 1/f noise.
  Against version 1 the search from the halo centre changes the detection of 2 of the 136 SZ views of the sample; with
  the white-filter ACT centroid version 2 had moved 64 of them by more than 1" (the first draft said 2). Flags
  (`CPFLAG`, `obs_meta.json`): `global-elsewhere` 3 views, `merged` 1 (with the saddle S/N), `off-halo` 3 (PSZ2), `edge`
  67 of 67 PSZ2 and 52 of 69 ACT views (with `edge_weight`); they select nothing (STATUS Q22-Q25). The rule version
  (`CATPOSV`) and the filter keys are checked at target build; the realism start of a mock is its own first pass,
  the guarded X-ray peak found from its catalogue entry (`realism.features.mock_start_centre`).
  Redshift: a mock of a donor with a photometric redshift (64 ACT targets) is fitted at z_obs plus a draw from the
  published `redshiftErr`; the truth (result.json, the mock table, the renderer at rho_c(z_obs)) and the count-matching
  annulus stay at z_obs, realism features and mock aperture photometry use the fitted z, and a photometric fit without
  z_obs is refused. Resume: generation, closure fits, collect, the closure table, the certification queue, the campaign
  cache and the fits' `--resume` follow the rule version, the full rule keys, the current redshift draw and the image
  tree; stale closure records never enter a verdict, and `GEN_FROM` stops rather than regenerate into a linked tree.
  Spec hashes unchanged (todo/r5-catalogue-entry, 3f5a296 and 5cffd64, taken into multisim as it stands). The catalogue,
  seed and centre tables are rebuilt from the merged tip (STATUS A0); until then the end-to-end v1 test fails against
  the data package (it passes against a scratch mirror holding the rebuilt table), and every view and cell made under an
  earlier rule is void.
* **The generator-instrument-fitter chain (STATUS B11).** The forward check of 2026-11-03 evaluated the fit's own model
  at the truth of clean closure cells (M500 4 and 8e14 Msun at z = 0.06, 0.2, 0.5) against the stage's counts per
  camera, band and annulus: the two sides did not use one calibration. Each fix is a preset field whose old value gives
  the previous spec (fef1c34c6c0fe914 with the five old values). Effective area (`instrument.arf_model = 'fit-linear'`):
  the stage folds the fit's on-axis ARF on the RMF's energy bins times the XAREAEF vignetting, from the CCF files
  `ccf_selection` picks for the donor's date; upstream `calc_arf` sampled the curves at the centres of 20 eV bins, so
  the bin over the O-K edge took 33 per cent too much MOS area and the MOS 0.5-1 keV area was 1.1-1.2 per cent high.
  Rejected: giving the fit the stage's ARF, which would put the sampling artefact into every real fit. Thin2 has no fit
  response and is refused on both sides (no donor uses it). Band channels (`massfit.band_channels = 'image-pi'`): the
  rate tables take the channels the stage images (EBOUNDS centre in [lo, hi)), where mbproj2d's energy ignore kept only
  those wholly inside the band (MOS 0.5-1 keV short by 1.2 per cent, pn 0.85); the PPS images select PI one value apart
  at each edge (pn -0.21 / +0.1 per cent in 0.5-1 / 1-2 keV, MOS at most 0.12). Vignetting (`massfit.vignetting_model =
  'sub-band'`, tolerance 0.01): a band's cluster model is [PSF * sum_j SB_bj V(theta, E_j)] x U over 14 energy nodes
  that follow the CCF vignetting within 1 per cent to 10 keV and 15', U the unvignetted exposure from the 0.5-1 keV map;
  before, a band was vignetted at its exposure map's one energy and after the PSF (the 4.5-12 keV model 6 per cent short
  at 3', up to 45 per cent at 12-15'). The earlier reading that the PPS hard-band maps are vignetted at 7.1-7.6 keV
  (report V4-W2, STATUS B11) did not hold in review: the PPS maps follow eexpmap's mean energy, 8.25 keV, and the CCF
  vignetting fits them best at 8.26-8.29 keV on both fixtures and all three cameras, so mock and real maps already
  shared one rule and the fault was the fit's. Rejected: a count-weighted band energy (spectrum- and angle-dependent,
  and the PSF order still wrong) and a full energy grid (the same accuracy at many times the cost); one model evaluation
  goes from 0.034 to 0.059 s. pn rows (`instrument.pn_edge_rows = 'footprint'`): 'drop' counted 12 rows from the
  footprint's edge, which already ends at the readout rows, and removed the real rows 14-25 (3.6-6.9 per cent of the pn
  events). The depth arms `v1_deep` and `v1_half` now scale the donor live times (EXPOSURE was unchanged before). What
  the evidence shows: the stage's and the fit's responses agree within 0.25 per cent per camera and band; the counts
  within R500 match the model at the truth to 0.996-1.002 at z <= 0.2. The fits the first report called noiseless are
  Asimov data, the fit model at the truth times response ratios: every spatial term cancels in them, so they test the
  response alone (Z 0.293-0.305, M500 within 0.7 per cent; with the old values Z 0.35-0.66, M500 -9 to +15 per cent),
  not the closure. Maximum-likelihood fits to the cells' own counts give M500 / truth 0.926-1.048 (STATUS, closed
  table). New open items: B12, the z = 0.5 normalisation, which the review showed to be mostly the model's one-pixel
  shells (and whose first errors were too small by sqrt 2), and B13, the mass of the deep z = 0.06 cell. Spec hash of v1
  `4f3e8995401e3c53`; `realism.frozen.spec_hash` registered again (todo/b11-chain-fixes, 45f2716 and e2e99b3; merge
  1bac885).
* **Sampler switches and chain diagnostics (STATUS Q18).** Fit-fix `sampler_moves`: `stretch` (default, mbproj2d's
  sampler) or `de`, emcee's DEMove alone; `walker_start`: `ball` (default) or `scaled`, a normal approximation at the
  optimum in which parameters without information (an eigenvalue below 1e3 eps of the largest) or within 1 sd of a
  prior bound keep the ball and the rest are drawn conditional on them, with draws outside the support redrawn without
  a model call. At the defaults the chain is bit for bit the old one, and the provenance's fit options and every
  closure `fit_config` are unchanged. The first version offered `de-snooker`, DEMove and DESnookerMove mixed 0.8 / 0.2
  as in the emcee documentation; the review found that the snooker move of emcee 3.1.6 does not keep the target (its
  direction is not a unit vector and its log Metropolis factor has half the exponent of ter Braak & Vrugt 2008 eq. 4:
  on a 34-dimensional Gaussian it samples 0.54 of the variance alone and 0.76 in the mixture, DEMove alone 1.00), so it
  is not used, and a test samples N(0, s^2 I) at d = 10 and 34 with every switch's moves. Every result.json carries
  `chain_diagnostics`: rank-normalised and classic R-hat, bulk ESS, tau and N/tau, the largest R-hat, the log-posterior
  rise, the acceptance and the ln M500 quantiles over all kept draws. The review found the pilot's ESS and N/tau at the
  estimator's ceilings, so they are numbers only where R-hat < 1.01 and the estimator truncated, otherwise "below
  resolution" with the raw values kept. The QC column `chain_lp_rising` (one-sided, above 2 sd) is reported and cuts
  nothing. Pilot (4 cells at z = 0.5, 2 seeds; rerun on the corrected code, the snooker rows and the first scaled start
  withdrawn): no chain, quick or long, resolves its ESS; quick-chain R-hat median 2.34 (stretch, ball), 1.53 (DE,
  ball), 2.37 (stretch, scaled), 1.78 (DE, scaled); seed rms of ln M500 0.121 (0.049 without the climbing cell), 0.044,
  0.023, 0.033, near the floor of about 0.02 of a median of 200 draws; long DE chains accept 0.03-0.06 with single
  walkers at 0.001. The scaled start is over-dispersed on real cells (median start 74-218 below the optimum in log
  posterior, 1,281 in one fit, against about 17 for a normal posterior of 34 parameters). The arms share starts and
  optima and the long chains are still moving, so the pilot supports no accuracy or width claim. Closure arms
  `v1sas_de`, `v1sas_scaled`, `v1sas_long` and `v1sas_delong` (the candidate reference) run after B1 on its views and
  its commit. The production sampler, the flag threshold and the reference are the author's (todo/q18-sampler,
  77b1958 and 7f7f5d0; merge 0711254).
* **Package calibration tables in the spec hash.** An edited table in a `calibration/` folder of the package (the pn
  CTI slopes of A5, the CMB spectrum of the ACT filter) made views that shared the spec hash of the old one. The sha256
  of every such table now enters the spec hash (`config.calibration_tables_sha256`), and every spec hash moves: v1
  `9937b33364b11ac4` (v1_tng300 `161e6fde3a9712cf`, v1_tng100 `eff54c2c29438adc`, v1_tng50 `beacb782342c503f`,
  v1_common `780c59bc5622fda5`, v1_flamingo `04b273b15e289753`, v1_flamingo_l1m8 `605d1c57e7f26f44`, v1_simba
  `c415903ea860ee02`, v1_eagle `ab954c86da7f066b`); `realism.frozen.spec_hash` registered again before any frozen run.
  The seed hash does not move (3283b9e).
* **Still open from round 5:** the adoption of the per-observation responses (STATUS Q20); the z = 0.5 normalisation
  and the deep z = 0.06 mass (B12, B13); the sampler (Q18); the catalogue-entry points (Q22-Q25); the data-package
  tables of STATUS A0 and the regeneration of every mock and closure cell.

## Independent review, round 6 (2026-11-04)

In the sixth read (`$TNGXMM_DEV/REVIEW_FACTORS/REVIEW6.md`, the code at 06942d6) no new tuned or mock-measured
number was found in the production path, and the changes of round 5 were found in the code. Three items change
results (A1 the shared responses, A2 no vignetting or PSF calibration arm, A3 the exact redshift of the PSZ2
photometric counterparts); five are shortcuts or stale docs (B1-B5) and eleven are minor points (C1-C11). Each went
to a branch, where the change was read independently and reworked before its merge.

* **Responses (A1).** The default no longer rests on the cancellation of two errors. The presets keep the shared
  responses with the highest file-name issue as the production choice until the author decides (STATUS Q20), and its
  real-side error is quoted for both selections: +1.0 / +2.2 / +4.1 per cent at 3 / 5 / 8 keV with the withdrawn files,
  +2.2 / +3.9 / +6.7 with cifbuild's. The three terms (ABSCORRAREA, the withdrawn MOS quantum efficiency, the rest),
  each measured alone, add to the total of every epoch within 0.06 points (0.3 at 8 keV in the XSPEC check); at 8 keV
  sum and total run from 2.31 / 2.31 in 2000 to 5.56 / 5.62 in 2025 (`docs/dev/reports/R5-A4-responses.md`). The arm
  `v1_xcallo` applies the ACIS/PN spline to every camera: at 10 keV pn -21.7, MOS1 -24.2, MOS2 -24.0 per cent against
  29 / 17 / 22 in Table 2 of Schellenberger et al. (2015), so its three-camera -22.6 against the paper's 23 is partly
  coincidental. Checks at fit: an epoch index whose `ccf_dir` or `analysis_date` is not the site's is refused
  (`build_responses.ccf_epoch`), and a mock fitted with the per-epoch shared ARFs must carry that interval's quantum
  efficiency file in `QEFILE` (`MockResponseMismatch`). The donor ARF enters the stage averaged over each stage bin
  (`obs_responses.arf_bin_average`), not read at the bin centres; SAS writes it on the RMF's bins (checked on
  0692934001), so under `fit-linear` the band means agree with the donor ARF to 1e-5, where the centre reading on the
  old 20 eV grid had put MOS1 0.5-1 keV 0.5 per cent high. `REGION_MAX_ARCSEC` is an OPEN_ITEMS P row (C9). The
  adoption (both sides on the per-observation responses and the SAS selection, `RESPSET` in the headers and
  `obs_meta.json`, the `v1_obsresp*` arms on the v1 cells) is on todo/r5-responses-adopt2 (f2403cc), cut before the
  other round-6 merges and not merged: it needs the response set, the coverage test made to skip until that set
  exists, and the docs (todo/r5-responses-followup, 947f19a and 2bad89e; merge 7a335fa).
* **Vignetting and PSF arms (A2).** `v1_vighi` and `v1_viglo` multiply the effective area of every camera (cluster, sky
  and AGN photons) by g(theta) = 1 + 0.05 theta / 10' or by 1 / g (`instrument.area_perturbation =
  'vignetting-tn18:+-1'`); exposure maps, templates and fit stay nominal. The size is XMM-SOC-CAL-TN-0018 Sect. 4.5
  (p. 13): with the current axis calibration "the differences in flux for offaxis sources for each camera are ~5%", a
  residual flux difference of off-axis sources per camera, of which a radially coherent g is the conservative reading.
  The form is ours and declared (OPEN_ITEMS P): 1 on the axis, linear through the quoted point and beyond 10', the
  same at every energy and for the three cameras, since the document gives one angle and no energy dependence. The
  MOS-to-pn difference of up to 20 per cent at about 12' (Sect. 2.3) is a difference between cameras, not an accuracy
  per camera; it stays a stated real-side term, not an arm. `v1_psfell` draws the cluster photons with the CCF ELLBETA
  envelope the AGN use, at each photon's energy and detector position (`psf_model = 'ellbeta'`, header `PSFMODEL`), and
  fits with the on-axis King as for real data. Read et al. (2011) call the ELLBETA fits of the 10.25 and 13.25 keV
  stacks unstable (p. 4), so the arm is reported per band and its slope is not capped. Only the parent photon list and,
  for `v1_psfell`, the PSF draw are paired with the base cells; the later draws are not. Set in no preset, not run;
  spec hashes unchanged.
* **PSZ2 counterpart redshifts (A3; redshift rule 2).** A PSZ2 redshift is `spec` when it lies within 5e-4 of a
  spectroscopic redshift of the counterpart's catalogue entry, `phot` when within 5e-4 of its photometric redshift (for
  redMaPPer also within the published z_lambda error, or when the public v5.10 lacks the cluster), else `untyped`
  (`catalogue_positions.psz2_redshift`, OPEN_ITEMS P). PSZ2 matched to the proprietary redMaPPer v5.10 (PSZ2 Sect.
  7.3.1), not v6.3; the public v5.10 (Rozo et al. 2015; Rykoff et al. 2016) reproduces PSZ2's values within 0.6 of their
  error for all 26 donors it holds, and its per-cluster errors are read from NED, which is published as Gaussian
  (Rykoff et al. 2014, Table 1). Where no per-cluster error is published, the paper's: redMaPPer 0.006 at z ~ 0.1 and
  0.02 at z ~ 0.5 (the nearer quoted redshift, for the two clusters absent from v5.10), WHL 0.018 (Wen, Han & Liu 2012,
  Sect. 2.4, an upper bound, used as sigma; the per-bin values of their Fig. 4 were not taken), MaxBCG 0.01 (Koester
  et al. 2007). Of the 40 donors 31 are phot (26 with catalogue errors, 5 with paper errors), 5 spec and 4 untyped (WHL
  counterparts whose value PSZ2 does not quote). A table without `z_rule == 2` is refused; the rebuild adds
  `--counterparts` and the columns `z_err_source`, `z_rule` (input table and query cache in
  `tngxmm_refs/psz2_counterparts/`). A scratch rebuild keeps the 638 rows and positions; 143 of the 2,840 targets (29
  physics donors) get a drawn redshift, none of the 9 closure donors changes type (todo/r6-arms, 85409f8 and 8703598;
  merge 44d8e45).
* **Donor-table checks (B1, B2).** The mock stage refuses the tables the real fit refuses. Each donor-table sidecar
  records its builder's `measure_version` and, for the MOS background and corner tables, the FWC rule
  (`epoch-normal-1`); `donor_tables.check_table` compares them, the corner table is built by the new node
  `donor_tables.build_qpb_corners` (shardable) with the FWC reference of each camera stored in its result, and the
  real fit reads it through the same check. The centre check chains to the seed check (the table's own bytes, a clean
  tree, the current seed table and seed hash, then `check_seed_table`, which refuses a dirty-built seed table), and a
  data-package input whose sidecar records an uncommitted tree is refused. Repository files are held by their commit
  and exempt (the packaged observations.csv records `git_dirty: true` at c9a0b0c).
* **The files a view is made from (the branch review's finding 8, and its second round).** Every data-package file a
  view or a closure cell reads is checked before the first view at the plan, again per view and per closure arm, and
  recorded with its sha256 (`sim.data_inputs`; `data_inputs` in `obs_meta.json` and `truth.json`). Files a builder of
  the package writes are checked by their sidecar: the donor tables, now with the in-field background, the total
  column and the RASS sky; the active CCDs and CCD maps; the source thresholds (measurement version and columns); the
  catalogue positions (redshift and position rules and columns). Files made from data outside the package are pinned
  by sha256 in `sim/data_manifest.csv`: the APEC tables, the frozen ECF table, the SFD reddening table and 35 AtomDB
  files the photon spectra are drawn from; every pinned row is checked. A sidecar for the APEC tables was rejected:
  their only inputs are the AtomDB files, so the pin carries the same information. The readers of the active CCDs,
  CCD maps, catalogue positions and source thresholds check the package copy, and a stored view or cell is reused only
  when its record equals the current one. The packaged observations.csv (z, N_H, exposures and matching mass of every
  donor) and the data manifest enter the spec hash (C10). Every package table but the pinned ones is refused until
  rebuilt; STATUS A0 and `background.md` hold one nine-step rebuild list. Because the in-field background table is now
  checked, its rebuild is required and takes 39 donors out of the conditions pool; that is the author's (STATUS Q19),
  and dropping `"background"` from `CENTRE_TABLES` would record the table without checking it (todo/r6-checks,
  374c5b5 and d92a6f7, merge bbbc02a; todo/r6-inputs, 933b174 and 7458bc3).
* **Docs and minor points (B4, C1, C3, C8, C9, C11).** The stale lines of STATUS, DECISIONS, INTERFACES, the table
  schema and OPEN_ITEMS are corrected, and "bounds" no longer names a one-sided arm. `n_pivot` counts the failed seeds
  (`pivot (<status>)`) too. The Lx photometry and the recentring take the preset's `mu_tvir`. The count-matching annulus
  at the true centre is stated as a diagnostic (the frozen evaluation does not use it). An unknown pn mode of a donor
  with a pn exposure is refused in the replica targets and the stage (`conditions_donor.pn_oot_fraction`). The PSZ2
  merge stays a greedy ascent, declared as a reading of PSZ2 Sect. 2.1 (10'), 3.1 and 7.1 (5'), which do not decide it;
  a single merge or the Sect. 3.1 reference rule would raise `POSITION_RULE_VERSION` (STATUS Q23).
* **Hashes.** Spec hash of v1 `9937b33364b11ac4` -> `9ee9e06acaf41d07` (the observation table) -> `0c5f1754d7c1220a`
  (the data manifest) -> `66a72be359876779` (the AtomDB files in the manifest); v1_tng300 `8ac22aef340a6fe2`,
  v1_tng100 `6f590d0202ab4503`, v1_tng50 `102b3412741ec450`, v1_common `b3b4aef94ece718c`, v1_flamingo
  `9ae2305fd1a9f131`, v1_flamingo_l1m8 `e01de926c76b8bbf`, v1_simba `a24f939d3f8929aa`, v1_eagle `646958bb539ea311`;
  `realism.frozen.spec_hash` registered again before any frozen run. The seed hash of v1 moves from `75d7b92d59b4b8ed`

## Analyses of 2026-11-03 (login, read only)

Four studies ran on the login node. Each report was read independently and revised afterwards.

* **B8, the fitted metallicity of the mocks** (`TODO_RUN/b8-metallicity/REPORT.md`). On the 18 D1 views above 20,000
  counts, Z_fit over the emission-weighted Fe of the gas is 0.60 [0.04, 1.10] at z < 0.15 and 1.33 [0.91, 2.53] above
  (pooled 0.97, 0.003-11 per view). The solar table gives nothing on the mock side (lodd in generator, fitter and truth),
  the element mix +7 per cent in the bands, the untracked-element rule about 2 per cent. The free Z of the four-band
  image fit follows the temperature structure and the redshift, not the metals: it is a nuisance parameter, not quoted as
  an abundance or compared between mocks and data (Q15 unchanged). The TNG gas Fe (0.25 lodd) is half of the
  spectroscopic 0.3 AG89 = 0.48 lodd of real clusters; the mock-against-real difference of Z_fit is confounded by
  redshift and is not evidence of a gas difference. The closure cells, built in the fitter's own model space, show a Z
  rise with redshift that is a defect of the chain: STATUS B11.
* **B9, convergence of the stored chains** (`TODO_RUN/b9-chain-convergence/REPORT.md`). On 4,473 distinct long chains no
  long chain has mixed per walker: the autocorrelation time of ln M500 is of order 10^3 steps, so tau, R-hat and ESS
  measure the window, and independent repeats give an ESS of the median about equal to the number of walkers. The
  ensemble's log posterior is still rising in a sixth of the fits by more than 2 sd. The Monte-Carlo scatter of a long
  chain's median about its shared start is NMAD 0.010-0.011 (RMS 0.014-0.027), on mocks; a bias shared by chains with the
  same start is not measured. Quick chains scatter about the long ones by NMAD 0.028-0.032 with 16-84 widths 20 per cent
  narrower. The chain-based Monte-Carlo errors understate the repeat scatter 1.5-3.7 times. The sampler is STATUS Q18.
* **F3, FLAMINGO L1_m8 against L1_m10** (`TODO_RUN/f3-l1m8-vs-l1m10/REPORT.md`). At fixed M500c L1_m8 is about 0.14 dex
  fainter at 4-10e14 Msun (three like-for-like estimators, -0.136 to -0.140 at z = 0.2), 0.04-0.07 dex at 1.6-3e14 and
  0-0.02 dex at 1.2-1.6e14. 0.05 dex is the gas fraction; the rest is hot gas missing at 0.06-0.5 R500c, where L1_m8 has a
  higher entropy and a lower density, while the gas beyond 0.5 R500c agrees. The two runs change particle mass and
  subgrid parameters together, so the difference cannot be split into resolution and calibration; L1_m8 stays the
  reference and the difference is quoted as its model systematic. The LXGAP report's "+0.1 dex at 1-1.6e14 Msun" was a
  binning artefact.
* **A6, the truth ratio of the L_X(1 Mpc) estimator** (`internal/ledger_tests/lx_truth_ratio_terms.py`). The median
  log10(measured / intrinsic) of -0.071 dex on the v1.0.1 TNG-Cluster views is the count image, which the on-axis ECF
  leaves out by design (exposure map -0.059, PSF -0.018, out-of-time -0.005 dex), not the background annulus (-0.003);
  the steps sum to -0.078 against -0.079 measured, with -0.02 to -0.03 dex unexplained above z_obs = 0.5. The pages state
  it as the net ratio of the measurement chain; whether it cancels between mocks and real data is not measured.

## Release 1.2 groundwork: E2 and E3 (2026-11-03)

Code and tests only, set in no preset; the existing presets give byte-identical target lists, gate P and matched draw.

* **E2, conditions donor per projection.** `donors.observing_conditions.per_projection` (hashed only when set): the
  views of a halo-snapshot draw in view order, view v taking the candidate picked by the first integer of its own
  `conditions` stream among the five nearest pool observations of its physics donor's fold that no earlier view took;
  the views never share a conditions donor. The anchors are those of every view of the halo-snapshot (the
  `conditions_anchors` column of the pairing, else all rows read before `runtime.exclude_donors`): the first version
  built them after the exclusion, so excluding one donor changed the conditions donor of 55 of 180 views of another.
  Where views share an anchor, a later view draws from what the earlier ones left (median distance 2.20 at view 0, 2.99
  at view 5); the view order is part of the stream contract and is kept. `donors.pair_projections` gives a halo-snapshot
  up to six distinct physics donors within dz 0.25 and 0.10 dex on mass columns the caller names (no default: the plan
  pairs M_fit on both sides), repeats the nearest in turn when short and never widens the window (the 0.10 dex rung is
  the author's, OPEN_ITEMS P); unpaired halo-snapshots are left out of gate P, the matched draw and the realism
  selection, and the generation refuses them until E1 writes them.
* **E3, folds and bootstrap by halo.** The split unit is (sim, zoom_target_id). The 3,258 halo-snapshots of
  `campaign_v1_full` hold 2,657 (sim, halo_id) pairs and 535 halos; `v4_common` has 292 zoom targets with up to 89 views
  each. Halo folds (5, seed 20261103; OPEN_ITEMS P) sit beside the linked donor folds with the same count; the
  halo-donor graph of every committed list is one component, so fold f trains on views with halo and donor both outside f
  and tests on those with both in f (19 per cent of the maximal and box views are ever tested). A simulation whose
  identity column is a snapshot's halo id at several snapshots is refused unless a following column or halo-snapshot
  groups are named; the rule is stored in the table and read back. The halo bootstrap draws halos with all their views
  and gives no interval below two halos. A candidate table is committed under `campaigns/halo_folds/` with its sha256;
  which table to freeze, and whether the pairing draws a halo's physics donors from its own fold, are the author's.

## The model averaged over the pixel (2026-11-10)

* **What changed.** `massfit.model_pixel_average = 8` in every preset. Within 24 fit pixels of the centre the painted
  model is the mean over an 8 x 8 grid inside each pixel, before the PSF (`massfit.model_shells.paint_profile`);
  mbproj2d reads the profile at the pixel's centre. It applies to mocks and real observations alike.
* **Why.** A pixel holds the integral of the surface brightness over its area (Peng et al. 2002, Sect. 3.1). Read at
  the centre, a cool core is too peaked on coarse pixels. At the true parameters of the 54 clean closure cells the pull
  on ln M500 of the cool-core cells fell from +8.0 to +1.4 per cent at z = 0.06 and from +2.9 to +0.1 per cent at
  z = 0.2; the non-cool-core cells did not move.
* **Refits (job 1896730, arm `v1sas_pixavg`, paired with `v1sas` of the grid of 2026-11-05).** Cool-core masses:
  -4.3, -1.2 and -7.4 per cent at z = 0.06, 0.2 and 0.5 on the clean cells, -11.5, -5.1 and -4.5 per cent on the
  real-background cells. Non-cool-core masses moved by at most 2.3 per cent. The fits took 1.82 times as long.
* **What it did not do.** The pooled clean ratio of the usable fits stayed at 1.046 (27 usable of 54, interval about
  1.010 to 1.084), outside the window of PREREGISTRATION section 1; the 54 ok fits give 1.015. The gate was designed
  for 54 usable fits. The fix is adopted because a pixel holds the integral over its area; it does not bring the
  grid inside the window.
* **Numerical choices.** 8 sub-pixels per side and the 24-pixel half-width: 16 per side or 48 pixels change ln M500
  by at most 0.21 per cent on three real closure cells (OPEN_ITEMS P).
* **Hashes.** Spec hash of `v1` 03b55819bfd0ff0d (was ec99a22c20347f31); generation hash unchanged, so no view is
  generated again; seed hash dbcd9c755fb3a153, so the seed table, the guarded centres and the core offsets are
  measured again before any fit.

## Three observations left out of the real list and the donors (2026-11-12)

* **What changed.** `runtime.exclude_donors` of every preset lists, besides 0720700101, the observations 0780291001,
  0747420101 and 0161560101, each with its reason. A listed observation lends no view and is not fitted:
  `massfit build-realfit-targets` leaves it out of the targets and names it with its reason under `not_fitted` in the
  summary. No hash changes (the field is a runtime field).
* **0780291001** (ACT-CL J1235.8+1331). MOS2 ran in FastUncompressed mode. In the fast modes the central MOS CCD gives
  no image (XMM-Newton Users Handbook, Sect. 3.3.2), and the cluster sits on that CCD, so SAS rmfgen finds no imaging
  events in the cluster region and stops (`invalidNormalisation`). The author chose to exclude the observation rather
  than fit it without MOS2. It had already left the conditions pool for the same mode (pool rule 2).
* **0747420101** (ACT-CL J0129.2-0023). The catalogue position and the guarded peak are 53' from the pointing, outside
  the 15' field of view; arfgen refuses the region (`MapYRangeInvalid`: the detector-map range is inverted) and no
  response exists. It was already an excluded donor for its 57' core offset (`donors.exclude_obsids`).
* **0161560101** (ACT-CL J2308.9-0651). The cluster is 17' off axis, at the edge of the MOS field (PartialW3); the
  MOS regions hold no events on a CCD (`ccd = 0` at the source position) and rmfgen stops as above. The pn response
  was built. Also an excluded donor for its core offset.
* **Evidence.** The SAS logs and `provenance.json` of the three entries of the per-observation response set
  (`$TNGXMM_OBS_RESP/<obsid>/logs`), manifest status FAIL.
* **Realism registration.** 0780291001 was a held-out donor with five replica views; the frozen block of
  `params:realism` is re-registered before any frozen run: 84 held-out donors (was 85), split sha256 499e3e00...
  (was 3f3104e4...). The other two have no replica views and change no role. The author signed off the split of 84
  held-out donors on 2026-11-12 (`docs/realism.md`, "The donor split").

## The observation table enters the hash by the columns that are read (2026-11-12)

* **Why.** A rebuild of `observations.csv` that changed only `m_ref_cosmology` and `n_ref_masses`, two columns no
  stage reads, moved the spec hash of `v1` (03b55819 to 8ab51a06) and its generation hash (4b7c3e82 to 81851dc2),
  because the file was hashed whole. Every generated view and every fit would have been stale for a change that
  cannot reach them.
* **Rule (the author's decision).** The hash takes the columns a view or a fit is made from and nothing else:
  `config.OBSERVATION_COLUMNS`, 38 of the 70 columns, each with its kind. It is the list of the columns that code
  outside the table's builder (`tngxmm.obs`) names. `config.table_columns_digest` sorts the column names and the
  rows (by obsid) and writes every number as the shortest text that gives the same double, so the order of rows
  and columns, the digits of a number and the line ends do not enter.
* **Not hashed.** `centre_source`, `mass_source_metadata`, `nh_source`, `n_files`, `n_exp_pn`, `n_exp_mos1`,
  `n_exp_mos2`, `r500_catalogue_kpc`, `lx_source`, `cluster_id_source`, the four `m500_catalogue_*` text columns,
  `m_ref_err_lo_msun`, `m_ref_err_hi_msun`, `m_ref_radius_definition`, `m_ref_method`, `m_ref_cosmology`,
  `m_ref_bias_adjustment`, `ref_mass_kinds`, `n_ref_masses`, `in_psz2`, `has_dr6_match`, `in_act_dr6`, `in_cccp`,
  `in_wtg`, `in_g3_obsrc`, `is_lowz_zdonor`, `in_reference_sample`, `reference_reason`, `catalogue_source_keys`.
* **Guard.** `tests/unit/test_observation_columns.py` fails when a module outside `obs/`, `schemas/`, `viz/` and
  `ledger/` names one of these columns: a stage that starts to read one adds it to the list, and the hashes move.
  The test reads the source for the column names; a column reached only through a whole-row copy would escape it.
* **Hashes.** Every spec and generation hash moves once, with no field and no table changed: `v1` 03b55819bfd0ff0d
  to 0e1f9258c78fd676 (generation 4b7c3e82aef60d70 to 6edbe80999c787ac), `v1_flamingo_l1m8` 951c4fa187514dd9 to
  d44c7e76ff77085e (generation fb0a32d23c71b36f to aefd2356416e0f17). With the file's sha256 of that day in place
  of the digest the old hashes come back (`test_the_column_digest_is_the_only_change_of_2026_11_12`). The seed hash
  stays dbcd9c755fb3a153.
* **Left as they are.** The calibration tables and `sim/data_manifest.csv` are hashed as files: they hold values
  and pins, are not rebuilt by a command, and change only when a value does. The seed table's own check of the
  observation table (`photometry.build_obs_seed_lx.check_seed_table`) still compares the whole file; that module's
  code is part of the seed hash, so the same rule there waits for the next change of the seed procedure. Until then
  a rebuilt `observations.csv` needs the seed table and the guarded centres measured again before real fits.

## The pass-1 preset (2026-11-12)

* **What it is.** `v1_flamingo_l1m8_pass1`: the physics and the fit of `v1_flamingo_l1m8` with projection 0 only
  (`sim.views = (0,)`), the conditions rule `donor` (every view under its donor as it is, no neighbours, no jitter)
  and the stream key `v4_flamingo_l1m8_pass1`. Its list is `campaigns/v4_flamingo_l1m8_pass1`, 5,001 views under
  0762440401. Spec hash 87a47ef20c163446, generation hash 6be0aa375a8cfcde.
* **Placement (the author's decision).** The cluster sits at the reference's core offset, 1.78 arcmin off axis, as
  every production view sits at its donor's. An on-axis placement is not written. At the core offset the
  background rule of the fit images holds up to theta500 = 7.3 arcmin (7.8 on axis).
* **Every view is fitted (the author's decision).** The domain prediction puts 2,290 of the 5,001 views outside
  the domain. They are fitted all the same: whether a fit is outside is known when it has run. The prediction is
  a column of the fit targets; no step selects or orders by it.
* **z = 0.** The 665 views of snapshot 78 keep the z_obs of their source row (0.042 to 0.114, the redshift of the
  donor the catalogue-mass rule gave them), since a halo at z = 0 has no distance. The rule is unchanged; the
  options are in the campaign's README.
* **Path.** Target list, generation (`slurm/campaign.sh`), SAS source lists (`slurm/sas_detect.sh`), fit targets
  built in pieces on a node (`slurm/fit_targets.sh`, `massfit fit-plan pieces | merge`), fit plans sized for the
  job length (`massfit fit-plan jobs`, `slurm/fits_vera.sh`). The pairing on the fit mass that follows has no
  command yet (since 2026-11-25: `tngxmm donors pair-projections`, "Pass 2 with six projections and the added
  observations").

## The concentration prior (2026-11-12)

* **What changed.** `massfit.cm_prior = 'dutton14'` in every preset: a log-normal prior on c200 about the relation
  of Dutton & Maccio (2014, eqs. 7, 10 and 11), 0.11 dex wide (their Sect. 4.1), with c200 bounded at 0.5-20
  (`massfit.cm_prior`). The relation is evaluated at every step at the M200 of the sampled NFW halo, not at the
  first-guess mass. The prior flat in ln c200 over 1-10 (Sanders et al. 2018) stays as the option `flat`.
* **Why.** In the pilot 18 of the 67 in-domain fits ended at a bound of the flat prior: 11 of 28 mock fits and 7
  of 39 real fits. The decision was the author's and was made with these results in hand. The relation and its
  width are the printed ones; nothing was fitted to the pilot.
* **Source and how it differs.** Ledger entry `fit.cm_prior`, 13 evidence blocks, all boxed. The reference
  analysis is not the same: Lovisari et al. (2020) take the Bhattacharya et al. (2013) relation through the method
  of Schellenberger & Reiprich (2017, eq. 32), where the concentration is fixed on a power law in M200 with no
  scatter. Here it stays free within the scatter measured in the simulations. The range 0.5-20 is the wider range
  Sanders et al. (2018, Sect. 2.5.10) tested; at cluster masses both bounds are more than five standard deviations
  of the prior from the relation.
* **The option that existed before.** `dutton14` had been a diagnostic that centred the prior on the first-guess
  mass and cut it at 1-10. A prior on c200 given the first guess is not the published relation, and no preset used
  it; it is replaced.
* **Measured.** The flat-prior chains of the pilot fits that were not at a bound, weighted by the new prior: M500
  moves by a median of 0.3 per cent on 32 real fits and 0.0 per cent on 17 mock fits, at most 10 per cent.
* **Not measured.** The 18 fits at a bound cannot be reweighted and are not refitted yet. No fit has run under the
  new prior (on 2026-11-12; the fits of pass 1 ran under it).
* **Recorded per fit.** `c200_prior_range` and `c200_prior_offset_sd` in `result.json`; the QC flag
  `prior_edge_c200` reads the range of the fit.
* **Hashes.** Spec hashes move, generation hashes do not, so no view is generated again: `v1` 0e1f9258c78fd676 to
  c2e6b282f6aab356, `v1_flamingo_l1m8_pass1` 87a47ef20c163446 to 6f1ce16eb9c5fff3. The seed hash moves from
  dbcd9c755fb3a153 to 67670d464ce8f888 although no seed value changes, so the real seed table and the guarded
  centres are built again before a fit.

## A full disk stops a run and the same command continues it (2026-11-12)

* **Why.** Production runs against a quota that is nearly full and that cannot be read from the login node. A write
  that fails in the middle of a view, a source list or a fit must not leave a file that the next run takes for a
  whole one, and must not be counted as a failure of the item.
* **Rule.** `core.diskguard`: every product is written under a temporary name in its own directory, synced and
  renamed. A view, a SAS detection and a fit each have a done-marker written last (`obs_meta.json`,
  `sas_detect/provenance.json`, the fit's `provenance.json`) and removed first when the item is made again. On
  resume an item counts as finished only when the marker is there and the products it names are whole.
* **A full disk is not an error of the item.** Its status is `interrupted: disk`; it enters no table and is made
  again. The driver takes no new item, lets the running ones end, prints one line starting with `DISK FULL:` and
  exits with 28 (`campaign run`, `sas-queue run`, `massfit fit-hydrostatic`; the job scripts pass the code on).
* **How a full disk is recognised.** By the errno of the failed write, by the message of a tool that does not pass
  the errno on (SAS, cfitsio, h5py), or by a probe write of a few MB in the output root. `os.statvfs` gives the free
  space of the file system, not of the quota, so only the probe write sees an exhausted quota. The probe runs at
  submission, at the start of a job and every two minutes, also while no item ends.
* **Also fixed.** `fits.sh` ended with 0 whatever its streams did; it now ends with 28 or 1. The rate cache of
  mbproj2d (`massfit.cache_io`) and the QPB template cache are written atomically, an entry that cannot be read is
  built again, and a fit no longer waits for ever for a cache lock that cannot be made on a full disk. A last row of
  a results table cut by a kill is removed when the table is opened.
* **Tested.** Per stage, with the physics replaced: the view loop, the pool, the SAS queue with a script in place of
  SAS, the fit batch. The end-to-end test on the fixture halo (`tests/e2e/test_campaign_disk_full.py`) needs a data
  package that matches the code and has not been run.

## Faster likelihood (2026-11-12)

* Exact: the radii of the sub-pixel grid, the painting buffers and the node list of the PSF are computed once
  and kept between calls. The painted and convolved images are the old ones to the bit (unit tests against the code
  they replace).
* To rounding: with a fixed centre the sub-band cluster is painted by one sparse matrix per image, in double
  precision, and the King-angle PSF is summed over its nodes in Fourier space with one inverse transform for the
  core and one for the tail. On nine pilot fits (4e4 to 5e6 counts) the model images change by at most 4e-7 of a
  pixel (painting) and 3e-7 of the image maximum (PSF). Over 500 posterior samples of each fit the log likelihood
  differs with a standard deviation of 3e-5 to 3e-2, rising with the counts; two FFTW plans of the unchanged code
  differ by the same amounts, because mbproj2d sums the pixels' terms in single precision.
* A likelihood call of 12 images of 168 x 168 pixels takes 41 ms with the PSF (98 ms before these two
  changes) and 16 ms without (55 ms). With the exact changes the likelihood is about four times faster than the
  pilot's.
* No full fit was run before and after. On the nine pilot chains the reweighted M500 percentiles move
  by less than 1e-3 of the 68 per cent half-width in eight; in the 5e6-count fit the mean of ln M500 moves by
  about 1e-3 of its standard deviation.

## Pass 2 with six projections and the added observations (2026-11-25)

The entries between 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-24 (the production path, the fit batches, the real templates, the
optimiser cache, the windowed PSF) are in `CHANGELOG.md`; this log has no entry for them.

* Pass 2 observes every halo-snapshot with an ok pass-1 fit along the six icosahedral axes
  (`sim.projection = 'icosahedral6'`, views 0 to 5; preset `v1_flamingo_l1m8_pass2`, the pass-1 preset with these
  projections and the stream key `v4_flamingo_l1m8_pass2`). This is the geometry planned as release 1.2 on
  2026-10-26.
* `tngxmm donors pair-projections` (`donors/pair_six_views.py`, rule in `donors/pair_projections.py`) pairs a
  halo-snapshot with up to six real observations of its window in redshift (0.25) and fitted mass (0.25 standard
  deviations of the log mass of the reference observations). The mass is the fitted hydrostatic mass on both
  sides, as decided on 2026-10-27. No two views of a halo-snapshot are observed under the same observation; a view
  is observed at the redshift of its physics donor, without jitter.
* Assignment. Under `nearest` every halo-snapshot takes its six nearest observations, which leaves observations
  that are nobody's nearest without a mock and loads a few with hundreds of views. `--assignment least-used` gives
  a halo-snapshot the observations of its window that serve the fewest views so far, the halo-snapshots with the
  fewest candidates first. The window and the distance are unchanged.
* An observation with an exposed camera and no corner counts (a pn window mode: 5 of 638) cannot lend a particle
  background and is left out of the pairing with that reason (`donor_tables.qpb_unit_gaps`).
* A trial list on the pass-1 fits finished by 2026-11-25, with the bank and the assignment `nearest`: 3,993
  halo-snapshots, 23,958 views, 3,950 paired, window 0.091 dex. Twelve views of two halos were generated as a
  test.
* 170 observations from an archive search of 2026-11-20 were added to `src/tngxmm/obs/catalogue/observations.csv`
  (808 rows; `in_bank` False, `sample` and `mass_class` = `extra`; inputs `obs/catalogue/inputs_extra/`, module
  `obs/extra_observations.py`, sky columns from HEASARC nH and IRSA dust in `obs/sky_columns.py`). The bank of 638
  stays what every existing preset reads: under `donors.reference_sample = 'bank638'` the spec hash covers the
  `in_bank` rows only, so no hash changed. The donor folds, the CCD label maps and the pool of the conditions rule
  `nn` are those of the bank.
* Not done on 2026-11-25: the fits of the 170 observations, and with them the pass-2 list that includes them.

## The 808 observations in the data package; the last pass-1 fits (2026-11-26)

* Preset `v1_flamingo_l1m8_pass2_extra`: `v1_flamingo_l1m8_pass2` with `donors.reference_sample =
  'bank638+extra'` (its hash covers all 808 rows), the stream key `v4_flamingo_l1m8_pass2_extra`, and
  `massfit.psf_eval = 'window'` and `massfit.projection = 'matrix'` (`docs/dev/PSF_WINDOW_VALIDATION.md`). Spec
  hash c439c88f639cc426.
* Data package `data_v1p2` and response set `responses_obs_v2` hold the tables and responses of the 808
  observations. The rows and files of the 638 are those of `data_v1p1` and `responses_obs_v1`: `tngxmm instrument
  obs-responses adopt --plan <plan>` takes over the finished responses of an earlier centre table where region,
  exposures and settings equal the plan, and builds nothing for them.
* The conditions table has the column `lends` beside `pool`. `pool` is the pool of the rule `nn` and holds
  observations of the bank only. `lends` says that an observation can lend its conditions to a view paired with
  it, and is read by the pairing of a preset whose reference sample includes the added observations: 700
  observations, 548 of the bank and 152 added.
* The last 978 fits of pass 1 were made on a second cluster with `psf_eval = window` and `projection = matrix`;
  the earlier ones, about 4,000, were made on the home cluster with the evaluation on the whole grid, as were the
  623 fits of the real observations. Before the switch, 75 finished z = 0.1 mocks were refitted with the two
  switches: ln M500, switched minus production, has mean +0.001 +- 0.006 and median +0.0005, 38 fits up and 37
  down; two of the 75 moved by more than 0.25. All pass-1 fits use the short chain (150 burn-in and 300 sampling
  steps, 136 walkers), which is short of convergence by the diagnostics the fit records. The fitted masses serve
  the pairing.
* `tngxmm data fetch` has the stages `fit` (a stored pass-1 mock with `data_v1p1` and `responses_obs_v1`), `mock`,
  `realfit` and `analysis` (`src/tngxmm/core/fetch.yml`, `docs/LOCAL.md`). The releases of the repository hold the
  pass-1 views, the fits finished by 2026-11-25, the images and event lists of all 808 observations, both data
  packages and both response sets.
* Open. The 170 added observations are being fitted with the settings of the 623. The pass-2 list with them is
  not built, and no pass-2 view of the final list exists. Some fits of the largest z = 0.055 mocks stop when the
  start points of the sampler are drawn; the cause is understood and the fix is pending. The pass-2 fits are to
  run with chains long enough to converge; the chain length is being measured and no criterion is fixed.

## The chains of pass 2 (2026-11-27)

Proposal of 2026-11-27, after the pilot below. The author has agreed to a fixed length with repeat fits; the
numbers of the rule (steps, move, size of the repeat sample) are not confirmed.

* Pilot. 22 finished pass-1 mocks (z = 0.1 to 1.5) were fitted again with 500 burn-in and 6,000 kept steps, 136
  walkers, three ways: the stretch move, emcee's differential-evolution move, and that move with the scaled start.
  All 66 fits ended. No chain mixed per walker. The rank-normalised split R-hat of ln M500 over walkers is about 2.0
  (differential evolution) and 2.6 (stretch) in the last 3,000 steps, where no fit is below 1.2. emcee's
  autocorrelation time is a tenth of the window at every window length, so it is not measured. The acceptance
  fraction falls during the run, from 15 to 9 per cent (stretch) and from 6.6 to 1.1 per cent (differential
  evolution).
* Cause. The log posterior is smooth: a stored chain's values are reproduced exactly and a scan along a proposal
  direction follows a parabola. About 12 of the 34 free parameters are background and soft-proton levels that the
  data leave at zero; each lies on a plateau that ends at the floor of its prior, with a steep rise of the penalty
  on the other side. The central density and core radius of the gas lie on a narrow ridge. A move of all parameters
  at once nearly always leaves a plateau, the prior box or the ridge.
* Other samplers. Started from the last ensemble of the pilot chains on two mocks: differential evolution on random
  subsets of the parameters, the same with reflection at the bounds, the same with small subsets, and an ensemble
  slice sampler (zeus). None mixes: R-hat 1.3 to 2.5, and the autocorrelation time is again a tenth of the window.
  Subset moves raise the acceptance to 17 per cent and the effective size per evaluation by 2.4 on the z = 1.0 mock
  and change nothing on the z = 0.1 mock. A chain of 50 autocorrelation times would take more than 10^7 evaluations
  of the posterior per fit.
* What is stable. Of the 22 mocks, 15 have a constrained mass (16-84 half-width below 0.3 in ln M500). For those the
  median of ln M500 of steps 2,000 to 4,000 differs from that of steps 3,000 to 6,000 by 0.006 in the median fit
  and 0.03 to 0.08 at most; the width is the same within 2 per cent. The two differential-evolution runs agree to
  0.01 in the median fit and 0.03 at most. The stretch move ends 3 to 5 lower in log posterior, 30 lower for one
  mock, whose mass then differs by 0.48.
* Proposed rule. Pass-2 fits of mocks and real observations discard 2,000 steps and keep 2,000, with the differential-evolution
  move. A sample of the fits is run a second time with another seed and the difference of the medians is reported
  beside the fit uncertainty; it selects and corrects nothing. The fits are described as fixed-length chains checked
  by repeats. They are not converged by the tests of the emcee authors (10 to 50 autocorrelation times, every
  walker crossing the posterior), and the paper states that.
* Precedent and its limits: ledger entry `method.chain_convergence`. Published MBProj2 and MBProj2D fits state a
  length (2,000 kept steps with 800 walkers; 4,000 with 190) and no statistic; the first MBProj paper applies the
  autocorrelation criterion and states that its chains meet it.
* Open. The preset fields for the chain length and the size of the repeat sample are not written. The seven mocks of
  the pilot with an unconstrained mass are a question of the fit centre, not of the chain.
